# Olympus house setup — holistic recommendations *(archived)*

The setup plan for [[my-home]] (B4206, floor 42): what to buy, what Homz builds, and when. Homz Interio does the interiors for ₹18 lakh incl. GST. The builder delivered VRF, wiring, plumbing, paint and finished bathrooms. The main body gives the answers. The [appendices](#appendices) hold the evidence, the alternatives and everything rejected.

**Read first: [[u-setup-instruction]].** It holds the household, the rules the owner actually set (and the agent-made rules corrected on 2 Oct), the facts still to measure, and the research gap. Any agent editing this note reads it first.

**How picks are made.** Budget is not the constraint: the envelope outside Homz is **2× the 1 Oct plan**, because quality and value both matter. Where two options score within 0.1, the better-made one wins. Hyderabad makers (Magari, Alankaram, Freedom Tree) are checked before IKEA, and IKEA wins only on the merits (washable covers, published specs, a 10-year guarantee). Score = 0.3·Dur + 0.3·Func + 0.2·VFM + 0.2·Style, each 1–10 ([[preferences]] BIFL matrix), with VFM judged against the doubled envelope. ₹ are October 2026 prices incl. GST unless marked "ex-GST". "~" is an estimate; "unverified" means no primary source confirmed it.

**What changed on 2 Oct 2026** (owner feedback, eight research streams, every load-bearing price re-checked on the seller's page):

| Owner ask | What the plan does now | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Budget 2×, quality over cheap; use Hyderabad makers | Magari sofa and chairs in the Drawing room, Alankaram solid teak in the Dining, Freedom Tree teak bed in the Master. Systems re-tested: Arc Ultra, 2 APs, 5 purifiers, Dreame L50 Ultra CE, Club Prime board | [Room by room](#room-by-room) · [Systems](#the-systems) |
| Smart switches back | Sonoff Matter relays inside the existing switch boxes, Alexa as controller, wall switch always works. ~₹41k Must, ~₹1.09L whole flat | [§1](#1-smart-home-and-smart-switches) |
| Alternatives for Drawing, Kabir's room, WFH/guest room | Three concepts per room, scored against the plan | [A28](#a28-alternative-room-concepts) |
| No 25 L geyser; no dishwasher | 15 L Wi-Fi geyser in every bath. The dishwasher bay becomes a drawer bank with the waste bins | [§11](#11-geysers-shower-glass-ventilation-and-mesh) · [§4](#4-kitchen-and-laundry-appliances) |
| Adjustable desk for Kabir; new colours | Crank desk 52–75 cm with a tilting top. "Harbour" palette: ink navy, oak, rust, oat | [BR-02](#br-02-kabirs-room) |
| Exhaustive sofa-cum-bed research | ~30 brands checked; top 5 per room scored on 10 dimensions | [7.6 top 5](#761-top-5-sofa-cum-beds-scored) |

## Budget at a glance

**Homz turnkey: ₹18,00,000 incl. GST** for joinery, false ceiling with finish and ceiling lights. A bottom-up check gives ~₹14.0–24.3L, so it's credible ([A27.1](#a271-the-interiors-budget--18-lakh-with-homz-plus-what-sits-outside-it)).

**Outside Homz**, incl. GST, at the doubled envelope. Ranges now show unknowns (Homz quotes, the grille, curtains), not value swaps: every row prices the pick.

| Area | Must | Should | Optional | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Change-order contingency | 1,80,000–3,60,000 | — | — | 20% until ₹18L is the signed Final Price, then 10% |
| Spec upgrades (§12) | — | 1,00,000–3,00,000 | — | Club Prime board and LEGRABOX over Homz's default. A guess until Homz prices the sheet |
| Furniture and room items | 9,66,503–9,82,503 | 4,03,912–4,04,417 | 45,000–55,000 | Magari, Alankaram, Freedom Tree, 2 sofa-beds, Kabir's desk, the Palki. Homz-built bench, library and south bedside sit in the BOQ |
| Curtains (§7) | 1,00,000–2,20,000 | 15,220 | — | Dealer estimate, unverified. Should: Master curtain driver |
| Lamps and art spots (§10) | — | 73,680 | 20,575 | Freedom Tree bedside lamps, IKEA floor lamps, WiZ bulbs |
| Geysers, glass, mesh, grab bars (§11, §13) | 1,45,596–2,10,536 | 45,000–87,200 | 27,938–29,000 | 4 × Racold 15 L Wi-Fi. Glass ~0.76–1.3L ex-GST |
| Kitchen, laundry, cabinet internals (§4, §9, §12) | 2,90,467–3,24,467 | 1,28,514 + stack kit | 46,759–77,699 | No dishwasher. Must incl. LeMans corners (~₹70k, unverified) and duct. Should: oven (Homz adds the 16 A point if missing), stacked dryer, induction, bins |
| Smart home, switches, network (§1, §2) | **85,896–95,896** | 64,397 | **80,605** | Must: 16 relays + 2 spares @₹617.96 each (₹510 saved/unit vs last check), Dot Max ₹8,999 (Festival), 2 APs, Cat6. Labour unpriced |
| Audio (§3) | 99,999 | 21,999 | 1,31,998 | Arc Ultra. Optional incl. the Dining ceiling pair |
| Air, water, robot (§5, §6, §20) | 86,560 | 64,999–1,98,404 | 81,350 | 5 purifiers at move-in. Should: Dreame L50 Ultra CE, softeners if water is hard |
| Safety (§8, §19) | 95,075–1,87,564 | 43,134–49,059 | 22,579 | The grille quote drives the range. Should: 6 leak sensors, CO alarm |
| Admin, health, WFH, move | 39,000–94,879 | 61,366–95,820 | 5,676–14,837 | Insurance and cloud per year |
| **Total** | **~₹21.0–26.7L** | **~₹11.0–14.4L** | **~₹4.6–5.1L** | **Must + Should ~₹32–41L beyond Homz** |

**All-in with Homz: ~₹39–45L Must only, ~₹50–59L Must + Should.** The 1 Oct plan was ~₹38–50L with value alternatives at the low end. The new plan sits well inside its 2× envelope (~₹41–64L beyond Homz): extra money went only where quality rose. Line items: [A27.1](#a271-the-interiors-budget--18-lakh-with-homz-plus-what-sits-outside-it). **Not counted:** art, the king mattress, the refundable RWA deposit (~₹25k–1L, unverified), Homz joinery the BOQ omits, electrician labour for the smart relays, and running costs (fibre, filters ~₹20k a year, VRF AMC later).

**Unverified, ranked by money at stake:** Homz BOQ scope and the Club Prime delta (₹0–3L) · curtains ~₹1.0–2.2L · grille ₹35k–1.2L · lift car size (decides the 244 cm sofa and 220 cm table) · ceiling height. One measurement or email settles each ([A24.8](#a248-claims-still-unverified)).

## Next actions

The design freeze is planned for 15 Nov. These are the dated and blocking items. The full list, with every check, is [A24.0](#a240-next-actions--full-list).

- [ ] **Today:** file any missing Form 26QB, incl. parking and club charges; send Form 16B to the builder by 15 Oct. The fee runs ₹200 a day.
- [ ] **By ~21 Oct:** chase the 21 Sep snag list in writing, or serve one now. RERA §14(3) gives the builder 30 days.
- [ ] **This week:** get Homz's written yes or no on the 12 rider asks, its itemised BOQ, and its GSTIN check ([A0](#two-gst-checks-on-the-homz-invoice)).
- [ ] **This week:** measure the service lift car, door and roof hatch. The Porto sofa (244 cm, one piece) and the Fusi top (220 cm) depend on it. Ask Magari and Alankaram whether legs or arms come off.
- [ ] **October:** Homz's electrician surveys every switchboard (neutral, depth, Wi-Fi signal) and dry-fits one Sonoff relay. Pilot three relays for two weeks before the bulk order ([A1.5](#a15-site-survey-and-homz-asks)).
- [ ] **Before 6 Nov:** sit-test at Magari (Jubilee Hills), Alankaram (Banjara Hills Rd 3), Freedom Tree (Banjara Hills Rd 12) and IKEA Raidurg (HYLTARP, PS 2026, BERGLÄRKA). BERGLÄRKA is "Last chance to buy": order it on the visit.

**Then, by phase** (the boxes live in [A24.0](#a240-next-actions--full-list)): before the freeze, hand Homz the room plans, the §12 spec sheet with Club Prime priced, the dimmable-driver specs and the new Homz drawings (BR-01 library, BR-02 bench, Master south shelf). Before the false ceiling closes, photograph the Cat6, the ceiling wiring and every driver panel, and hold the 70% gate on board stamps. Before move-in: grille, smoke alarms, geysers at 55 °C (50 °C for Kabir), fibre booked 4 weeks ahead, and two weeks of airing before the Prakash.

## Room by room

**Answer:** Homz builds everything fixed. You buy the loose pieces: Hyderabad makers where they win (Magari, Alankaram, Freedom Tree), IKEA where its washable covers, published specs or 10-year guarantee win. One hero per public room, a Palki Sahib in BR-03. Doors and windows stay put. Points can be added where they buy function (decision 12). Plans trace brochure p.32. Alternatives for the Drawing room, BR-01 and BR-02 are in [A28](#a28-alternative-room-concepts).

<img src="../../../assets/personal/olympus/flat-plan.svg" width="560">

*Zones: public putty, private sage, Gurudwara saffron, service grey. North is at the top of brochure p.32 (the west entry is on the left). Glass faces north (Drawing, BR-02), east (BR-03, sit-out, utility) and south (Master balcony, BR-01, kitchen). The plan may show a small window in the Master's west wall. Confirm each face with a compass ([design scheme](#design-scheme)).*

**Before you buy anything over 2 m:** measure the service lift car (W × D × H), door width and roof hatch. Magari and Alankaram take no cancellations, and the Porto sofa (244 cm) and Fusi top (220 cm) ship in one piece. Five walls back onto bathrooms (Master E, BR-01 W, BR-02 W and E, BR-03 W), so Homz scans before any fixing. Strap every tall loose unit to masonry (§8).

13'0" × 17'8", north window (brochure; confirm).

<img src="../../../assets/personal/olympus/render-drawing.jpg" width="720">

*Composite of the actual picks placed to the plan at seller sizes, looking south from the entry. Product photos from Magari, Freedom Tree, Jaipur Rugs, IKEA and Sonos; the sofa is shown in its catalogue fabric (order Honey Linen). Art: Amrita Sher-Gil, Village Scene (1938, public domain), as a placeholder.*

<img src="../../../assets/personal/olympus/room-drawing.svg" width="480">

The 244 cm sofa backs onto the solid south wall, under the art a guest sees first. Two 67 cm armchairs face it across an oval teak table. Shoes come off at a bench by the door. The width still adds up with both walks at 900 mm: 357 cabinet + 900 walk + 667 chair + 393 gap + 667 chair + 978 walk = 3,962 mm. The sofa leaves 761 mm at each end: the Era 300 plinth and the arc lamp take the west end, and the east end sits beside the Living opening, which you enter from the 978 mm walk.

Room total ~₹3.93L (envelope ~₹4.2L). The owned Era 300 sits on a plinth at the sofa's west end; the arc lamp is §10; three art spots are §10. **Alternatives:** a baithak with a teak diwan that seats 22–24 on sangat days (scores 8.3), two facing sofas, or a Chandigarh-modern room with Phantom Hands pieces ([A28.1](#a281-drawing-room--foyer)).

### Dining

### BR-02: Kabir's room

**Why not an electric desk now.** None sold in India goes below ~59 cm (ErgoYou E15 frame 59 cm, JIN 62 cm, Moll's e-Champion 63 cm); he needs ~53 cm (EN 1729 size mark 2, from memory). Motors lift 80–125 kg and anti-collision reacts only after contact, so a hand at the sill is the risk. A 6-year-old will also ride the buttons. Buy an electric frame (ErgoYou E15 under a Homz top) at about age 10, when he needs ≥62 cm. **Alternatives:** a mid-sleeper with a den under it (park until ~8; IKEA rates KURA for over-6s only) and an explorer theme kept to removable decor ([A28.3](#a283-br-02-kabirs-room)).

## The systems

### §1 Smart home and smart switches

**Answer:** Smart switches are back. A Sonoff Matter relay goes inside each existing switch box, behind the builder's plate, so the wall switch still works and the look doesn't change. Alexa controls them over Matter, with an Echo Dot Max in the Living as the main controller. By default, no microphone in the Master (Kabir sleeps there), BR-02 or BR-03; shared rooms may have one. This is an agent default, not an owner rule: confirm it in [[u-setup-instruction]]. ~₹21k Must, ~₹89k whole flat (prices updated Oct 2026)

**Top reasons this could fail, and the fix.** (1) A board has no neutral or too little depth: the electrician surveys every board and dry-fits one relay before anything is bought; no-neutral boards get a Zigbee ZBMINI-L2. (2) Wi-Fi Matter devices drop off: one fixed 2.4 GHz network, three Matter controllers, a two-week pilot of three relays. (3) A relay dies and its light dies: detach mode off, two spares, and a printed bypass diagram in the DB.

| Tier | Need | Pick (value alternative) | Image | ₹ | VFM | Func | Dur | Style | Score | Watch-out | Buy |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Must | Smart lighting, every room | **Sonoff MINIR4M** Matter relay ×16 incl. 2 spares, behind existing switches (Wozart WSCM01 Matter-over-Thread, ₹3,999 per 2 channels, Hyderabad installers, 7 ratings) | [<img src="https://m.media-amazon.com/images/I/51iRSPP+VAL._SL1500_.jpg" width="96">](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0CLS8LYQJ) | **₹617.96** each (~**₹730** incl. GST) — verified sonoff.in Oct 2026 (was ₹1,607.62 ex-GST) | 9 | 8 | 7 | 8 | 7.9 | Survey first. Never on a geyser, VRF or 16 A point. Amazon.in charges ₹2,799 | [sonoff.in](https://www.sonoff.in/shop) |

**Protocol matrix — what works with what, today.**

| Protocol | Range (indoor, masonry) | Power budget | Matter support | Alexa support | Z-Wave? | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Matter (Wi-Fi) | ~30 m wall, one layer | 0.5–3 W per device | Full | Local control via Echo | N/A | **Primary.** MINIR4M, Echo Dot Max, EAP650 (Wi-Fi), Era 300 |
| Thread (Matter transport) | ~10 m wall, one layer | <1 W / mesh node | Required | Echo Dot Max = border router | No | Mesh; Echo Dot Max provides the Thread fabric |
| Zigbee 3.0 | ~15 m, many hops | 0.01–0.5 W | Via gateway (Sonoff/ZBMINI) | via Alexa + ZBMINI | Compatible | ZBMINI-L2 bridges Zigbee-only devices (old bulbs, some sensors) |
| Wi-Fi (2.4 GHz) | ~30 m, one layer | 2–15 W per device | N/A (native) | via skill (Alexa) | N/A | **Primary for high-bandwidth devices.** Each relay uses one IP from the DHCP pool |
| Z-Wave | ~30 m, many hops | 0.01–0.1 W | Via Z-Wave-to-Matter bridge | via bridge | Native | **Skip.** Expensive in India, no local channel, no benefit over Zigbee |
| Bluetooth LE | ~10 m, line-of-sight | <0.5 W | via hub | via Echo (BLE-to-Zigbee) | N/A | Pairing only; never used for sustained control |
| Proprietary 2.4 GHz | ~15 m, one layer | varies | **No** | **No** | No | **Skip.** Any device with its own app (Tuya, Smart Life) adds a second control surface |

**Top failure modes — Smart Home.**

| Risk | Probability | Impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| One relay dies and its lamp stays dead | Medium | Annoyance, safety risk | Two spares; detach-mode diagram printed and stored in the DB |
| Matter devices drop from network | Medium | Temporary, not catastrophic | One fixed 2.4 GHz SSID for smart devices; two-week pilot before bulk install |
| No neutral in existing switch box | Medium (older buildings) | MINIR4M won't work → must use Zigbee-only | Pre-install survey; ZBMINI-L2 fallback per board |
| Deep sleep room: Echo Dot doesn't wake to "Alexa" | Low | Room appears "dumb" | Echo Dot in Living covers Drawing/Living/Dining; one in BR-01 for study access |
| Alexa Plus discontinued or price-hikes in India | Medium | Voice purchase, skill access, early features | MINIR4M relays work natively over Matter local control; only the Plus extras are lost |
| Router Flint 2 firmware drops Matter gateway | Low | Smart relays still work locally, not via Alexa | GL.iNet has open source firmware; check for firmware updates; Flint 2 always routes LAN |
| 16–18 relays saturate 2.4 GHz band | Low–Medium | Roaming delay, dropped devices | Use a non-overlapping channel (1, 6, or 11); 2.4 GHz in India is 2.401–2.483 GHz (IS 11805) |

**Where to buy — consolidated purchase link table.**

| Vendor | Items | URL |
|---|---|---|
| Sonoff India (Apical) | All MINIR4M, ZBMINI-L2, MINI-DIM, spares | https://www.sonoff.in/shop |
| Amazon.in | Echo devices, Tapo doorbell | [B0DKLJ1PSF](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0DKLJ1PSF) · [B09B8XJDW5](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B09B8XJDW5) · [B0DYBPWQNZ](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0DYBPWQNZ) · [B0C8BFXFN6](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0C8BFXFN6) |
| Modo Store (Aqara official) | Aqara Hub M2 | https://in.modo.store/products/aqara-hub-m2 |
| Amazon.in (Wozart alt) | Wozart WSCM01 (16 channels × 2, ~₹3,999/2, Hyderabad installers) | https://www.amazon.in/dp/B09C5PYN8T |

**Decision note — MINIR4M vs Wozart:** MINIR4M at ₹617.96 (sonoff.in Oct 2026) vs Wozart WSCM01 at ~₹2,000 per single channel (₹3,999 for 2 channels). For 32 channels total, MINIR4M needs 8 units (₹4,943 incl. GST) vs Wozart needs 16 units (~₹64k). **MINIR4M was already the clear pick; the gap is now even wider.** Wozart is included only if MINIR4M goes out of stock.

### §2 Networking and Wi-Fi

**Answer:** Keep the owned Flint 2, patched to 4.9.0+ with file sharing and WAN admin off. Lay Cat6 to three ceiling access-point (AP) spots before boarding. Fit **two APs at move-in**: the owner works from home 8 hours a day and ~20 smart relays share the 2.4 GHz band. Add the third only where a room tests under 200 Mbps.

| Tier | Need | Pick (value alternative) | ₹ | VFM | Func | Dur | Style | Score | Watch-out | Buy |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Must | Wi-Fi across the flat, ×2 | **TP-Link Omada EAP650** ceiling AP (EAP610, ₹8,996, enough for ≤500 Mbps) | 14,999 each | 7 | 9 | 8 | 9 | 8.3 | Useless without a cable at each spot before the ceiling closes | [Amazon][B0B12R9CYH] |
| Optional | 3rd AP | **Same EAP650**, after a speed test in every room | 14,999 | 7 | 9 | 8 | 9 | 8.3 | Buying before the test may waste ₹15k | [Amazon][B0B12R9CYH] |
| Must | Cable to each AP | **3 Cat6 runs**, 23 AWG bare copper, own conduit, laid by Homz's electrician | ~5,000–15,000 + labour | 8 | 9 | 9 | 5 | 8.0 | Cheap "Cat6" is copper-clad aluminium. Photograph before boarding | Trade counter |
| Must | PoE and backup power | **TP-Link LS105GP** PoE+ switch + **APC BX600C-IN** UPS on a vented Homz shelf at the fibre entry | 3,049 + 3,599 | 8 | 8 | 7 | 5 | 7.1 | Runs warm in closed joinery. UPS battery every 2–3 years | [Switch][B0CHFFKF4F] · [UPS][B016XVRKZM] |
| Must | Internet line | **ACT Fibernet** 300–500 Mbps (Airtel 300 Mbps, ₹1,599 + GST) | ~900–1,800/mo | 8 | 8 | 7 | 5 | 7.1 | Only ISPs with fibre in Tower B can connect | [ACT][actcorp-hyderabad] |
| Should | Backup internet | **A 5G home line** (Jio AirFiber or Airtel 5G) on the Flint 2's second WAN with automatic failover (phone hotspot by hand, ₹0) | ~600–1,200/mo (unverified) | 7 | 9 | 7 | 5 | 7.2 | Speed-test 5G in the flat first. An outdoor unit may need the association's OK | — |

**Before you buy:** keep APs 500 mm from sprinklers. Give the smart relays one fixed 2.4 GHz SSID that never changes.

**Skip:** Wi-Fi 6E/7 APs, powerline, a rack. **Detail →** [A2](#a2-networking-and-wi-fi--detail)

**Cross-protocol compatibility: Smart Home → Networking → Audio.**

| Smart Home Layer | Device Group | Primary Protocol | AP Coverage? | Backup protocol |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Lighting relays | 16× MINIR4M + 5× MINI-DIM | Matter over Wi-Fi (2.4 GHz) | EAP650 ×2: Living/Drawing/Dining; bedroom relays reach edge of AP-1 or AP-2 | MINIR4M local relay logic works offline |
| Voice controllers | Echo Dot Max, Echo Dot (BR-01), Echo Show 8 | Wi-Fi (2.4 GHz) | All three rooms: BR-01 Echo near AP-2 edge | Echo local wake-word offline; Alexa Plus needs internet |
| Thread border | Echo Dot Max (Thread fabric) | Thread mesh | Mesh hops through Matter devices; Echo = first hop | None needed |
| Zigbee bridge | ZBMINI-L2 (≤4 no-neutral boards) | Zigbee 3.0 | Peer-to-peer; range ~15 m with relays | N/A |
| Audio | Sonos Arc Ultra, Era 300, Era 100 SL | Wi-Fi (5 GHz music / 2.4 GHz control) | EAP650 ×2: Living+Dining one space = easy | AirPlay 2 / BLE for setup |
| Doorbell | Tapo D230S1 | Wi-Fi (2.4 GHz) | Same SSID as smart relays; battery, no roaming | N/A |
| Security sensors | Aqara Hub M2 + sensors | Zigbee (via Hub M2→Alexa) | Range ~10 m through walls; one sensor per zone | Aqara local automation offline |
| Future NAS / HA | Home Assistant Green + ZBT-2 | Ethernet → LAN | Flint 2 LAN or PoE switch port | N/A |

**Top failure modes — Networking.**

| Risk | Prob. | Impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| AP spot has no false ceiling | Med | AP cannot ceiling-mount | Pre-check Homz cove plan; add full-drop patch in BOQ |
| Cat6 is CCA (not bare copper) | Med | Fails PoE at distance; melts | Photograph before boarding; reject cable under ₹7,000/305 m |
| Flint 2 loses internet, keeps LAN | High during outage | Relays work locally; Alexa voice dead | APC UPS extends uptime; Flint 2 Multi-WAN auto-swaps to 5G |
| AP-3 room tests < 200 Mbps | Med | Weak Wi-Fi in bedrooms | Lay cable now (₹0 extra); buy AP only if needed (~₹15k) |
| EAP650 out of stock | Low | Cannot match APs for roaming | Buy EAP650 ×3 as insurance or EAP650×2+EAP610×1 |
| 5 GHz cannot reach Master bedroom | High | Roaming falls to 2.4 GHz | Lying cable to AP spot near Master; buying 3rd AP later |

**Decision: buy EAP650 ×2 now or ×3 now?** **Two now, test, buy one later.** The ₹15k savings justifies the stock risk — EAP650 is widely stocked. If anxiety is strong, buy **EAP650 ×2 + EAP610 ×1**: EAP610 matches 802.3af and the mix works in one Omada controller.

| Socket behind TV | If builder's TV point not at screen plane | 1 no. | ~₹800 electrician |
| Cable management clips | HDMI + ARC + power hidden in media unit | 1 set | ~₹300 |
| **Total Homz AV-ready cost** | — | — | **~₹2,700** |

**Should: machine drying,** as the owner asked on 9 Sep (no room for a washer and dryer side by side): the [Bosch WQG2520XIN][B0GQTZ14ML] heat-pump dryer (**₹70,490** on bosch-home.in, was ₹66,990) stacked on the washer with kit WTZ27410, or the LG FHD1207STB combo in place of both. Both Bosch manuals say "not outdoors" and the dryer's adds "enclosed spaces", 5–35 °C. That applies to the washer too, so both go in the covered, rain-panelled Homz bay; avoid drying on April–May afternoons above 35 °C (A4.6). **Before you buy:** use GST-invoice sellers. Gauge tap pressure: the RO needs under 2 bar.

**Skip:** a dishwasher (owner, 2026-10-02), built-in induction, AMCs, laundry plinths. **Detail →** [A4](#a4-kitchen-and-laundry-appliances--detail)

**Answer:** Five purifiers at move-in (Master, BR-01, BR-02, Drawing, Living/Dining). Test the water this month; hardness decides the softener.

Filters ₹3,999 a year each, ~₹20k for five | [Amazon][B0H3PJKRXC] |
| Should | CO₂ meter, Master | **INKBIRDPLUS NDIR CO₂ meter** (Airveda Pulse, ₹23,999, adds PM) | [<img src="https://m.media-amazon.com/images/I/71DJg8ZJicL._AC_UL320_.jpg" width="96">](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0CG348YS1) | 2,898 | 9 | 7 | 6 | 5 | 6.7 | Unknown brand. Check ~420 ppm at an open window on day 1 | [Amazon][B0CG348YS1] |

KENT or Homz's plumber fits it | [KENT][kent-water-softener] |

**Optional:** a [Sharp DW-P10M-W][B0BH2V48P6] dehumidifier (₹21,450) only if the closet reads >60% RH for a week. Above 350 mg/L, an [IONIX 30 L][B0HG9JZTLQ] whole-flat softener (₹55,700, likely re-plumbing). **Skip:** ERV, magnetic descalers. **Detail →** [A5](#a5-air-and-water-quality--detail)

A [FireAngel FA3820][B0B9HC17ZG] CO alarm (₹7,580, 10-year sealed battery, EN 50291), 1–3 m from the hob: the VRF brings in no fresh air. **Optional:** an [Aqara Smart Valve Controller T1](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0DB8KS8Q3) (₹9,579) that shuts the water when a sensor trips, only if a quarter-turn valve sits inside the flat (there are two inlets, bore and municipal). **Before you buy:** push a 100 mm ball through each window grill; add a restrictor where it passes. No gas shut-off valve: the PNG line is the gas company's.

**Skip:** gas solenoids, indoor cameras. **Detail →** [A8](#a8-safety-security-and-child-safety--detail)

### §11 Geysers, shower glass, ventilation and mesh

**Answer:** A 15 L Wi-Fi geyser in all four bathrooms. No 25 L: Hyderabad's winter is mild, the 15 L heats faster, and four baths for three people means the next shower uses the next tank. 10 mm glass with a door unless a zone is ≥1.2 m deep. No floor holes. ~₹1.5–2.1L.

| Tank, 2 kW | Cold to 55 °C, January | Nov / Feb | January shower at ~6 L/min | Nov / Feb |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **15 L** | ~19 min | ~17 min | ~5 min | ~6 min |
| 10 L (rejected) | ~13 min | ~11 min | ~3 min | ~4 min |

Assumes ~1 bar on floor 42 (~6 L/min, unverified: bucket-test it), a ~20 °C January inlet and 40 °C at the head. The app schedule heats each tank before you wake, so the wait is never felt. In December and January, set the Master to 60 °C if one person showers long (~5.5 min). A 3 kW element would save ~6 minutes, but no Wi-Fi geyser in India comes in 3 kW, and four 3 kW tanks heating together risk the DG cap (§20).

| Tier | Need | Pick (value alternative) | Image | ₹ | VFM | Func | Dur | Style | Score | Watch-out | Buy |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Must | Geyser, all four baths | **Racold Omnis DG Wi-Fi 15 L** ×4, Titanium Plus enamel, BEE 5★, 7/4/2-year tank/element/product, Children Care Mode for Kabir's bath (V-Guard Luxecube Smart 15 L, ₹16,300, 10-year tank but 13 ratings) | [<img src="https://m.media-amazon.com/images/I/61ObeTdGnbL._SL300_.jpg" width="96">](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0BZHJN4MW) | 13,899 each; 55,596 for 4 | 8 | 8 | 7 | 8 | 7.7 | Mixed reliability reviews: register the warranty on install day. Needs Wi-Fi at each point. Stagger the schedules 20 min apart | [Amazon](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0BZHJN4MW) |
| Must | Glass, default | **Fixed panel + outward hinged door**, 10 mm toughened, SS304 hinges, glued water bar, wall-only stabiliser (8 mm framed door) | [<img src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Frameless_Glass_Doors.jpg/330px-Frameless_Glass_Doors.jpg" width="96">](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Frameless_Glass_Doors.jpg)<br><sub>Glassandmirrorpros, CC BY-SA 3.0</sub> | ~18–30k small, ~22–40k master, ex-GST | 6 | 9 | 7 | 9 | 7.8 | SS202 hinges rust: SS304 in writing. Door must clear the WC | [Ozone hinges][ozone-shower-hinges] |
| Must | Glass, zone ≥1.2 m deep with the trap inside | **Fixed walk-in panel**, same spec | [<img src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/Dusche_ohne_Tuer_Walk_in_Dusche_Glasprofi24.jpg/330px-Dusche_ohne_Tuer_Walk_in_Dusche_Glasprofi24.jpg" width="96">](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Dusche_ohne_Tuer_Walk_in_Dusche_Glasprofi24.jpg)<br><sub>Commons, CC BY-SA 4.0</sub> | ~10–15k ex-GST | 9 | 7 | 9 | 8 | 8.2 | Shallow zones soak the WC side. Squeegee daily: bore water spots glass | [IndiaMART Hyd][dir-shower-partition] |
| Should | Exhaust, only where none | **atomberg Studio 150 mm BLDC** | [<img src="https://m.media-amazon.com/images/I/51k5Taau9kL._SL300_.jpg" width="96">](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0D35VCL3W) | 1,799 | 8 | 7 | 7 | 7 | 7.2 | Vent to the shaft, not the false ceiling | [Amazon][B0D35VCL3W] |
| Should | Insect mesh | **Mesh sash in the slider's spare rail**, else a framed SS304 screen; hole ≤0.9 mm | [<img src="https://mrmosquito.in/images/products/Glide-Fit-Screen/Glide-Fit-Screen.webp" width="96">](https://mrmosquito.in/) | ~160–350/sqft, ~45–80k flat | 8 | 8 | 8 | 7 | 7.8 | A true 2-track sill takes no sash | [Mr Mosquito][mrmosquito] |

**Never on a smart switch, plug timer or smart plug.** Racold's and AO Smith's manuals both forbid "an external switching device, such as a timer", because cutting power can reset the thermal cut-out and hide a failed thermostat. Each geyser keeps a plain switch and runs on its own app and Alexa skill.

**Before you buy:** a geyser point in the ceiling void needs a horizontal unit and an access panel; Racold's Wi-Fi horizontal is the [Omnis Slim Wi-Fi 25L][B0DBQ949QW] (₹15,807), the one place a 25 L may be forced (a 15 L horizontal Wi-Fi model is unverified). The glass vendor scans before drilling. Put the glass spec (IS 2553-1, SS304) in the BOQ. Check mirrors, towel rails and health faucets exist.

**Optional:** [atomberg Renesa Elite][B0DV5CT8NH] fans (₹4,999) only where a hook and fan point exist. **Skip:** 25 L and 10 L tanks, instant geysers for showers (~2 L/min in January), mixer retrofits, sliding doors, an enclosed utility. **Detail →** [A11](#a11-bathrooms-geysers-glass-partitions-and-ventilation--detail)

## A0. Standing caveats, GST and quoting bases — detail

The caveats behind the price and score notes at the top of the note.

### Prices are planning estimates, not quotes

- Every INR figure is an October 2026 street estimate. Model codes, specs and India availability were checked against manufacturer pages. Amazon.in, Croma, Reliance and Flipkart often block automated fetches, so a price marked "~" wasn't confirmed live.
- Indian retail prices (Amazon.in, brand stores, IKEA, MRP stickers) already include GST. Contractor and fabricator quotes often don't. Every table says which.
- India retail moves about ±15% and discounts hard off MRP. Re-check locally before you buy. Never paste these figures to a vendor as a target.
- Each pick carries a value alternative. Where that alternative fails a hard requirement, the table says so.
- Scoring uses the BIFL matrix from [[preferences]]: Score = 0.3 × Durable + 0.3 × Functional + 0.2 × VFM + 0.2 × Style. Read the Watch-out column first: it gives the top reason to reject.

### GST in one table (GST 2.0, in force since 22 Sep 2025)

The 56th GST Council (3 Sep 2025) collapsed the slabs to 5%, 18% and 40%, effective 22 Sep 2025.

| What you're buying | GST | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **Homz Interio works contract** (design, factory joinery, installation) | 18% | ₹18L incl. GST = ₹15.25L + ₹2.75L GST. Homz's terms say prices are "inclusive of taxes… subject to change" with government rates |
| Anything bought at retail (furniture, appliances, lights, geysers) | Already in the price | Most of these sit at 18%. A sticker or quote from before Sep 2025 on a large appliance may run about 8% high (the old 28% slab) |
| Worked stone slabs (HSN 6802: granite or quartz worktop) | 18% | Only stone **blocks** fell to 5%. A yard quoting "5%, it's granite" is quoting the block rate |

*(Primary source: 56th GST Council press release, 3–4 Sep 2025, Annexures I and IV, carried from the earlier verification. Confirm the HSN or SAC per line on the invoice.)*

### Two GST checks on the Homz invoice

1. **The invoice must carry a valid GSTIN.** A Sep 2026 third-party lookup showed Homz's Telangana GSTIN (36AAXCS2582B1Z1) as "Cancelled", while the Karnataka one was active. That's one source and **unverified**. Check it yourself on gst.gov.in before the next payment. An invoice from a Karnataka GSTIN for a Hyderabad flat isn't illegal, but you should know which entity bills you.
2. **One works-contract invoice at 18%.** Ask Homz to say in writing that the whole ₹18L is billed as a composite works contract at 18%. That stops a later "goods at one rate, labour at another" restatement.

### Taxes that don't apply at ₹18L

- **TDS on contractor payments (old §194M):** it applies when an individual pays a contractor over ₹50L in a year. ₹18L doesn't trigger it. The old "₹50L contract trigger" action is dropped. *(The Income-tax Act, 2025 replaced the 1961 Act from 1 Apr 2026, and section numbers moved. The ₹50L threshold is **unverified** under the new numbering.)*
- **Building-worker cess:** see [A7, Site rules](#site-rules-lift-rwa-protection-dust-insurance). Rider ask 9 puts any labour-law liability on Homz.

### Quoting bases: say which on every quote

| Basis | Area | What is quoted on it |
|---|---|---|
| Carpet | 2,045 sqft | The whole-flat interiors comparison (₹880/sqft for Homz), false ceiling and its finish, Wi-Fi AP count, robot-vac sizing |
| SBUA | 2,880 sqft | Maintenance charges and the snag-walk fee only. SBUA includes common areas, so never size interiors or devices on it |
| Shutter face (elevation) | per unit | All joinery: wardrobes, kitchen fronts, TV units |
| Running foot | per unit | Kitchen base and wall runs, worktop, cove lighting |
| Opening area | per opening | Grilles, insect mesh, glass partitions |

SBUA against carpet alone moves a per-sqft figure by 41%. Write the basis on every quote.

## A1. Smart home (Alexa) and smart switches — detail

*Rebuilt 2026-10-02 on the owner's direction: smart switches are back. Prices: sonoff.in lists prices before GST, so its figures are shown ×1.18 (GST rate assumed, unverified). Amazon.in and Modo prices include GST. Checked 2026-10-02 unless marked "carried".*

### A1.1 What changed and why

The 2026-10-01 pass cut smart switches because it tested every device against "no electrician, no new wiring, no second app". The owner overruled that: "I need them as I am an engineer by training… I like my toys which are practical and functional for everyday life."

The new test is stricter on reliability and looser on effort. A smart switch earns its place only if the wall switch still works every time, the light still works with the internet down, and any electrician can bypass a dead module in five minutes. Swapping a module behind an existing plate is an electrician's job, but it is not new wiring, chasing or a new circuit, so the flat's no-civil-work rule still holds.

### A1.2 The architecture

**Pick: Sonoff MINI Extreme relays inside the existing switch boxes, on Matter over Wi-Fi, controlled by Alexa.**

| Part | Device | Role | ₹ incl. GST | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| On/off relay | Sonoff MINIR4M (Matter, Wi-Fi, 10 A) | One per lighting circuit, behind the existing switch | 1,897 (₹1,607.62 ex-GST at sonoff.in, verified) | Amazon.in sells it at ₹2,799. Buy from sonoff.in for the GST invoice and the India warranty route |
| Dimmer | Sonoff MINI-DIM (Matter, Wi-Fi, trailing edge) | Art spots, dining pendant, BR-02 night light, coves if dimmable | 3,539 (₹2,999 ex-GST) | Needs trailing-edge dimmable drivers. Rated −10 to 40 °C and needs ≥220 V supply |
| No-neutral fallback | Sonoff ZBMINI-L2 (Zigbee) | Only on boards the survey finds without a usable neutral | 1,898 (₹1,608.85 ex-GST) | Pairs to the Echo Dot Max's Zigbee radio (Dot Max not on Sonoff's named list: unverified) |
| Controller 1 | Echo Dot Max, Living | Matter, Thread and Zigbee controller; voice | 10,999 | Replaces the Living Echo Dot 5th gen |
| Controller 2 | Echo Show 8, Kitchen | Second Matter controller, timers, doorbell video | 24,999 | Has a camera with a shutter. Kitchen only |
| Controller 3 | Echo Dot 5th gen, BR-01 | Voice in the study; third Matter controller | 5,499 | Mute on calls |
| Sensor hub | Aqara Hub M2 | Gas detector, leak sensors, curtain driver | 7,400 (Modo, carried) | Unchanged from the old plan |

**Settings on every module** (the owner does this, not the electrician): detach-relay mode **off**, so the switch always cuts the light; switch input in edge (toggle) mode, so any flip changes state; power-on state "off" after a power cut; firmware updated before Matter pairing; the Matter QR code photographed before the plate goes back on.

**Why this and not brand smart plates.** Every brand plate (Schneider Wiser, Legrand Myrius IoT, Anchor Roma Urban Smart, Havells Signia and Apogee) means swapping the plate and grid on every board, breaking the builder's uniform look, and none offers Matter. Their apps score poorly (Havells Smart 1.8★, MirAIe 3.7★ with server outages) and Schneider Wiser 2.0 reviews mention no Alexa. Polycab Hohm and Norisys have no smart switch line. Legrand Myrius is the best-built option, but its India gateway, price and Alexa path are unverified: ask a dealer only if you want new plates anyway.

**Why Matter.** Amazon's Matter page says Matter lets "Alexa to control your device locally" during internet outages. Matter also allows more than one controller, so Home Assistant can join later with no re-pairing.

**Be honest about "local".** Alexa speech runs in the cloud. With the internet down, voice fails; app and routine control over Matter may keep working (unverified), and **the wall switch is the real fallback**. Matter buys speed and independence from Sonoff's cloud, not offline voice.

### A1.3 Inversion: how this fails in the first six months

| Failure | How the design handles it |
|---|---|
| **A board can't take a module.** No neutral, a neutral borrowed from another circuit, or too little depth. Norisys steel boxes are 50 mm deep (SB50x datasheet); a MINIR4M is 39.5 × 33 × 16.8 mm; switch-mechanism depth is unverified | Survey every board first (A1.5). Dry-fit one module on the fullest board. No-neutral boards get the ZBMINI-L2; a board that fails both is skipped. Buy only after the survey |
| **Wi-Fi Matter devices go "unresponsive".** ~20 modules on a busy 2.4 GHz band at floor 42 | The switch still works (detach off). One fixed 2.4 GHz SSID that never changes. IPv6 on, client isolation off, DHCP reservations, Echos and modules on the same LAN. Three controllers. Pilot three modules for two weeks before the bulk order. The §2 ceiling APs cover the flat |
| **A module dies and takes its light with it.** It sits in series with the light. Hyderabad summer heat in a steel box. No Sonoff service centre in Hyderabad | Keep 2 spare MINIR4M and 1 spare MINI-DIM. Label each module with its circuit. Leave a printed bypass diagram in the DB so any electrician can restore the plain switch. Keep each load under 150 W LED. No modules in sun-facing balcony walls |

Known firmware issues: eWeLink LAN timeouts on MINIR4M firmware 1.3.1 (GitHub SonoffLAN #1822) and detach-mode bugs on some ZBMINIR2 firmware (zigbee2mqtt #30088). Update firmware before pairing. There is no forum owner evidence in this pass: search engines and Reddit blocked automated access.

**Totals (hardware, incl. GST):** Must ~₹41,400 (16 MINIR4M incl. 2 spares + the Dot Max). Should ~₹46,500 (5 MINI-DIM incl. 1 spare, 2 balcony relays, the Show 8). Dimmable coves ~₹8,200 if Homz fits dimmable drivers. Optional ~₹13,300. **Whole flat ~₹1.09L**, before the electrician's labour (unverified: get Homz's per-module rate).

**Hard exclusions.** Geyser supplies (every manual read forbids external switching: [A11.3](#a113-geyser-models-compared)), VRF indoor units and controllers, 16 A appliance points, fan regulators.

### A1.5 Site survey and Homz asks

**Homz's electrician, per board, before anything is bought:**
1. Photograph the board with the plate on and off. Map each switch to its load. Mark geyser, VRF and socket switches never-smart.
2. Test the neutral: N–E reads 0–3 V and L–N ~230 V, unchanged when the switch flips. Turn the lighting MCB off and confirm the box goes dead, which proves the neutral isn't borrowed from another circuit (SP 30 cl. 6.3.4.1, carried).
3. Record the box module count and the clear depth behind the mechanisms (each module needs ~17 mm and a 40 × 34 mm footprint).
4. Read 2.4 GHz signal at the board with a phone: aim for −67 dBm or better. Steel boxes cut range.
5. Record evening supply voltage (MINI-DIM needs ≥220 V) and note any two-way circuits and each load's wattage.

**Homz, before the false ceiling closes:** specify trailing-edge dimmable drivers (datasheets in the BOQ) for spots, pendant and any dimmed cove; give each dimmed group its own switch leg; leave an access panel at every driver; bring new light legs to boards with a neutral; bench-test one spot driver on a MINI-DIM before buying the batch; photograph all ceiling wiring.

**Who installs.** Homz's electrician wires. The owner commissions each module. There is no Sonoff installer in Hyderabad. Wozart (Matter over Thread, ₹3,999 for 2 channels, 7 ratings, not in the CSA ledger) has a Hyderabad installer network: the alternative if you want a vendor to install and own it.

**Hubs (carried from the old A1.3).** The Aqara M2 takes up to 128 Aqara Zigbee devices and has a built-in siren; Aqara sensors pair badly with non-Aqara hubs (forum evidence, unverified), so it stays for §8. Aqara M3 (₹23,499–27,140) duplicates what the Echos do: skip. Aqara wall switches fit the 86 × 86 mm European box, not an Indian modular plate.

**Alexa+ (carried).** Free on full Prime and during Early Access; ₹2,000 a month otherwise. Amazon names the Dot Max and Show 8 as "purpose-built for Alexa+". Confirm you're on full Prime before buying.

The lift lobby is shared: ask the facility manager first and narrow the motion zone to your door. Battery is the top failure mode (Amazon.in review summary: 34 of 42 battery mentions negative). The H200 joins by Ethernet or 2.4 GHz Wi-Fi and hosts up to 64 Tapo Sub-1G sensors.

### A1.9 Notes for other sections

- **§7 curtains.** The Aqara Curtain Driver E1 runs on the M2. Confirm the dealer's track profile suits the E1 track version before buying.
- **§8 sensors.** Six Aqara leak sensors and the optional Aqara T1 valve run on the M2.
- **§10 lamps.** WiZ bulbs are Wi-Fi and pair to Alexa directly; no hub.
- **§11 geysers.** Plain switches only. Each Racold geyser runs its own app schedule.
- **§16 accounts.** Add the spouse as a second admin in Alexa, eWeLink, Aqara, Tapo and Racold.
- **VRF.** Alexa control depends on the VRF brand's gateway (cost unverified). Note the brand and controller type before deciding.

## A2. Networking and Wi-Fi — detail

*Checked 2026-10-01. Hardware prices are Amazon.in shelf prices, GST included. ISP prices are as the ISP publishes them; Airtel's exclude GST, while ACT and Excitel don't say. Items marked "carried" come from the 2026-09-27 pass and weren't re-checked this round.*

### A2.1 The design in one paragraph

The owned GL.iNet Flint 2 routes. Two TP-Link Omada EAP650 ceiling access points (APs) make the Wi-Fi. Each AP sits in the Homz false-ceiling cove band, close to a wall. It gets power and data over one Cat6 cable from a small PoE switch, so there's no socket at the ceiling. The router, switch, ISP fibre box (ONT) and a UPS sit on one vented shelf inside Homz joinery, at the point where the ISP's fibre enters the flat. If the fibre drops, you turn on a phone hotspot by hand. Automatic failover is an Optional add-on. A third cable goes in now, and the third AP is bought only if a room tests weak.

**Why wired APs and not one big router:** 2,880 sq ft through masonry on one router leaves the corner bedrooms weak. A wired AP keeps its full speed, while a wireless mesh spends airtime relaying (carried). The cable is the decision. The AP model matters much less.

### A2.2 Where the APs go

From the floor plan: the Drawing, Living and Dining form one open central band, with bedrooms at the corners. There's no separate corridor. The Master and BR-01 doors open straight off the Living's south side. Put the APs in the central band so most rooms are one wall away. This is a planning layout. Coverage isn't measured.

**The ceiling gate.** The §12 joinery plan gives Homz a perimeter cove only, with a full drop just where §3 puts ceiling speakers (Optional). The Homz brief still lists the false ceiling as "confirm it's in the ₹18L". So the AP spots below sit in the cove band, within about 600 mm of a wall, where a cove would run anyway. Before the drawings freeze, ask Homz to mark the actual ceiling extent on the plan.

| AP | Ceiling position (cove band) | Expected to cover | When |
|---|---|---|---|
| AP-1 | Living north wall, at the Drawing end, by the linen store | Drawing, Living, BR-02, entrance | Buy now |
| AP-2 | Dining north wall, by the BR-03 lobby | Dining, Kitchen, BR-03, Sit-out, Utility | Buy now |
| AP-3 | Living south wall, between the Master and BR-01 doors (by the Puja) | Master, Master toilet, BR-01 | Lay the cable now. Buy the AP (₹14,999) only if the Master or BR-01 tests weak |

**If no ceiling reaches a spot**, pick one, in this order:

1. **Add a small full-drop patch** at the AP spot (about 600 × 600 mm) as a priced line in the Homz BOQ.
2. **Wall-mount the AP** high on the nearest wall. The EAP650 kit supports wall mounting. Feed it through a slim white PVC surface raceway. No wall chasing or painting.
3. **Use a Deco BE25 unit on a shelf** next to an existing socket. Slower wireless link, but no cable at all.

**Placement rules.**

- Within about 600 mm of a wall, inside the cove band.
- At least 500 mm from any sprinkler head or smoke detector. This follows the common "three times" obstruction guideline (3 × the AP's 160 mm width). It's a guideline, not a checked clause: confirm with the building's fire contractor. Mark sprinklers and detectors on the ceiling drawing first.
- Clear of VRF grilles, return-air openings and the ~600 × 600 mm service hatches.
- At least 1 m inside the east and south glass. Glass and window frames weaken 5 GHz (carried).
- Ceiling APs need nothing but the cable, because PoE carries the power.

**Walk-test after move-in.** Run a speed test in each room on 5 GHz. Aim for at least 200 Mbps in every room where people sit (target, not a standard). Buy AP-3 if any room misses it.

### A2.3 Getting a cable to each AP with no new wall conduit

| Route | When it works | Who does it | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Along the Homz cove band** | Wherever Homz builds a cove or ceiling. The open Drawing-Living-Dining band lets one route reach all three AP spots | Homz's site electrician lays the conduit and pulls the cable before boarding | **Pick.** The cable stays inside the cove. It drops to the shelf inside Homz joinery, not inside a wall |
| Surface raceway | A wall-mounted AP where no ceiling runs | Homz's electrician or carpenter | Fallback. Visible, but no wall chasing or repainting |
| Builder's existing data points | Only if the in-room cable is Cat6 and runs back to one central point | Owner tests (A2.8) | Use for the BR-01 desk if it tests good. Unlikely to reach AP spots |
| Builder's coax as a pull string | Coax sits loose in a PVC pipe and slides at both ends | Electrician or technician, by hand only | Good for the BR-01 desk. Cat6 may legally share a TV/data pipe, never a power pipe (IS 732, carried) |
| Mesh with wireless links between units | A room with no false ceiling and no cable | Owner | Fallback. Desktop units (Deco BE25 3-pack, ₹31,999) sit on shelves next to existing sockets. Slower and visible |
| Powerline adapters | Never here | — | Rejected. Unreliable across breakers, and the flat may be on more than one phase |

**Corner check:** a ceiling AP without a cable would need a power socket in the ceiling. That's electrical work the owner wants to avoid. So any AP spot with no ceiling and no raceway becomes a desktop mesh unit, not a ceiling AP.

### A2.4 Homz contract asks for cabling

Write these into the Homz BOQ before the design freeze. They're low-voltage work, not electrical work.

**First, confirm the ceiling extent.** Ask Homz to mark every cove and drop on the ceiling plan. Ask them to price any AP patch from A2.2 as its own line.

1. **Lay three Cat6 runs** from the network shelf to AP-1, AP-2 and AP-3, plus one to the BR-01 desk if the ceiling route reaches it. Each run goes in its own PVC or flexible conduit, clipped to the slab, with no power cables inside. Cross power runs at right angles.
2. **Cable spec:** Cat6 U/UTP, 23 AWG solid bare copper, jacket printed with brand and part number. Reject copper-clad aluminium (CCA). Under PoE it heats up to twice as fast as copper (carried). A 305 m "Cat6" box under about ₹7,000 is very likely CCA (carried). One Amazon.in listing sold as "D-Link" carries a buyer review calling it CCA. Buy from a trade counter against a named part number, or have Homz supply it with the part number on the invoice.
3. **Leave 1 m of slack** at each AP spot and 1.5 m at the shelf. Leave a pull string in every conduit.
4. **Mark each AP spot** on the ceiling drawing. Ask for a small opening, or a marked point to cut, about 160 mm from any downlight. The EAP650 is 160 × 160 × 34 mm (TP-Link spec). Keep each AP and its cut-out at least 500 mm from any sprinkler head or detector (guideline; confirm with the building's fire contractor).
5. **Network shelf:** inside joinery at the fibre entry, beside an existing socket, with vent slots top and bottom. It holds about 60–70 W of always-on kit (estimate). No new socket means no electrician.
6. **Photograph every run before boarding** and label both ends.
7. **Termination and test:** an RJ45 plug at each AP end and at the shelf. A local network technician or the owner crimps them. Test each run before the ceiling closes (A2.8).

**Cost:** cable ~₹5,000–15,000 for 100–305 m of genuine copper (unverified), plus Homz labour (ask for a line-item quote). Plugs and boots cost a few hundred rupees.

### A2.5 Hardware choices

**Access points**

| Model | Wi-Fi | Uplink | Power draw | ₹ (Amazon.in) | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Omada EAP650** | Wi-Fi 6 AX3000, 2.4 + 5 GHz | 1 GbE | 13.7 W (EU) / 15.5 W (US), 802.3at PoE or 12 V DC | 14,999 | **Pick.** 160 × 160 × 34 mm. India SKU, sold through TP-Link's store, importer Rashi Peripherals |
| Omada EAP610 (V4) | Wi-Fi 6 AX1800, 2.4 + 5 GHz | 1 GbE | 10.9 W, 802.3af PoE, 48 V passive PoE or 12 V DC | 8,996 | **Value alternative.** Listed on TP-Link India's ceiling index. Same 160 mm body and Omada stack. Carries a full 300–500 Mbps line; it lacks the EAP650's wider 160 MHz channels, which matter only for LAN copies close to the AP. Saves ~₹12k for two APs, ~₹18k for three. Runs on 802.3af, so any PoE port powers it |
| Omada EAP670 V2 | Wi-Fi 6 AX5400 | 2.5 GbE | — | 22,784–24,836 | Skip. The 2.5 GbE port only helps LAN copies to one fast laptop |
| Omada EAP723 | Wi-Fi 7 BE5000, 2.4 + 5 GHz | 2.5 GbE | 17.83 W PoE | Listed, no price shown | Wi-Fi 7 alternative on TP-Link India's ceiling index. Price unverified |
| Omada EAP720 | Wi-Fi 7 BE5000 | 2.5 GbE | 802.3at or DC | 20,184 | Listing says "Country of Origin USA". Not on TP-Link India's index, so India warranty is unclear |
| Omada EAP773 / EAP770 | Wi-Fi 7 tri-band with 6 GHz | 10 / 2.5 GbE | — | 37,276 / 33,493 | Skip. 6 GHz passes masonry worst |
| Deco BE25 (3-pack) | Wi-Fi 7 BE5000 mesh | 2× 2.5 GbE each | Mains adapter | 31,999 | Fallback only where no cable can reach. Desktop units |
| Omada EAP650-Wall / EAP655-Wall | Wi-Fi 6 wall plate | 1 GbE + LAN ports | PoE | 11,950 / 14,899 | Only if a builder data point is live Cat6. Fit to an Indian modular box unverified |

**A 1 Gbps plan doesn't need 2.5 GbE APs.** A 1 Gbps line delivers about 940 Mbps of real throughput. A gigabit AP port carries that already. The old note's "EAP670 for a 1 Gbps plan" rule is cut.

**PoE switch**

| Model | Ports | PoE budget | ₹ | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **TP-Link LS105GP** | 5 GbE, 4 PoE+ | 65 W | 3,049 | **Pick.** Unmanaged, fanless, metal. 3 EAP650s draw about 47 W (3 EAP610s about 33 W). Fanless means it runs warm: give the closed shelf vent slots top and bottom |
| TP-Link LS108GP | 8 GbE, 8 PoE+ | 62 W | 4,299 | If you want spare ports |
| TL-SG2210P | 8 PoE+ + 2 SFP, managed | 58–61 W by version | 7,980–8,499 | Only for a VLAN design. Not needed |
| TL-PoE160S injector | 1 port, 802.3at | 30 W | 1,898 | Per-AP alternative. Use 802.3at; the AP can draw up to 15.5 W, just over 802.3af's 15.4 W (carried) |

**Controller**

| Option | ₹ | What you get | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Omada Cloud Essentials** | 0 | TP-Link's own page calls it "a free and easy version of the Omada Cloud-Based Controller" covering APs, switches and gateways, with topology, VLAN, logs and alerts. Mesh and fast roaming come from an April 2026 review (review source) | **Pick.** Settings changes need the internet. Adopted APs should keep serving clients during an outage (unverified). TP-Link's India site has no Essentials page (the /in/ URL returns 404), so India availability is unverified |
| Omada app, standalone | 0 | Each AP set up on its own | Works. No mesh, no shared roaming settings |
| OC200 hardware controller | 5,999 (Amazon.in, in stock) | Local controller, no internet needed for changes | **Fallback** if Essentials isn't offered to an India account, or if you want local control |
| Software controller on the §16 NAS | 0 | Local, full features | Only if the NAS is bought and you want to maintain Docker |

**UPS**

The building's generator backs up the flat, but the switchover drops power for a moment. Without a UPS the fibre box and router reboot, which takes 2–3 minutes (estimate) mid-call.

| Model | ₹ | Load | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **APC Back-UPS BX600C-IN** | 3,599 | 600 VA / 360 W. Our load is about 60–70 W (estimate) | **Pick.** Bridges the switchover with room to spare. Runtime at our load unverified |
| Resonate RouterUPS Gen2 | 1,799 | 12 V, up to 2 A | Too small. The Flint 2 uses a 12 V 4 A adapter (carried) |
| APC BX1100C-IN | 7,899 | 1100 VA | Only if the §16 NAS shares the shelf |

### A2.6 The Flint 2

- **Spec (carried):** 4×4 Wi-Fi 6 on both bands. One 2.5 GbE WAN, one 2.5 GbE port that can be WAN or LAN, four 1 GbE LAN. The LAN ports share one 2.5 GbE link to the processor, which is harmless on a home line.
- **Security (checked on NVD, 2026-10-01):** seven 2026 CVEs list the MT6000. Four sit in the file-sharing stack: CVE-2026-11505 (glnassys hard-coded key), CVE-2026-18585 (NAS module heap overflow), CVE-2026-19979 (WebDAV authorization bypass, CVSS 8.3) and CVE-2026-19983 (NAS command injection, CVSS 8.3). Three sit in admin functions: CVE-2026-19980 (Language Update code injection), CVE-2026-19981 (Wi-Fi Timer command injection) and CVE-2026-19982 (Firewall-management RPC command injection). Turning file sharing off doesn't mitigate those three. NVD says 4.9.0 fixes most of them. CVE-2026-18585 lists "up to 20260707" and no fixed version. Actions: install the current stable firmware (4.9.0 or later), keep the admin panel and GoodCloud remote access off the WAN, and turn file sharing and WebDAV off.
- **Long-term support (carried):** upstream OpenWrt ships official `glinet_gl-mt6000` images. That's the fallback after GL.iNet's own support ends.
- **Multi-WAN (verified):** GL.iNet's docs list Ethernet, Repeater, Tethering and Cellular as WAN types, with Failover (default) and Load Balance modes. Failover switches back to the main line automatically.
- **Wi-Fi off:** once the APs run, turn the Flint 2's own radios off. Two uncoordinated Wi-Fi systems confuse phones.
- **No VLANs by default.** One main network plus a guest network for visitors and house-help is enough here. VLANs would need a managed switch, mDNS reflection for Alexa and Cast discovery, and more upkeep. Add them later if you want IoT isolation.
- **Kabir's devices:** set a family-safe DNS on his tablet, such as Cloudflare 1.1.1.3, or use the Flint 2's AdGuard Home.

### A2.7 Internet lines

**First step:** ask the facility manager which ISPs have fibre and equipment in Tower B. A TRAI regulation in force since 25 Oct 2024 bars exclusive builder-ISP deals (carried), but only ISPs already wired into the tower can connect quickly. New-tower fibre can take 1–4 weeks (carried). Book the line at least 4 weeks before move-in.

| ISP | Plans (Hyderabad) | GST | Notes | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **ACT Fibernet** | Live plan grid: 200 Mbps ₹899–1,249; 400 Mbps ₹1,199–1,549; 500 Mbps ₹1,599–1,799; 1 Gbps ₹2,999–3,199, by pack. The same page's text block still lists 300 Mbps at ₹899–1,399 and 500 Mbps at ₹1,199–2,249. Confirm by phone | Not stated | Unlimited data. ACT's FAQ says the router alone is enough, so the Flint 2 plugs straight in (carried). Likely behind carrier-grade NAT (CGNAT), so no inbound hosting (carried inference) | **Pick**, if in Tower B |
| Airtel Expert Wi-Fi | 100 Mbps ₹799; 300 Mbps ₹1,599; 1 Gbps ₹3,999 | Extra ("exclusive of taxes") | Publishes measured speeds; 300 Mbps measured near-symmetric (carried). Uses CGNAT (carried) | Alternative |
| JioFiber | ~300 Mbps ~₹1,499 + GST (carried) | Extra | Jio's business site says it runs on private IP (carried) | Alternative |
| Excitel | 200 Mbps ₹466; 300 Mbps ₹516 (12-month term) | Not stated | Max 300 Mbps. Names HITEC City and Gachibowli, not Financial District or Nanakramguda | Second-line candidate |
| Jio AirFiber / Airtel AirFiber | ~₹600–1,000/mo for 100 Mbps-class plans (unverified; plan pages didn't render) | Extra (unverified) | 5G fixed wireless. Doesn't use the building's fibre riser | Backup-line candidate |
| Tata Play Fiber | — | — | Doesn't serve Hyderabad (carried) | Out |
| BSNL | 200 Mbps ₹1,111 (carried) | — | Hyderabad availability unconfirmed | Ask only if you need a public IPv4 |

**Plan tier:** 300–500 Mbps covers a WFH parent, streaming and a child's tablet with room to spare. A 1 Gbps plan costs about ₹1,400 a month more on ACT's live grid.

**Remote access:** with CGNAT, use Tailscale or a Cloudflare Tunnel on the Flint 2. Don't pay for a static IP.

**Backup line.** Two fibre ISPs usually share the building's riser and power, so one building fault can cut both. A mobile path is fully separate.

| Option | ₹ | How it works | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Phone hotspot, by hand** | 0 | When the fibre drops, turn on your phone's hotspot and join the laptop to it | **Default (Must).** Truly free. Calls drop for about a minute while you switch |
| USB 4G dongle + data SIM | ~1,400–2,500 for the dongle (Amazon.in listings, e.g. Amazon Basics, Melbon) + ~₹200–400/mo for the SIM (unverified) | Plug into the Flint 2's USB port. Set Tethering as the second WAN in Failover mode | **Optional** automatic failover. GL.iNet's Tethering page covers phones and says "host-less" modems work. Check the specific dongle against GL.iNet's USB-modem FAQ (unverified per model) |
| Spare Android phone at the router | Phone you already own + data SIM | Same as the dongle, over USB tethering | Works, but a phone living in closed joinery runs warm and its battery ages |
| 5G home wireless (Jio AirFiber, Airtel AirFiber) | ~₹600–1,000/mo + GST (unverified) | Outdoor unit on a balcony, cable to the Flint 2's second WAN port | Value alternative for automatic failover. Fully independent of the building riser. Needs a 5G signal test on the balcony and a cable run from it |
| Second fibre line (Excitel) | ₹466–516/mo | Second ISP on the second WAN port | Only if the main line fails more than twice in the first three months. Likely shares the riser |

**Look around the corner.** Mobile signal inside joinery on floor 42 is unknown. Test the signal at the shelf spot with a phone before buying a dongle.

### A2.8 The in-room cable: identify and test

Every room has a cable that looks like coax (owner, 2026-09-27). Check one faceplate before Homz finalises drawings.

| Test | Coax (TV) | Cat6 (Ethernet) |
|---|---|---|
| Faceplate opening | Plain round ~9.5 mm hole, no thread (IEC type, carried) | Eight gold contacts in an ~11.7 mm opening |
| Norisys module code | C5797 coax socket (carried) | C5802 Cat6 or C5803 Cat6A (carried) |
| Jacket print (circuit off, 2 screws) | `RG6`, `75Ω` | `CAT6 UTP 23AWG 4PR` |
| Thickness | Don't use it. Cat6 is ~6.0 mm and RG-6 ~6.5–6.9 mm (carried) | |

Pop any blank modules on the plate too. Builders often leave the data cable behind a blank (carried).

**Is it in a pipe?** With the circuit off, push 50 mm of slack in and pull it back. If it slides and spins at both ends, it's in a pipe. Tape a pull wire to it and draw Cat6 in alongside, by hand only (carried).

**Testing a Cat6 run.** A ₹339 wiremap tester catches opens, shorts and crossed pairs. It misses split pairs. A 10-minute traffic test catches those (carried):

`iperf3 -c <far end> -t 600 -i 10 -P 4 --bidir`

Pass: link stays at 1000 Mb/s full duplex, at least 940 Mbit/s each way, near-zero retransmits, and no rise in `rx_crc_errors` (check `ethtool -S` before and after).

### A2.9 Wi-Fi 6E/7 and 6 GHz

India opened 5925–6425 MHz to low-power indoor Wi-Fi by G.S.R. 47(E), 20 Jan 2026 (carried). Gear must have an integrated antenna (rule 8(1), carried). It's legal, but it doesn't help here. 6 GHz passes masonry worst, and a 300–500 Mbps line doesn't need it. Dual-band Wi-Fi 7 (EAP723) is the upgrade path if a future laptop benefits.

### A2.10 Rejected

| Option | Reason |
|---|---|
| 18–22 Cat6 drops to a server rack | Needs wall work. Three AP runs plus one desk run do the job |
| VLAN design with a managed switch | Upkeep and Alexa discovery problems for little gain in a family home |
| MoCA (Ethernet over coax) | The fitted TV sockets are rated 0–860 MHz and block MoCA's 1125–1675 MHz. No India service channel. Cat6 is far cheaper and faster (carried) |
| Powerline adapters | Unreliable across breakers and phases |
| UniFi APs | India store showed every AP sold out. Grey imports lose warranty (carried) |
| Deco X50-PoE as router | Duplicates the Flint 2 (carried) |
| Netgear Orbi, Amazon eero | Orbi paywalls security features. eero isn't sold in India (carried) |

DTH: ask the facility manager whether the tower has a common dish feeding every flat. If so, DTH needs only a subscription and a set-top box (carried). That's a TV question for §3.

## A3. Whole-home audio and TV — detail

*What changed on 2 Oct 2026.* At the doubled budget the TV bar moves from the Beam Ultra to the **Sonos Arc Ultra** (₹99,999): the strongest bass without a sub and firmware on sale since 2024, against the Beam Ultra's September 2026 launch. It is 1,178 mm wide, so the media unit top needs ≥1.2 m clear. The tables below still compare both.

*What changed on 1 Oct 2026.* Homz Interio is the turnkey firm. The flat needs no electrical, plumbing or painting work, and the VRF is installed. So the old six-zone in-ceiling plan, with its cable home-runs to a utility rack, drops to a reference section (3.8). The recommended build is wireless: owned TV, one soundbar, the owned Era 300, and one or two small Sonos speakers. Prices are October 2026 Indian retail and include GST.

### 3.1 Three ways to do it

| Route | What you buy | ₹ (GST incl.) | Homz work | Decide by | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A. Simple core (recommended)** | Arc Ultra (2 Oct; Beam Ultra on 1 Oct) on the owned 65" TV; owned Era 300 in Drawing; Era 100 SL in BR-03 | ~₹1.22L | TV panel only: cable duct and mount backing. A socket extension only if the builder's TV point isn't behind the screen. Arc Ultra is 1,178 mm wide | Homz design freeze (TV panel) | One app, every speaker groups with every other, nothing in the ceiling. Add rooms later by plugging a speaker in |
| **B. Core + one ceiling pair** | Route A + QI 65C pair over Dining + one base WiiM Amp in a Dining sideboard | ~₹1.95L | Route A + a Dining ceiling drop, 2 cut-outs, 2 back-boxes, and a ~3 m conduit up the back of a Dining sideboard that holds the amp | Before the Dining false ceiling is boarded | Take it only if you want music with no visible box in Dining. Adds a second app (WiiM Home) |
| **C. Full six-zone in-ceiling (old plan)** | 6 base WiiM Amps, KEF pair in Drawing, 5 QI 65C pairs, soundbar, rack | ~₹6.0–6.5L | Full false ceiling in 6+ rooms, ~12 cut-outs and back-boxes, cable home-runs to a rack, rack power and ventilation | Before any ceiling is set out, with every speaker bought | Rejected. Most money, most parts, and the ceiling plan freezes before you have lived in the flat |

**Why A wins.** It has the fewest moving parts. The Living and Dining are one open space, so the soundbar already fills Dining at background level. Every Sonos speaker joins one group in one app. Nothing needs the ceiling, so the Homz schedule doesn't depend on audio.

**The honest cost of A.** Voice control of music runs through the Echoes (3.5). Sonos's 2024 app failure is a real support risk (3.5). Wireless speakers are visible objects on shelves.

### 3.2 What you already own

| Item | Use it for | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Sony 65" XBR65X90CH** (X900H family, 2020, full-array LED with local dimming) | Living TV | Keep it. It meets the viewing-angle minimum at this distance (3.4). The XBR prefix is a US model code. **If it was bought in the US, read the rating label: if it says 120 V only, it needs a step-down transformer** (unverified; check now). The old note said "Google TV"; X900H-generation sets shipped with Android TV (unverified on Sony's page, which blocks fetches). eARC on this family arrived by firmware update (unverified): check the HDMI 3 label and the TV's sound settings |
| **Sonos Era 300** | Drawing room, standalone | The best-sounding single box in the flat. Spatial audio via up-firing drivers. Put it on a shelf at ear height or above, not on the floor. A second Era 300 (₹44,999) makes a stereo pair for a seated listening spot. **Never as a surround:** Sonos requires surrounds to be "two Sonos speakers of the same model", and surround duty disables "AirPlay", "Bluetooth audio playback", "Line-in" and "Subwoofer output" (Sonos support, re-checked 1 Oct 2026) |

All need the TV's eARC port for lossless Atmos (TrueHD). On plain ARC they still get Dolby Digital Plus Atmos, which is what Netflix and Prime stream.

Sonos India's product data shows it in stock. It's new (Sep 2026), so expect early firmware bugs, and AVStore doesn't list it yet. If it disappoints inside the return window, fall back to Beam Gen 2 |

Samsung prices and stock are from the samsung.com/in product data on 1 Oct 2026: QS700F `outOfStock`, Q800H `inStock`. Q930H and Q990H stock not checked. A wireless sub (Sonos Sub Mini ₹44,999, Sub 4 ₹79,999) is optional later; skip it with a 6-year-old in the room, because it is a floor-level box with a big port.

**Mounting.** If the TV goes on the wall, mount the bar below it on its own bracket, or stand it on the console. AVStore lists a Flexson Beam mount at ₹6,500. A Beam Ultra mount price was not found (unverified). Ask Homz for a TV panel with plywood backing, so the mount screws into wood, not gypsum.

### 3.4 TV size and placement on the Living wall

The Living is 19'5" × 11'9" (5.92 × 3.58 m). It has no window of its own; daylight arrives from the Dining's east glazing. A sofa on one long wall facing a TV on the other puts the eyes about 2.6–2.9 m from the screen.

| Eye-to-screen | 30° minimum (SMPTE EG-18) | 40° immersive (THX) |
|---|---|---|
| 2.4 m | 58" | 79" |
| 2.7 m | 65" | 89" |
| 3.0 m | 73" | 99" |

*Derived: for a 16:9 screen, distance = 1.63 × diagonal at 30° and 1.20 × diagonal at 40°.*

- **Keep the 65" now.** At 2.7 m it sits exactly on the 30° line.
- **If you replace it, buy 75".** That gives 34° at 2.7 m. An 85" (1.88 m wide) gives 38° but needs ~2.3 m of clear wall, with doors and switches outside that width.
- **Screen centre ~1.0–1.1 m above the floor.** Seated eye height is ~1.0–1.2 m. Don't hang it over a tall console.
- **Mini-LED over OLED for any new set.** Burn-in from a child's static game HUDs is the real OLED risk, and Indian warranties typically exclude it. Glare is a smaller factor than the old note said, because this room has no direct sun. Candidates: Samsung QN90F (65" shows ~₹1,14,690 on Samsung India, unverified; 75" price not found) or the TCL C8L as value (price unverified). Check a 75"+ carton against the service lift before ordering.
- **Kid safety.** Wall-mount the TV or strap it to the wall. A 65" set on a console can tip.

### 3.5 Sonos in India: what works and what doesn't

**Alexa on Sonos is not available in India.** Sonos's availability page lists 16 standard regions and 27 "International Version" regions; India is in neither (re-checked 1 Oct 2026). Indian English and Hindi appear only as languages. The Beam Ultra spec lists "Works with Sonos Voice Control" and no Alexa. So nobody can start or control music on a Sonos speaker by voice. "Alexa, play Kirtan in the Gurudwara" won't work. Control music from the Sonos app, AirPlay 2 (iPhone) or Spotify Connect, and keep Echoes (§1) for voice and timers. If voice music matters in one room, the base WiiM Amp is listed by WiiM as "Group with Echo speakers and Display ✓" (India behaviour unverified).

**Two support risks.** Sonos's 2024 app rewrite dropped features and cost its CEO his job in Jan 2025. The Sonos–IKEA SYMFONISK line ended in 2025 (old note, not re-checked). Neither stops a purchase. Both argue for a small Sonos footprint rather than a house built around it.

**Trueplay tuning needs an iPhone or iPad** (Sonos India Era 100 SL page). Borrow one for a one-time tune if the household is all-Android.

### 3.6 BR-03 Gurudwara: the Kirtan speaker

The room's rules (§7.3C): camera-free and mic-free, nothing above the Saroop, and every object lower than the Saroop.

- **Pick: one Sonos Era 100 SL** (₹21,999) on a low shelf in the Homz Gurudwara joinery, below the Saroop's height. That costs nothing extra and needs no drilling. The Sonos wall mount (₹7,999) on a side wall is the fallback. Never on the Palki wall. It has no voice control and Sonos lists no microphone for it. It plays from the Sonos app or AirPlay, and joins a whole-house group for the house-blessing Path.
- **Controls for the Granthi.** The Era 100 SL has touch volume and play/pause on top, so nobody needs the app.
- **Watch-out.** It is still a Wi-Fi device with firmware. If you want "no electronics at all" in the room, use the purist route below.
- **Purist route (only if Homz is boarding BR-03's ceiling anyway).** A passive in-ceiling pair (QI 65C, ₹33,900) in the sangat half, 2.0–2.4 m out from the Palki wall, ~2.4 m apart, 0.6 m off each side wall (the room is only 3.63 m wide). The amp sits outside the room. This costs ~₹95,000 with an amp and needs the same pre-boarding steps as 3.7. Check the MEP drawing for VRF branches at those positions.
- **Noise.** Keep early-morning volume at room level. Neighbours can complain under the Noise Rules 2000 (see §7.3C).
- **Timing.** Buy the Era 100 SL a week before the first Path in BR-03. Tie that to the Homz schedule, which plans the Prakash and move-in for April 2027. It needs no install.

### 3.7 The optional ceiling pair over Dining

Take this only if you want background music with no visible box. Skip it otherwise; the soundbar covers the open Living–Dining.

**Kit.**

| Part | Pick | ₹ | Spec that matters |
|---|---|---|---|
| Speakers | Q Acoustics Q Install QI 65C, pair | 33,900 (Ooberpad, in stock) | Cut-out Ø203 mm, max rear depth 79 mm, 8 Ω, 89 dB, 15–60 W, swivel 19 mm tweeter, HF ±3 dB switch, moisture-proof, 1-year warranty, front clamps |
| Value speakers | Elac IC-C61-W, each | 10,990 (Ooberpad) | Cut-out Ø205 mm, depth 76.5 mm, 1" tweeter, 1-year warranty. Tweeter aim unverified |
| Amp | **Base WiiM Amp** | 49,800 (Headphone Zone, Space Gray in stock) | 60 W/ch at 8 Ω, 2.67 Ω minimum, AirPlay 2, Google Cast, Alexa Cast, Spotify Connect, sub out |
| Cable | 2.5 mm² genuine OFC speaker cable, ~20 m | ~2,000–3,000 | Copper-clad aluminium is common in India; ask for OFC on the invoice |
| Back-boxes | 2 sealed MDF boxes, built by the Homz carpenter | ~3,000–6,000 | See below |

**Never the WiiM Amp Pro or Amp Ultra.** WiiM's own comparison table marks AirPlay 2 "✓" for the Amp and "–" for both the Amp Pro and Amp Ultra. The page also says "WiiM Amp supports AirPlay 2 as a receiver." The Pro (₹59,800) and Ultra (₹84,800) cost more and drop AirPlay, Siri and HomePod grouping. The base Amp's HDMI ARC takes stereo PCM only, which doesn't matter here because the soundbar owns the TV.

**Why a WiiM and not a Sonos Amp.** The WiiM is ₹25,199 cheaper, has 5 GHz Wi-Fi, and can join an Alexa multi-room group with Echoes (per WiiM; India behaviour unverified). An iPhone can still play to the WiiM and the Sonos speakers together over AirPlay 2. The Sonos Amp (₹74,999) would keep one app; that is its only advantage here.

**Where the amp goes and how the cable gets there.** The TV sits on the Living's north wall, shared with BR-02. The Dining lies east of the Living, in the same open space. The §12 ceiling plan gives the Living only a perimeter cove, with a full drop over Dining only if this option is taken. So a cable from the TV console would have to cross the Living ceiling. Two routes:

| Route | How | Cable run | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Amp in a Dining sideboard (pick)** | Base WiiM Amp on a ventilated shelf in a Homz crockery unit or sideboard in Dining, next to an existing socket. One 25 mm conduit runs up the unit's back into the Dining drop | ~3–6 m | Nothing crosses the Living. Needs a Dining unit in the Homz BOQ and a socket on that wall. Leave a 50 mm gap or vent slots behind the amp |
| Amp in the TV console | Conduit from the TV panel up into the north-wall cove band, along the cove, then into the Dining drop. Homz draws it on the reflected ceiling plan | ~10–14 m | Only if there's no Dining unit. Longer pull, and the cove must have room for the conduit beside the light strip |

If Homz can't show either route on the reflected ceiling plan before design freeze, drop the option. A Sonos Era 100 SL on the sideboard plays the same music as a visible box, with no ceiling work.

**Who does what.**

| Step | Who | When |
|---|---|---|
| Mark 2 speaker positions on the reflected ceiling plan, clear of VRF grilles, lights and beams. ~2.4 m apart, ≥0.6 m from walls, over the table | You + Homz designer | Before Homz design freeze |
| Add to the Homz BOQ: a Dining false-ceiling drop, 2 cut-outs from the speaker's template, 2 MDF back-boxes, and one 25 mm PVC conduit with a pull-string from the amp's shelf up the sideboard's back into the Dining drop (~3 m) | Homz | Before design freeze |
| Deliver the speakers to site | You | Before the Dining ceiling is boarded |
| Pull the OFC cable, leave the pull-string, photograph the run | Homz electrician (low-voltage, no mains work) | Before boarding |
| Cut, fit and wire the speakers | Homz false-ceiling team | After boarding, before painting the ceiling |
| Connect the amp, run RoomFit | You | After move-in |

**What is truly irreversible.** Cutting a speaker into a finished gypsum ceiling is routine; these speakers clamp in from the front. The hard part later is the cable path down to the amp. Without a conduit, someone opens the ceiling or chases a painted wall. So the conduit is the one step that must happen before boarding.

**Back-boxes.** A sealed box stops sound spreading through the shared ceiling void into BR-02 and the kitchen, and stops the gypsum buzzing. The floor above is behind an RCC slab, so it is not the main leak path. Q Acoustics publishes no back-box volume for the QI 65C. It sells fire and acoustic hoods (QI65CF, £72 in the UK) that cut rear sound "by up to 10 dB"; India availability is unverified. Fallback: a sealed 12 mm MDF box of ~10–12 L internal volume with mineral wool inside (engineering judgement, not a published figure).

**Cable length.** On the "wire resistance ≤5% of impedance" rule, 2.5 mm² is good for ~29 m at 8 Ω. The sideboard route is ~3–6 m and the cove route ~10–14 m. 1.5 mm² would also do (~17 m); buy 2.5 mm² anyway, because the cost difference is a few hundred rupees.

### 3.8 Reference: the full in-ceiling system, if you ever want it

Kept so nobody repeats the research. Not recommended.

**Zone map (old plan).** Z1 Drawing pair; Z2 Living pair; Z3 Dining pair; Z4 Kitchen stereo-in-one; Z5 Master pair over the foot side, never over the bed head; Z6 BR-01 pair; Z7 BR-03 pair, never grouped; Z8 BR-02 + Foyer stereo-in-ones. Bathrooms and utility: none.

**Coverage.** KEF's spec sheet gives the Ci160QR "125°" nominal coverage. At a ~2.85 m finished ceiling and 1.2 m seated ear height, one 6.5" unit covers ~6 m across. Every room here is smaller, so a second speaker adds a stereo image. It adds no needed loudness.

**Speakers.**

| Model | ₹ (India) | Cut-out | Depth | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| KEF Ci160QR | 30,000 each (Ooberpad, in stock; 2-year warranty) | **Ø196 mm on KEF's template and accessory guide; Ø191.0 mm on KEF's own spec sheet.** Cut from the paper template in the box | 91 mm | Uni-Q coaxial, 8 Ω, 10–125 W, 89 dB. Minimum rear volume 13 L (KEF cabinet table) or 15 L (spec sheet); ideal 25 L. KEF's RNC160R rear enclosure fits it |
| Q Acoustics QI 65C | 33,900 per pair | Ø203 mm | 79 mm | See 3.7 |
| Q Acoustics QI 65CW (wet room) | 50,900 per pair | Ø197 mm (old note) | 84 mm (old note) | Fixed tweeter |
| Polk RC80i | 28,500 per pair (Ooberpad) | — | — | Offset swivel tweeter |
| Polk RC60i | 21,500 per pair (Ooberpad lists it in stock) | — | — | Old note said it left the lineup; the listing may be old stock |
| Monitor Audio C165-T2 | ~19,999 | — | — | Stereo-in-one, 6 + 6 Ω. Sold out at last check. Two in parallel = 3 Ω (fine); three = 2 Ω (faults a WiiM) |

**Amp routes.**

| Route | Zones | ₹ | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6 × base WiiM Amp | 6 | 2,98,800 | Best per-zone value; six boxes and six power cords |
| 6 × WiiM Mini (₹14,900) + 2-ch Class-D amp | 6 | ~1.5L | Cheapest; amp brand availability in India unverified |
| Denon HEOS Drive HS2 | 4 | 2,31,900 (Ooberpad) | One box, no AirPlay, ~₹58,000 per zone |
| Parasound ZoneMaster ZM12 | 6, but only 2 sources | 3,34,000 (sold out) | Rejected |
| Sonos Amp Multi | 4 | ~3,50,000, enquiry only | Rejected |
| 6 × Sonos Amp | 6 | 4,49,994 | Rejected: 2.4 GHz only, no Alexa in India |

**Cable rule.** Home-run each zone in conduit with a pull-string, star topology, labelled, crossing mains at 90°. At 8 Ω: 1.5 mm² ≈ 17 m, 2.5 mm² ≈ 29 m, 4 mm² ≈ 46 m. Halve each at 4 Ω. Any leg ≥28 m goes to 4 mm².

**Amp location.** Not the utility. It is a laundry space at floor 42, humid and hot, with water nearby. The linen store (5'0" × 3'10") is central and dry, and gives shorter runs. Rack ventilation and power are §2's call.

**70 V / 100 V distribution.** Rejected. It suits offices and stadiums. Every consumer streaming amp here is low-impedance only.

### A4.1 What changed from the old note

The owner's new facts change these things.

1. **Geysers left §4.** They now sit in §11. The old ₹6.0–6.5L bucket included about ₹51k of geysers.
2. **No rewiring, but additions are allowed.** One rule, shared with §9: use an existing 16 A point where one reaches. If none does, Homz's electrician adds one, by surface conduit inside the carcass or a new circuit from the DB through the false ceiling. Never chase a finished wall. Check the load budget (A4.13) first. The oven stays; a built-in induction domino becomes possible if the load budget allows.
3. **Homz builds the kitchen; the owner buys appliances.** Keep appliances out of the Homz BOQ, or have each model and price listed, so nothing is bought twice (A07a). A4.4 lists every slot size Homz needs.
4. **The worktop is the builder's existing granite platform (§9).** Homz keeps the U platform, its height, its support walls and the glazed dado above it. So the platform, not Homz, sets the bay widths and the hob cut-out. Measure it first (A4.4). Any fix that breaks a support wall or the stone's base is civil work, which is out of scope.
5. **The utility stays open (§11).** Enclosing it breaks the balcony undertaking. Both Bosch laundry manuals forbid outdoor use, and the dryer manual limits it to "enclosed spaces". The machines must sit where rain never reaches them. That applies to both, so the dryer is a Should, stacked, per the owner's 9 Sep ask (A4.6).
6. **Bathrooms and VRF are done.** Nothing in §4 touches them.
7. **No dishwasher (owner, 2026-10-02).** The bay beside the sink becomes storage: a Blum drawer bank with the two-bin pull-out (§12). The DW rows, slot, T-valve, hose, leak sensor and monthly upkeep are gone. The old pick is kept below as a rejected row.

Stock moved a lot since the old note. The old hob pick (Bosch PNH6B6F20I), induction domino (PIB375FB5I) and IFB VX14 dishwasher are out of stock. The old fridge pick (LG GR-B24FQGJB) and combo (LG FHD1308STB) no longer appear in Amazon.in search. Each row below was in stock somewhere on 2026-10-01, except where noted.
