### A4.3 Where to buy, and the Bosch warranty

Bosch's own store was out of stock on the washer on 2026-10-01. The Amazon offers come from third-party sellers, and the seller behind each offer changes week to week.

Bosch India's warranty terms say three things that matter here.
- "Repairs, installations & removal of the appliance shall be carried out by an Authorized Service Personnel, failing which this warranty will be void." So the Homz carpenter must not install the hob or oven.
- The warranty starts on the date of purchase. A machine bought early and installed months later loses those months.
- The terms do not require an authorised dealer. Bosch India does not publish a list of authorised Amazon sellers (not found).

**Rule.** Buy from a seller that issues a GST invoice in your name. Before paying, give the serial number to Bosch service (1800-266-1880) and confirm it is Indian stock. Register on MyBosch. Order each appliance about 2 weeks before its install date, not months ahead. Ask a Hyderabad Bosch dealer for a bundle price on all four; it may beat Amazon (unverified).

### A4.4 Slot sizes for the Homz drawings

Homz owns the drawings. Give them this table, and ask for it on the kitchen elevations with model numbers. Any model change after sign-off needs a re-check of the slot.

**Measure the builder platform first.** The kitchen keeps its builder U platform (§9). Its support walls, not Homz, fix the bay widths. Record four things at handover, with photos:

1. Height from floor to the underside of whatever carries the stone.
2. What carries the stone: brick walls with open space below, or a kadapa or RCC shelf directly under it.
3. Clear width between support walls in each bay, especially the bays either side of the sink.
4. Whether a hob cut-out already exists, and what sits under it.

**Gates.**
- **Bay beside the sink.** No dishwasher, so this bay takes a drawer bank or the two-bin pull-out (§12). Record its clear width and height anyway: Homz sizes the drawers to it.
- **Hob.** The Bosch body is 45 mm deep overall (Bosch spec sheet). If a kadapa or RCC shelf sits directly under the stone, a cut-out exposes the shelf and the hob has nowhere to sit. Cutting the shelf is civil work. Then the Elica FLEXI goes on top of the platform (A4.7).

| Appliance | Slot (W × H × D, mm) | Other conditions |
|---|---|---|
| Gas hob | Cut-out 555–557 × 475–477 in the existing stone, wet-cut by Homz's stone vendor | Open space below. ≥100 mm to a side wall, ≥50 mm to the front edge, ≥55 mm to the back, worktop ≥600 mm deep. 30 mm stone or a Bosch spacer at the cut-out (A4.7). PNG point and a 6 A ignition socket below |
| Chimney | 900 wide, centred on the hob | Mount height per the Elica manual (commonly 650–750 mm above a gas hob; unverified for this model). Socket above the hood line. Duct route to the builder's core-cut |
| Built-in oven (if bought) | Niche 560–568 × 575–597 × ≥550 | Leave the 320 × 115 mm socket space at the back. Oven front 595 × 594 mm |
| Microwave | Open niche about 600 × 400 × 500, on the counter or at a 16 A point | Draws 2.2 kW on convection (IFB). Not in the tall unit unless a 16 A point already sits there. Keep the side and top gaps the IFB manual gives. Unit is 539 × 300 × 440 mm |
| Fridge | Opening ≥870 × 2,000 × 650 | Flat hinges (Bosch spec), so the side gap is small. Still measure the spot and the side wall. 240 cm cord |
| RO, under-sink | Sink base must take 270 × 420 × 410 | Socket inside the base. Tap hole wet-cut in the existing granite by Homz's stone vendor before install (AO Smith does not drill) |
| Washer + dryer | Side by side: 1,250 × 650 floor, counter at ~900. Stacked: 600 × 650, about 1,700 tall with the kit | On the floor, no plinth (A4.6). Wall furthest from the open side, under a Homz overhang |

### A4.5 Points and hookups: who does what

The builder fitted a chimney provision, an RO provision, a PNG point and separate municipal and bore lines. No document lists socket positions or circuits. Indian builders usually fit fridge, microwave, RO, chimney and washer points. They often omit oven and dryer points (general pattern, not verified for The Olympus).

**Rule (same as §9).** A 2–3.3 kW appliance needs a 16 A socket on a 16 A circuit, not just a 16 A faceplate. Use an existing 16 A point where one reaches. If none does, Homz's electrician adds one: surface conduit inside the carcass, or a new circuit from the DB through the false ceiling and down inside the joinery. Never chase the glazed dado or a finished wall. Check the load budget (A4.13) first. One appliance per socket, no extension boards.

| Job | Needed if | Who | Rough cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| Map every existing point and its breaker | Always, at handover | Owner, with photos, a tape and the DB labels | 0 |
| Move the RO supply into the sink base | Only if you want the under-sink RO and the point is above | Same | as above |
| No 16 A point reaches the oven or microwave bay | Never add a circuit from the board | Nobody: skip that appliance, or move the microwave to a counter socket | 0 |
| Natural-gas nozzle swap and flame test | Always, Bosch hob | Bosch service at installation | Get it on the job card |
| Gas line from the PNG point to the hob | Always | The flat's PNG operator (BGL or Megha Gas: read the meter plate) or its approved fitter | Ask the operator |
| Chimney hood, duct and wall-sleeve flap | Always | Elica installer for the hood; Homz boxes the duct | Duct and flap extra, ~₹2,000–5,000 (unverified) |

Fit braided-steel inlet hoses on the washer and RO on day one. Rubber hoses split with age.

### A4.6 Laundry in a 4 ft 11 in open utility

The utility is 1,499 mm (4 ft 11 in) wide and faces east. Its length is ~12 ft 4 in (~3.76 m), the figure §7.4 uses; scaled from the plan, unverified, so measure it at handover. The plan shows a washer position and a utility sink. §11 leaves it open.

**What the Bosch manuals say.**
- Washer and dryer: "Do not install or operate this appliance anywhere that is liable to experience frost or outdoors."
- Dryer: intended use is "in private households and in enclosed spaces in a domestic environment". Room 5–35 °C. Hyderabad afternoons pass 35 °C in April and May.
- Both manuals: "Do not install a washer-dryer stack on a platform; the appliances may tip over."
- Washer: "When using the appliance on a base, the appliance may tip over. Always secure the feet of the appliance to a base using the manufacturer's fixing brackets."

A covered utility is a judgement call under "outdoors". Direct rain on the machine is not. The dryer's "enclosed spaces" line is stricter: keep its bay covered and closed on three sides, and skip drying on April–May afternoons above 35 °C.

**Placement.** Put the machines on the floor, on the wall furthest from the opening. Ask Homz for a tall unit or worktop overhang above them, with a drip edge on its front lip, so rain runs off in front of the machines. No plinth: the stack must never stand on one, and a single washer on one needs Bosch fixing brackets. Bosch India lists no bracket part (the EU parts WMZ2200 and WMZ2381 return 404 on bosch-home.in), so the floor is the simple answer. Keep 50 mm behind for hoses. With a 6-year-old in the flat, a machine that walks at 1,200 rpm is the failure to avoid.

| Option | Floor space | Time per dry | Cost | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Washer + ceiling airer** | 600 × 650 mm | air-dry | ₹46k + airer | **Backup.** Light loads and delicates. Hyderabad dries clothes fast eight months a year |
| Bosch washer + heat-pump dryer, side by side | 1,250 × 650 mm | ~4.5 h for 10 kg cotton at 1,200 rpm (4:17 at 1,400, 4:48 at 1,000); 1:10 for 3.5 kg synthetics; 2.35–2.65 kWh a cycle (Bosch manual) | ~₹1.13L | Alternative only if the owner finds the room after measuring. Folding counter on top, controls at waist height, no kit needed |
| Same pair stacked | 600 × 650 mm | same | ~₹1.13L + kit | **Pick** (owner, 9 Sep: a washer-dryer or a stack, no room side by side). On the floor only, with Bosch kit WTZ27400 (out of stock at Bosch India) or WTZ27410. Dryer controls near 1.6 m |
| LG WashTower FWT1310BG | 600 × 660 mm; ~1,655 mm tall (listing, unverified) | heat pump | ₹1,61,000 (Amz, 23 reviews) | ~₹48k more than the Bosch pair. Thin reviews |
| LG FHD1207STB 12/7 kg combo | 600 × 565 mm | Several hours; not compared, LG cycle time unverified | ₹63,790 | Dries only 7 kg of a 12 kg wash, so big loads go in two parts. Condenser drying uses more power than a heat pump (general rule, unverified for this model). Lint handling not checked. Value route if you dry only some loads. LG inlet-pressure minimum not checked |

**Dryer versus combo.** Both are slow, so speed doesn't decide it. The heat-pump dryer dries a full 10 kg wash in one go at ~2.4 kWh, and the washer stays free while it runs. The combo saves ~₹49k and 600 mm of floor, but dries 7 kg at a time.

**Dryer chores and power.** No self-cleaning condenser (Bosch spec). Rinse the fluff filters after every cycle and the base filter as the manual says. It draws 600 W on 10 A protection, so a normal socket works.

**Correction.** The old note said no heat-pump combo exists in India. True for single-drum machines. The LG WashTower is a heat-pump washer and dryer sold as one stacked unit.

**Other corners.**
- **Hard water.** Hyderabad bore water scales washer heaters. The softener decision sits in §5.
- **Child.** Set the child lock on both machines.
- **Drain.** Run the dryer's condensate hose to the washer drain, so nobody empties the tank.

### A4.7 Gas hob on PNG, worktop thickness, and induction

The flat runs on PNG. Every Bosch India gas hob ships preset for LPG with a natural-gas nozzle set in the box. The nozzle swap is the commonest real failure. Make the swap and a blue-flame test a written condition of the sale.

| Bosch hob | Width | Burners | FFSD documented | Stock (Bosch, 2026-10-01) | ₹ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PNH7O6G20I** | 70 cm | 4 (2.2, 3.0, 2×1.4 kW) | Yes, "all burners" | In stock | 44,990 |
| PNH6B6F20I (old pick) | 60 cm | 4 | Yes | Out of stock | MRP 46,990 |
| PND7B6F20I | 75 cm | 3 (2.3, 2×4.0 kW) | Yes | In stock | 52,990 |
| PND7B6G20I | 75 cm | 3 | Not stated | In stock | 58,990 |
| PNH9O6G20I | 90 cm | 4 | Yes | In stock | 52,990 |
| PNF9B6F20I | 90 cm | 4 | Yes | In stock | 65,490 |

Flame failure is a must with a six-year-old in the house. The 70 cm four-burner fits a standard run and leaves space either side.

**Worktop thickness.** Bosch: "Minimum worktop thickness (without spacer / with spacer, order separately) = 30 mm / 12 mm." The builder's granite is likely 18–20 mm; measure it. The Bosch India page lists no accessories for this hob, and the spec sheet names no spacer part.

**One rule, shared with §9.** Ask Bosch service (1800-266-1880) for the spacer part number for 18–20 mm stone. If they give one, order it with the hob. If not, there are two fallbacks:
1. A 30 mm built-up edge: a granite strip bonded under the cut-out perimeter. This works only if the stone has open space below it (A4.4) and the fabricator agrees in writing before cutting.
2. A freestanding FFD cooktop on the platform. No cut-out, no thickness rule, no civil work.

**What sits under the stone decides it.** Check before ordering the Bosch. A kadapa or RCC shelf directly under the stone blocks the 45 mm hob body. Cutting that shelf is civil work, so go straight to fallback 2.

**Freestanding fallback and value pick.**

| Model | Image | ₹ | Freestanding? | PNG | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Elica RG FLEXI 470 LOTUS IND HD BRASS FFD**, 70 cm, 4 burners | [<img src="https://m.media-amazon.com/images/I/61F1RIXXuQL._SL300_.jpg" width="96">](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0GV7Z1NHX) | 18,990 (Amz) | The non-FFD RG FLEXI 470 is sold as "Built-in & Freestanding". Not stated for the FFD SKU: confirm with Elica (unverified) | "PNG conversion kit available separately" (price unverified) | FFD on all burners, battery ignition, 2-yr comprehensive, 10-yr burners |
| Sunflame Flexi Hob 76 cm, 3 burners, FFD | [<img src="https://m.media-amazon.com/images/I/51-0TpDaRdL._SL300_.jpg" width="96">](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0F59RW2GN) | 19,299 (Amz) | Yes: "suitable for countertop and built-in installation" | Not stated; the listing names only an LPG inlet (unverified) | Battery ignition, 2-yr product, 5-yr glass, pan-India service claimed |

Elica is the pick because its listing names a PNG kit. Elica service must fit the kit and confirm in writing that flame failure still works afterwards. A freestanding hob stands roughly 80–130 mm proud of the stone (typical, unverified) and needs a wipe-down round its edges. That is the cost of avoiding civil work.

**Induction.** The Bosch PIB375FB5I domino is 3.7 kW, ships with no plug and is out of stock. It needs a dedicated hard-wired circuit. That is allowed if it runs through the false ceiling and inside the joinery. **Decision:** buy a portable 2.1 kW plate for PNG outages and fast boils, on a 16 A counter socket. A built-in domino can come back if Homz prices the circuit and the load budget (A4.13) has room.

### A4.8 Chimney, duct and make-up air

- **Width.** A 90 cm hood over a 70 cm hob overhangs both sides.
- **Duct.** 6-inch rigid smooth PVC to the builder's core-cut, at most two bends. Corrugated flexible duct loses roughly 30% of flow (old-note figure, unverified).
- **Route.** On the brochure plan the hob sits on the wall shared with the utility, so the core-cut probably opens into the utility. If it is on another wall, the duct crosses the ceiling and Homz boxes it.
- **Floor-42 wind.** Fit a gravity non-return flap inside the wall sleeve, from indoors or the utility side. Keep the builder's existing outside louvre. Plan no work on the façade: no installer works outside on floor 42.
- **Make-up air.** A strong hood in a sealed VRF flat starves for replacement air. Default to a passive path: a door undercut or a transfer grille. First test which way air moves at the entry door (§8.1).

### A4.9 RO: format and the fluoride trap

**Why RO is a Must.** The old note cites CGWB's Rangareddy aquifer report (March 2022): fluoride up to 5.09 mg/L and nitrate up to 515 mg/L in district groundwater. UV and UF remove neither. Not re-checked this round. Test both of the flat's lines (§5).

**The trap.** KENT's "TDS control" blends UF-only water back in to raise minerals. That blend carries fluoride and nitrate straight through. If you buy the KENT, set the knob to minimum and test the output. AO Smith's Z2 Pro states 100% RO with no bypass and adds minerals after the membrane.

**Format follows the builder's RO point.**

| Builder RO point | Pick | Needs | ₹ |
|---|---|---|---|
| Inside the sink base | AO Smith Z2 Pro, under-sink | Tap hole in the granite | 23,990 |
| On the wall above the counter | AO Smith Z5, wall-mount | Nothing new | 14,799 |
| On the wall, budget | KENT Grand | Nothing new; knob at minimum | 11,899 |

Decline every AMC. Pay per filter change. Put a leak sensor under the sink (§8).

### A4.10 Fridge and the service lift

**Pick logic.** The Bosch KCP88E256I is 594 mm deep, flush with 600 mm cabinets. 503 L suits a family of three. No dispenser: dispensers freeze, leak and eat freezer space.

**Lift check (corrected).** The old note said a 914 mm fridge couldn't pass an 800 mm lift door. That logic was wrong: a fridge goes through a door depth-first. The binding numbers are door height and car height. The Bosch is 1,925 mm unpacked and 2,018 × 902 × 656 mm crated. The guide uses one set of lift numbers everywhere (A12.9): a ~1,100 × 1,300 mm car, ~2,300 mm high, with a ~800 × 2,100 mm door. These are typical figures, unverified for this tower. On those numbers the upright crate passes with ~80 mm to spare. Fridges travel upright. If the crate won't fit, uncrate it downstairs and move it upright on a trolley.

**Placement.** The plan puts the fridge in the corner beside the kitchen entry. Bosch lists flat hinges for this model, so the doors need little side gap to open. Still measure the spot and the side wall before ordering. The 240 cm cord reaches a socket up to about 2 m away; never use an extension board.

**Measure at handover:** car width, depth and height; door clear width and height; flat entry; kitchen opening; fridge spot width and the side wall.

**Value alternative.** The Samsung RF57A5032S9 is ₹19k cheaper. It is 765 mm deep per its listing, so it stands ~165 mm proud of the cabinets.

### A4.11 Oven and microwave

**KISS default:** one convection microwave in an open niche. It reheats, grills and bakes for a family of three.

**Built-in oven, Optional.** Take it only if someone bakes weekly and a 16 A point already sits at the tall unit. The Serie 2 HBF011BA1I is ₹77,990 at Bosch. The ₹50,830 Amazon offer is 35% lower, so confirm the seller and serial with Bosch first (A4.3). The steam HUA717BA0I costs ₹1,22,990 at Bosch; steam isn't worth it for occasional baking.

Keep the oven and microwave separate. A combination unit is a single point of failure on an expensive part.

### A4.12 Water pressure on floor 42

The old A20 put pressure at the flat at about 1 bar, falling under flow, if fed from the roof tank. §20 asks the facility about a top-floor booster.

| Appliance | Minimum inlet pressure | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Bosch washer WGA252ZSIN | 0.3 bar | Bosch manual |
| AO Smith Z2 Pro RO | 7–30 psi (0.5–2 bar) | AO Smith |
| LG FHD1207STB combo | not checked; LG front-loaders often quote 1 bar (unverified) | |

At ~1 bar the Bosch picks work. Measure static pressure at the utility and kitchen taps with a ₹500 gauge at handover. If it is below a machine's minimum, the building booster is the fix (§20). An in-flat pump is plumbing work, which is out of scope. If a booster raises line pressure above 2 bar, the RO installer fits a pressure-reducing valve on the RO feed.

### A4.13 Electrical load these appliances add

This table feeds the §20 load budget. Geysers are counted in §11.

| Appliance | Rated load | Source | Plug or point |
|---|---|---|---|
| Washer | 2.3 kW | Bosch manual | 16 A |
| Heat-pump dryer | 0.6 kW | Bosch manual: 10 A minimum protection | normal socket |
| Oven (if bought) | 3.3 kW | Bosch spec | 16 A India plug |
| Portable induction | 2.1 kW | Philips listing | 16 A counter socket |
| Microwave | 2.2 kW on convection, 1.4 kW on microwave | IFB product page | 16 A counter socket |
| Chimney | 0.13 kW | Elica listing | 6 A |
| RO | 0.05 kW | AO Smith spec | 6 A |
| Fridge | ~0.06 kW average (480 kWh/yr) | Bosch spec | check plug |
| Hob ignition | negligible | Bosch spec | 6 A India plug |

All at once is about 10.7 kW (2.3 + 0.6 + 3.3 + 2.1 + 2.2 + 0.13 + 0.05 + 0.06), or 7.4 kW without the Optional oven. Real peaks overlap two or three heaters, about 4–6 kW. Run the washer on delay start, outside the morning geyser window.

## A5. Air and water quality — detail

*Timeline: water test now; purifiers and monitor at move-in; dehumidifier only after a monsoon of readings.*

What changed from the old note: the purifier picks moved to models you can actually buy in October 2026. Purifiers plus monitor fell from ₹1.5–1.7L to ~₹53k at move-in (three units), or ~₹86k if all five are bought. The monitor is now a ₹2.9k CO₂ meter. The ERV is dropped. The dehumidifier is conditional. The "whole-home softener as base case" rule is replaced by a check on how the flat is plumbed.

### A5.1 Purifiers

**Why you need them.** The VRF recirculates room air. It doesn't filter PM2.5 to HEPA grade and doesn't bring in fresh air. So standalone HEPA purifiers do the particulate work.

**Size for winter, not the annual mean.** Hyderabad's annual PM2.5 is ~40 µg/m³ (IQAir annual series, 39–51 over five years). The monthly swing is ~3×: Jun ~18 against Nov ~59 and Jan ~57 (IQAir World Air Quality Report 2023, via the old note; not re-checked this round). Use ~55–60 µg/m³ as the design load. Move-in falls in that peak. IQAir's Hyderabad page showed no monthly history on 2026-10-01, so the monthly figures stay unverified.

**Room volumes** (from [[my-home]]; ceiling assumed ~10 ft, measure it):

| Room | Floor area | Volume | Air changes/hr at full speed, one AC3221 (520 m³/h) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Master | 169 sq ft | ~48 m³ | ~11 | Must. Kabir sleeps here. Run on Auto or Sleep; ~5 ACH at a quiet speed |
| BR-01 | 169 sq ft | ~48 m³ | ~11 | Must. Work-from-home room; 8+ hours a day |
| BR-02 | 155 sq ft | ~44 m³ | ~12 | Should. Kabir's daytime study and play room |
| BR-03 (Gurudwara) | 155 sq ft | ~44 m³ | — | No unit planned. Add one only if incense or a jot burns daily |
| Living + Dining | 391 sq ft | ~111 m³ | ~4.7 | Must. Open to the Drawing; with the Drawing unit, two units share ~176 m³ |
| Drawing | 230 sq ft | ~65 m³ | ~8 | Should. Formal room, used occasionally |

CADR figures are the maker's claims under the Chinese GB/T test, not AHAM. Real delivery at quiet speeds is far lower. That's why the plan uses one large unit per room rather than a smaller one at full speed.

**Model comparison** (prices from Amazon.in on 2026-10-01, GST-inclusive):

| Model | CADR m³/h | ₹ | Filter ₹ | India status, Oct 2026 | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| [Philips AC3221/60](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0H3PJKRXC) | 520 | 16,552 | FY3200/30 set, [₹3,999](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0F63D1XNJ) | In stock. Philips India service network | **Pick.** Best CADR per rupee of the in-stock brands |
| [Philips AC3220/60](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0DNK11RY1) | 520 | 19,850 | FY3200/30, same | "Only 1 left" | Same machine family. Buy whichever is cheaper |
| [Xiaomi Air Purifier 4 Lite](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0C1P65Y4H) | 360 | 10,999 | Genuine price not found; third-party ₹1,500–2,300 | In stock | **Value pick for bedrooms only.** Adds a second filter type to stock |
| [Mijia Air Purifier 6](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0HCYVZYGK) | 443 | 17,999 | Not found | In stock | Rejected. Costs more than the AC3221 for less CADR. Has a negative-ion generator and UVC, which add no PM benefit |
| Xiaomi Smart Air Purifier 4 | 400 (387 on the India listing) | last ₹17,999 | ~₹3,499 (old note) | **"Currently unavailable"** on [Amazon.in](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0C1P45C31); mi.com shows no price | Dropped. The old pick |
| Philips Series 3000i AC3059 | 520 | — | FY3430/30, ₹5,089 | Philips page lists it with no buy option | Dropped. Superseded by AC3220/3221 |
| [Coway Airmega 150](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B08GJ57MKL) | not on the listing | 16,999 | 8,500 hr life claimed | Sold via importer Frootle India; [cowayindia.in](https://cowayindia.in/) is live | Rejected on size. The old note's "Coway has no India presence" was wrong |
| Dyson, Levoit | — | — | — | Dyson publishes no CADR; Levoit has no India filter channel | Excluded (unchanged) |

**Deploy in two steps.** Buy three AC3221s at move-in (₹49,656): Master, BR-01 and Living/Dining. These are the rooms used most hours a day. After a month, read the PM2.5 on those three units. Add the Drawing and BR-02 units (₹33,104) only if the Living unit can't hold PM2.5 under ~15 µg/m³ with the Drawing open, or BR-02 is in daily use for hours. All five cost ₹82,760. The value mix (Xiaomi 4 Lite in the bedrooms) saves money but adds a second filter type. The plan prefers one model.

**Running cost.** One FY3200/30 set per unit per year is ₹12k for three, ₹20k for five. Change the Master and BR-01 sets every six months and add ₹8k. That replaces the old ₹35–50k/yr. Filter life in Indian winter loads is unverified. Philips claims up to 12 months; trust the unit's filter indicator. Electricity is small: 36 W at full speed.

**Timing.** Buy at move-in, not during carpentry. Fine sanding and gypsum dust would load a new HEPA filter within weeks. Run all units on high for the first week, then vacuum the pre-filters.

**Buy genuine filters only.** Counterfeit and "compatible" filters are common on Amazon.in. Buy items sold by the brand or its listed distributor.

### A5.2 Monitoring

One CO₂ meter, in the master bedroom. The purifiers already display PM2.5, temperature and humidity in every room, so a second PM monitor duplicates them. The missing number is CO₂. Three people asleep behind a closed door in a 48 m³ room can pass 1,500 ppm, and the VRF brings in no fresh air.

**Only NDIR meters measure CO₂.** Cheap "CO₂" readings from a VOC sensor (eCO₂) are estimates. Buy a listing that names an NDIR sensor. Several do on Amazon.in at ₹2.9–5k.

Options, Amazon.in search and Airveda's catalogue, 2026-10-01, GST-inclusive:

| Model | Measures | ₹ | Sensor | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [INKBIRDPLUS portable CO₂ detector](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0CG348YS1) | CO₂, temp, RH | 2,898 | NDIR, auto calibration (listing title) | **Pick.** Measures the one missing number |
| [INKBIRDPLUS portable CO₂ detector, colour LCD](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0GYZ1MQGM) | CO₂, temp, RH | 3,499 | NDIR (listing title) | Same family, ₹601 more. Backup if the pick is out of stock |
| [INKBIRDPLUS PTH-9A 6-in-1](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0B7QG8D8R) | CO₂, PM, HCHO, TVOC, temp, RH | 4,764 | Not stated in the title | Rejected: PM duplicates the purifiers |
| [YOWEXA CO₂ monitor](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0FVXFN5KG) | CO₂, temp, RH | 4,954 | NDIR (in the title) | Alternative if the INKBIRD is out of stock |
| **Airveda Pulse** PM2510CTH | PM2.5, PM10, CO₂, temp, RH | 23,999 | CO₂ sensor type not published | **Premium.** Only if you want an Indian-calibrated PM unit too |
| Airveda Pulse Touch PM2510CTH-TW | Same, 3.5" touchscreen | 29,925 ([Amazon](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0DB5SD3CT)) | Same | Same sensors for ₹5,926 more |
| Airveda Aurora / Sky | PM only | 18,999 / 10,999 | — | No CO₂ |
| Aranet4 | CO₂, temp, RH, pressure | — | NDIR | Reference-grade, but no seller listing on Amazon.in on 2026-10-01. Import only |

Brand reliability for the ₹2.9–5k meters is unknown. NDIR is the seller's claim. Check it on day 1: by an open window it should read ~400–450 ppm. Their auto-calibration assumes the room reaches outdoor air about weekly, so air the bedroom fully once a week.

**What to do with the reading.** Over ~1,200 ppm overnight: leave the bedroom door ajar or crack a window ~2 cm. At floor 42 the wind makes a wide opening impractical.

### A5.3 Fresh air (ERV): dropped

The old note called a ducted ERV "Phase 1 or never" and quoted Zehnder Metro 120 ERV-ECM or Panasonic FV-10VE1. It is now dropped.

- It needs supply and exhaust ducts above Homz's false ceiling, two façade penetrations of ~100–150 mm, and a new power point. The owner's flat needs no electrical work, and a high-rise façade hole needs RWA and structural consent.
- No India price was ever published for the Zehnder unit. The Panasonic unit moves ~100 CFM, so several would be needed.
- The CO₂ problem it solves is small at night and easy to manage with a door.

Revisit only if the monitor reads >1,200 ppm on most nights with the door ajar. Astberg and "Ventara" stay rejected (no reachable India presence).

### A5.4 Off-gassing from new joinery

Homz will fill the flat with board, laminate and adhesive. These release formaldehyde for weeks. Two contract asks:

1. Write the emission grade of every board into the BOQ: E1 or better, E0 where the brand offers it. This turns a vague "branded ply" into a checkable spec (unverified which grades Homz stocks).
2. Leave 2 weeks (4 if the flat still smells) between installation and move-in, windows open in daytime. The AC3221's carbon layer helps; it doesn't replace airing.

### A5.5 Humidity and the dehumidifier

The old note called a dehumidifier "a requirement". That overstated it. The case:

- A VRF controls to a dry-bulb set point. In mild, humid weather it cools quickly and cycles off, removing little moisture (DOE/PNNL guidance cited in the old note).
- Hyderabad's morning RH runs ~80% in Jul–Sep (IMD normals via the old note; not re-checked).
- Against it: Nov–May is dry. Most VRF indoor units have a Dry mode. Every recommended purifier shows RH. You'll know within one monsoon.

**Watch RH from June 2027.** The purifiers report RH in the rooms that have one. The private-rooms brief already puts a ThermoPro TP49 hygrometer (₹711, [Amazon](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B07WCR5Y4B)) in the walk-in closet and the store. Add one in BR-03: it has no purifier, and it holds the Saroop and rumalas. Don't buy hygrometers for rooms with a purifier.

**The rule has two halves, because two rooms have no AC.**

| Where | Trigger | Action |
|---|---|---|
| Rooms with a VRF indoor unit | RH above 60% for a week | Run the VRF in Dry mode. Buy a dehumidifier only if that fails |
| Walk-in closet (ex-puja) and store (ex-linen) | TP49 above 60% for a week in monsoon | No window, no AC, so Dry mode can't reach them. Buy the Sharp DW-P10M-W. This is the most likely reason you'll ever buy one |

**Placement.** Don't run a 10 L/day compressor unit inside the closed closet (~24 sq ft) or store (~19 sq ft). It would warm the room and fill its 2.5 L tank within hours. Stand it in the living room at their doorway, with the closet and store doors open, and run it for a few hours a day. Homz's trimmed door leaves (10–15 mm, private-rooms brief) help air move when the doors are shut. Empty the tank; it needs no new drain point or socket. Don't run a hose across a floor or under a door: Kabir will trip on it.

| Model | Capacity | Area | ₹ | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [Sharp DW-P10M-W](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0BH2V48P6) | 10 L/day | ~250 sq ft | 21,450 | 2.5 L tank plus drain hose. The old note said 12–20 L/day; the listing says 10 |
| [Sharp DW-J20FM-W](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B07TC54LS4) | 20 L/day | ~550 sq ft | 35,630 | Adds a HEPA purifier (CADR 258). 1-year onsite warranty. Use if one unit must cover the open zone |
| Novita ND 298 | — | — | — | Old value pick; not re-checked |

### A5.6 Water

**The facts.** The kitchen has separate municipal and bore lines (builder spec). Which line feeds each bathroom is unknown. Bathrooms came finished with concealed pipes, so nothing here should involve re-plumbing.

**Step 0: ask the builder for the as-built plumbing drawing.** It's free. It shows which line feeds each bathroom and the kitchen, and where each bathroom inlet runs. That decides both the test and whether a softener can be fitted at all. Label every stop-cock while you're at it.

**Step 1: test both lines at IHS.** Flush the tanks first. The lab is the Institute of Health Systems, SRH Campus, Kukatpally 500072. Phone +91 40 23210136 / 23210139, ihs@ihs.org.in. Use `ihs.org.in`, not the unrelated `ihslab.com`. Codes and prices below are from IHS's own catalogue PDF, checked 2026-10-01:

| Code | Name | For | ₹ | Includes hardness, fluoride, nitrate? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GPT` | Groundwater / Borewell Potability Tests | Bore line | 2,400 | Yes, plus TDS and coliforms |
| `BPT` | Basic Potability Tests | Municipal line | 1,400 | Yes |
| `CPT` | City-water Potability Tests | Municipal, fuller | 2,100 | Yes, adds ammonia and nitrite |
| `HRO` | Household RO | RO output, after install | 1,700 | Yes |
| `GSP` | Groundwater-fed Softener water Profile | After a softener, to prove it works | 2,700 | Yes |
| `GSQ` | Groundwater-fed softener quality | Not needed | 2,400 | Same price as GPT; order by code |

The NABL accreditation number (T-4179 / TC-15240 in the old note) is not printed on IHS's pages. Confirm it on NABL's directory if it matters. GPT excludes pesticides, toxic metals and radioactivity.

**Step 2: read the result against IS 10500:2012** (verified from the BIS text hosted by CPCB):

| Parameter | Acceptable | Permissible, no other source |
|---|---|---|
| Total dissolved solids | 500 mg/L | 2,000 mg/L |
| Total hardness as CaCO₃ | 200 mg/L | 600 mg/L |
| Fluoride | 1.0 mg/L | 1.5 mg/L |
| Nitrate as NO₃ | 45 mg/L | no relaxation |

**Step 3: decide.**

- **Drinking (owned by §4).** RO is needed if the line feeding the kitchen tap exceeds 500 TDS, 1.0 fluoride or 45 nitrate. The kitchen has both lines, so the test also tells §4 which line to connect.
- **Bathing (hardness as CaCO₃ on the line feeding each bathroom):**

| Hardness | Action | ₹ |
|---|---|---|
| < 200 | Nothing | 0 |
| 200–350 | [KENT Bathroom Softener 5.5 L](https://www.kent.co.in/products/water-softener-kent-bathroom-water-softener) per hard-water bathroom; value [Bepure M7 7 L](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0G4TW3KQR) | 14,949 / 7,998 each |
| > 350 | First ask the RWA for a building-level softener on the bore supply. If the flat has a single inlet, a 25–30 L automatic such as [IONIX 30 L](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0HG9JZTLQ) or [Bepure A28](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0DB8CTMVZ) (resin volume not stated). If each bathroom has its own down-take, fit point-of-use units instead | 55,700 / 34,999, plus plumber |

**Fitting a point-of-use softener is plumbing work.** KENT's own page says it "is installed directly on the bathroom water inlet pipe" and "can be installed by a plumber in under 30 minutes". Who does it: KENT's installation support (9278912345, listed on the product page) or Homz's plumber. Ask KENT first; it keeps the 1-year warranty clean. It stands on the floor, 380 × 220 × 890 mm. So it needs an exposed stretch of inlet pipe (a shaft door, a loft or a visible stop-cock) and floor space next to it. Three of the four bathrooms are 5'0" × 7'11". The resin bed also drops pressure, and floor 42 may get only ~1 bar. Before buying more than one unit:

1. Find each bathroom's inlet on the as-built drawing or in the §11 bathroom photo walk. If the inlet is concealed in tile, a softener there means breaking tile. Skip that bathroom.
2. Do the §20 bucket test before and after the first unit goes in. If flow falls below ~6 L/min, don't add more.
3. KENT's page showed both "Add to Cart" and "Sold Out" labels on 2026-10-01, so stock is unclear. Call KENT before ordering. The Bepure M7 7 L (₹7,998) was in stock on Amazon.in; its resin type and regeneration interval were not checked.

**Look around the corner.** High-rise flats often feed each bathroom from its own riser in a shaft. Then no "whole-flat" inlet exists, and a whole-flat softener means new pipework. That contradicts the owner's no-plumbing fact, which is why point-of-use is the realistic default.

**KENT details** (verified on kent.co.in, 2026-10-01; prices include tax):

| Model | Resin | Regenerates after | Size | Power | ₹ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bathroom Softener | 5.5 L | ~1,500 L at 200 ppm hardness; manual salt | 380 × 220 × 890 mm, floor-standing | None | 14,949 |
| Automatic 8 L | 8 L | ~2,200 L at 200 ppm; automatic | 472 × 225 × 648 mm, wall-mount | Needs a socket | 30,500 |

KENT's own FAQ rates the 8 L for 1–3 people or a single bathroom or kitchen point. It is not a whole-flat unit for 2,880 sq ft. At 400 ppm, regeneration intervals halve.

**Where the old base-rate argument stands.** The old note cited CGWB district data for Rangareddy: groundwater TDS of 938 mg/L pre-monsoon and 1,203 post-monsoon, with no source URL. That makes a hard bore line likely. Neither those figures nor any Rangareddy fluoride or nitrate figure were checked this round. The CGWB site failed TLS checks and returned 404 on its district-brochure paths on 2026-10-01. The "up to 1,000 ppm hardness in Hyderabad" claim remains uncitable. None of this changes the plan: the IHS test measures fluoride and nitrate on your own lines, and that number decides.

**RO is not automatically mandatory.** The old note called RO "non-negotiable for fluoride and nitrate both". That holds only if your kitchen line exceeds the IS 10500 limits above. Expect it on the bore line; the municipal line may pass.

**Rejected.**
- Magnetic or electronic "descalers". Lawrence Livermore report UCRL-ID-125551 (1996): "No beneficial effect was found when using the magnetic device."
- Shower-head "softener" filters (₹1,000–2,000). KDF and vitamin-C cartridges reduce chlorine. They don't remove calcium or magnesium. Consider one only if a chlorine test comes back high; the bore line isn't chlorinated.
- Whole-home RO. Softened water adds sodium, so keep drinking on the kitchen purifier.

## A6. Robot vacuum and cleaning — detail

*Timeline: dock niche into the Homz drawings before design freeze; buy at move-in.*

*Timeline: dock niche into the Homz drawings before design freeze; buy at move-in.* (The L50 Ultra CE is the pick.)

A 2,045 sq ft carpet-area flat on one level, mostly vitrified tile, with a six-year-old: a self-emptying robot that also mops earns its place. Service in Hyderabad and genuine consumables decide the brand more than suction numbers do.

### A6.1 Options

Prices from Amazon.in and in.dreametech.com, 2026-10-01, GST-inclusive.

| Model | ₹ | What you get | Watch-out | Verdict |

| [Dreame L50 Ultra CE](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0GX5HFRHZ) | 64,999 | 25,000 Pa, auto-empty, 80°C mop wash, hot-air mop drying, auto refill of the robot from the dock tank, 10.5 mm mop lift, extending side brush and mop. Dock 457 × 340 × 590 mm, 8.9 kg | Dock heater and pump are the usual failures. Obstacle sensor is described only as "smart 3D" | **Pick.** Heated drying matters in the monsoon |
| [Dreame D30 Ultra](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0H2YVXLBR) | 52,999 | 25,000 Pa, auto-empty, mop washing, mop air-drying, 4.5 L clean / 4.0 L dirty tanks, same dock size | Dreame's page doesn't confirm hot-water washing or heated drying. Navigation is "dynamic laser" with no camera named | **Value pick.** Saves ₹12,000 |
| [Dreame L50s Pro Ultra](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0GX58N77R) | 79,999 | 30,000 Pa, 100°C mop wash | ₹15,000 more for numbers you won't feel | Rejected |
| [Dreame X60 Ultra Complete](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0H6PC12W4) | 1,29,999 | 7.95 cm slim body, 35,000 Pa | Twice the price | Rejected |
| [ECOVACS Deebot N50 Pro Omni](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0GKFDM4JZ) / [T30e Omni](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0GYR9QHXV) | 59,995 / 69,999 | Comparable docks | Service network not checked this round | Credible second choice |
| [Roborock Qrevo S Pro](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0GLFYWBD5) | 99,999 | Strong hardware | India first-party service not confirmed; costs ₹35,000 more | Rejected |

**Service.** Dreame India's home page claims a "service network across 165+ cities" with dedicated service centres. It does not explicitly promise onsite visits. The old note said "verified onsite service"; that was more than the page says. Support: 1800 309 3942, Support.in@dreame.tech, WhatsApp +91 90631 43560. An extended warranty costs ₹7,990 (L50 Ultra CE) or ₹6,990 (D30 Ultra). Ask about onsite cover before paying for it.

### A6.2 Setup in this flat

- **Dock niche.** Dreame lists the L50 Ultra CE dock as 457 × 340 × 590 mm with no axis labels. It lists the robot in length × width × height order, so read the dock as 457 deep × 340 wide × 590 high. The ramp adds depth, and a docked robot sits partly on it. You service the dock from the top: lift out the clean and dirty tanks every 2–3 days and open the lid to change the dust bag. So ask Homz for an open-topped niche, or one ≥100 cm high, ≥55 cm wide and ≥55 cm deep, with ventilation, no back panel that eats depth, and ~1 m clear floor in front. A 65 cm-high niche would force you to pull the dock out at every refill. **Unverified:** Dreame's side and front clearance rules for docking. Its India product page gives none. Check the printed L50 Ultra CE manual, or ask Dreame support, before Homz draws the niche. Robot makers often ask for ~0.5 m free on each side of an open-floor dock (typical, not checked for Dreame), so a tight niche may hurt docking. Place it in a dry hall or living-room cabinet beside an existing socket. No new wiring. Don't use the utility: it's a wet zone behind the kitchen, and the robot can't cross the UPVC sliding-door tracks to reach the rest of the flat.
- **Plinths and furniture clearance.** The robot body is 350 × 350 × 97 mm. Tell Homz, before design freeze: every bed, sofa and TV-unit plinth is either sealed to the floor or has ≥11 cm clear underneath. A gap of 9–11 cm can wedge it. A gap under 9 cm leaves a dust strip it can't reach. Free at design time; carpentry to fix later. Check loose furniture from the §7.1 list against the same rule.
- **Water for the dock.** Fill the clean tank from the municipal line or the RO, not the bore line. Hard bore water scales the dock's 80°C heater. Skip the plumbed water-hookup kit; it needs plumbing.
- **Floors.** Set the laminate master bedroom to vacuum-only. Over-wetting swells laminate. Use the lowest water flow on vitrified tile elsewhere.
- **No-go zones.** Set all four bathrooms, the utility and the balconies as no-go zones. Bathrooms are wet and may step down at the door, and the mops shouldn't spread bathroom water onto the bedroom floors. Set BR-03 (the Gurudwara) as a no-go zone too if the family prefers to clean it by hand. The old puja room is now the walk-in closet; let the robot clean it.
- **Kabir's toys.** Run it when the floor is picked up, for example during school hours. No obstacle sensor reliably avoids small Lego.
- **Thresholds.** Dreame's L50 Ultra CE page caps a single step at 2.2 cm (the D30 Ultra page says 20 mm). Its 4 cm figure is for a two-step threshold with >3 cm between steps. Tell Homz to keep any new threshold or saddle strip under 2 cm between rooms the robot should cross. Balcony UPVC sliding-door tracks are usually higher; that's fine, because the balconies are no-go zones.

### A6.3 Running cost and spares

Budget ~₹4–6k a year for dust bags, brushes, mop pads and filters (estimate). Third-party kits for the L50 Ultra CE sell for ~₹2,199 on Amazon.in. Buy genuine parts from [Dreame's accessories store](https://in.dreametech.com/collections/robot-vacuum-accessories) when the warranty depends on it. Buy one spare kit up front.

### A6.4 What not to buy

- **A stick vac.** You own a Dyson V15 Detect, a tier above the V12, with three spare HEPA filters ([[assets]]).
- **A floor washer** (Tineco class). Redundant with a robot mop on tile.

## A7. Interiors: Homz Interio, design scheme and rooms — detail

### Homz Interio: scope, contract and controls

The owner has picked Homz Interio as the turnkey firm, at ₹18 lakh including GST. This section doesn't reopen that choice. It turns the firm's known risks into contract asks and site checks. Everything below was re-read from Homz's own website on 2026-10-01 unless marked otherwise. The material spec lives in one place only, the §12 spec sheet ([A12](#a12-joinery-storage-and-materials-spec--detail)).

#### What the published terms say

Re-read at homzinterio.in/terms-conditions on 2026-10-01. Each clause is followed by what it means for you and the rider ask that addresses it.

| Clause (verbatim or close) | What it means for you | Rider ask |
|---|---|---|
| "All payments made by the Customer at any stage are non-refundable on termination for any reason" | You can't exit without losing everything you have paid | 7, plus pay in small gates |
| "Homzinterio shall be deemed the author and owner of the Designs", which can't be used "for future additions or alterations… without the prior written" consent | You can't hand the drawings to another carpenter later | 1 |
| Warranty: "In case only of a manufacturing defect… a replacement will be provided subject to… the Warranty Letter", "expressly in lieu of all other warranties" | The real cover sits in a Warranty Letter you haven't seen. The terms give no duration, though the FAQ advertises up to 10 years including workmanship | 6 |
| Liability "limited to the total fees received". Excludes "acts and omissions on the part of auxiliary persons… or the suppliers / vendors" | This contradicts the "no subcontractors" claim. Homz disclaims the people who will actually be in your flat | 2, 9 |
| Delay compensation is void for "discontinuity in access to site", "Restrictions in working at site – timings, manpower restrictions", "any customer vendor working at the site", and "Delays caused due to customer approvals pending" | Every RWA restricts working hours. Your geyser installer will be on site. Approvals have no deadline on either side. As written, you'd almost never qualify | 5 |
| Delay compensation is also void if the "Budget of property [is] greater than Rs 30 Lakhs" | At ₹18L the interiors budget is under ₹30L. But "property" could mean the flat's value, which is far above ₹30L | 5: define it as the contract value, or strike it |
| "In case of an increase in the estimated project value, additional payment will have to be made immediately" | Price rises are built in | 3, 8 |
| "All vendor products will have to be paid for, in full, along with the Production Initiation Fee" | Bought-in items (sink, lights) get paid upfront | 4: list them, and pay them against delivery |
| If the customer doesn't take handover of products "within a period of 30 days" of the ready notice, or hasn't paid the "Execution Initiation Fee… and any Load Holding Charges", Homz isn't "liable for any damage… to the products lying at the factory or warehouse" | A lift-booking or RWA hold-up moves storage cost and damage risk to you. The charge isn't defined or priced | 12 |
| "Final Site Handover… will only happen upon receipt of all outstanding payments" | No retention is possible as written | 4 |
| "The courts at Bangalore shall have exclusive jurisdiction" and claims are "barred" after "3 (Three) months" | See the note below | 10 |
| Additional terms "become part of these T&C". No order-of-precedence clause | Without a precedence clause, the standard terms may still govern a signed rider | 10 |
| Prices "inclusive of taxes… subject to change as per prevalent government tax rates" | Any GST change passes to you. Neutral | — |

The terms say nothing about protecting the finished flat or repairing damage to it. Rider ask 11 fills that gap.

**Two of the old disqualifiers are weaker than the old note said** (my reading of the statutes, not legal advice). A consumer can file before the District Consumer Commission where they live or work, under §34(2)(d) of the Consumer Protection Act 2019. Its pecuniary limit is ₹50 lakh under the 2021 jurisdiction rules (the 2019 Act said ₹1 crore), which covers ₹18L. The Act gives two years to file (§69). §28 of the Indian Contract Act voids clauses that cut short the time to enforce a right. So the 3-month bar and Bangalore exclusivity probably don't stop a Hyderabad consumer complaint. *(Statute sections from knowledge; the official texts weren't reachable, so these are **unverified**.)*

The forum depends on what you ask for. A deficiency-of-service claim (delay, poor work, refund) goes to the District Commission. A request to strike an unfair term, such as "non-refundable for any reason", goes only to the State or National Commission (PRS summary of the 2019 Act). The Telangana State Commission also sits in Hyderabad.

The leverage is still poor. One comparable case (Interior Company, C.C. 106/2025, DCDRC-I Hyderabad, decided 3 Sep 2026; carried from the old audit and **unverified**) reports an owner who paid ₹12.45L and recovered ₹4.25L after about 16 months. **Prevention through the rider beats litigation.**

#### Is ₹18 lakh sane for this scope?

**Yes, for a laminate-tier modular package with a finished false ceiling. It's thin if the quote also claims beds, curtains, wallpaper, branded board and Blum hardware throughout.**

| Basis | Figure |
|---|---|
| Quote incl. GST | ₹18,00,000 |
| Ex-GST at 18% | ₹15,25,424 (GST ₹2,74,576) |
| Per sqft of carpet (2,045), incl. GST | **₹880** |
| Per sqft of carpet, ex-GST | ₹746 |
| Per sqft of SBUA (2,880), incl. GST | ₹625. Don't use this basis: a fit-out finishes carpet area |

| Benchmark | Says | Read |
|---|---|---|
| Homz's own Hyderabad guide | 3 BHK ₹10–18L | ₹18L sits at the top of its 3 BHK band, for a 3.5 BHK |
| Homz Bangalore cost blog | 3 BHK "Standard" ₹16–27L for kitchen, all wardrobes and core TV units only | ₹18L is near the bottom of Standard, so expect laminate and MR ply unless named otherwise |
| Arune Interiors, Hyderabad | "Basic ₹1,200–1,800 per sq.ft." (basis unstated, scope likely includes paint and civil) | ₹880 incl. GST is below Arune's Basic. That fits a quote with no full repaint, MEP or waterproofing |

₹18L incl. GST lands near the middle of that range. **The quote is credible for this list at laminate tier.** If it also lists beds, curtains, wallpaper and loose furniture, something has been thinned. Ask which line. Branded board and Hettich or Blum hardware per the §12 sheet may come back as upgrades: ~₹1–2L is a guess (**unverified**). One Gachibowli reviewer saw soft-close hinges quoted at ~₹42,000 against ~₹15,000 elsewhere. Trustpilot shows quotes of ~₹11L rising to ₹19–20L after the deposit. A low headline price is the risk to watch.

#### Electrical touches the flat still needs

The flat needs no rewiring. These small jobs still happen, all on existing circuits.

| Touch | Why | Who does it | Put in the BOQ |
|---|---|---|---|
| False-ceiling lights (cove strip, downlights) | Each new fixture needs cable from an existing ceiling point | Homz's electrician | Point count, ₹ per point, FRLS cable brand (Havells or Polycab), tested with the RCCB in circuit |
| Wardrobe and under-cabinet profile lights | A 230 V supply to an LED driver inside the unit | Homz's electrician | Driver brand, sensor or switch, and where the driver sits (accessible) |
| Kitchen and utility appliance points | Map every builder 16 A point at handover. Use existing points where they reach. A missing point gets added, by surface conduit inside the carcass or a new circuit through the false ceiling; it is not a reason to drop the appliance. No chasing of finished walls (§4) | Homz's electrician, for the extension only. Size the cut-outs to the chosen models | A point list against §4's appliance list, each marked "existing" |
| TV wall | Power and HDMI behind the TV panel | Homz carpenter and electrician | A 50 mm duct from TV to console, plywood backing for the mount (§3) |
| Geyser hookups ×4 | The provision exists. Units don't | The brand's installer (plumber and electrician visit) | Not in Homz scope |
| Ethernet or speaker cable in the ceiling | Optional. Keep it only if §2 puts access points or §3 puts speakers in the ceiling | A network installer. Or Homz's electrician pulls cable you supply | A 3-day "ceiling-close" notice from Homz, so cable goes in before boarding |

Every light point needs a ceiling paint touch-up (scope matrix).

#### Material annexure: use the §12 spec sheet

**There is one material schedule: the §12 spec sheet ([A12](#a12-joinery-storage-and-materials-spec--detail)).** Attach it as the BOQ's material annexure. Don't write a second one here. The agreed answers, for the record:

| Line | §12 spec (verbatim intent) |
|---|---|
| Carcass, whole flat | 19 mm Century Sainik 710, IS 303 BWR grade (stamped BWR or BWP), product named. One board everywhere: no MR grade, no 18 mm, no wet-wall exception. Upgrade: Club Prime, ~40% more board cost, with Century's termite cover in writing |
| Backs | 9 mm, same grade and emission class as the carcass |
| Shutters | 18–19 mm BWP ply or HDHMR, laminated both faces (matches §12) |
| Emission | E0 or E1 (EN 717-1), or CARB Phase 2 / TSCA VI, with the mill's test report naming the product. Sainik 710 states no class, so get the report or take Club Prime |
| Termite | Sainik 710 states borer and termite proof. Club Prime makes no termite claim on its page, so get the cover in writing. No soil treatment on floor 42 |
| Hardware | Brand and catalogue article number on every line |
| Cure after install | About 2 weeks, ventilated, before anyone sleeps in the flat |

Ask Homz to price the sheet line by line. Their materials page names no brand, so any line may be an upgrade.

#### The contract rider: twelve asks to paste

Paste these into the agreement as numbered clauses. Get Homz's written yes or no on each before the next payment. Asks 1 to 4, 6 and 10 are the minimum.

1. **Drawings.** "Homz grants the Customer a perpetual, royalty-free licence to use all drawings for maintenance, repair and future alterations of Flat B4206. Homz hands over the final 2D working drawings, electrical point layout and false-ceiling layout as PDF at handover."
2. **Named materials, no substitution.** "Every BOQ line names brand, product, grade and thickness, per the attached material annexure. No substitute without the Customer's prior written approval of a sample. An unapproved substitute will be replaced at Homz's cost."
3. **Binding renders and BOQ.** "The signed 3D renders and the itemised BOQ are binding contract documents. The renders govern finish, colour, layout and visible features. The signed 2D drawings govern dimensions. Any conflict is resolved in the Customer's favour."
4. **Payment gates.** "Payments follow the attached schedule. Each payment falls due only after the stated inspection passes. The final 5% falls due when the snag list is closed, and site handover is not withheld for it. Vendor products are paid against delivery to site."
5. **Delay penalty and approvals.** "Handover date: [date]. Homz has seen the RWA fit-out rules and its schedule allows for them, so they're no ground for delay. Other vendors working on dates agreed in writing are no ground either. Labour availability, festival absences, raw-material or factory delays, and power and water at site (the building's DG supplies power) are Homz's risk and no ground for delay. Only these excuse delay: Customer-signed scope changes, for the days agreed in the change order, and Customer approvals beyond 3 working days. The Customer answers each complete approval request within 3 working days. For each day late, ₹1,000 comes off the final payment, capped at 5% of the contract value. The exclusions for 'unforeseen issues… on raw material, manpower or others', 'Power, Water supply issues at site', 'Customer staying in property' and 'Budget of property greater than Rs 30 Lakhs' do not apply."
6. **Warranty in writing.** "The Warranty Letter is attached and states years per component: carcass, shutters, hardware, false ceiling, electrical and installation. Workmanship is covered for the years Homz advertises, up to 10, stated per component. Manufacturer warranties are registered in the Customer's name. The warranty starts at handover, whatever the payment status of the final instalment."
7. **Exit.** "If Homz misses the handover date by 60 days or more, the Customer may terminate. Homz refunds payments for work not yet produced and delivers all produced items."
8. **Price lock and changes.** "The contract value is the Final Price for the signed scope. Changes need a written change order priced at the BOQ's unit rates before the work starts. Discounts are fixed in rupees per line and don't lapse if scope changes."
9. **Labour, insurance, statutory duties.** "Homz is responsible for every worker on site, including subcontractors. Homz holds contractors' all-risk and employees' compensation insurance, and shows certificates before work starts. Homz alone bears any labour-law registration, cess and wage liability."
10. **Precedence, RWA and disputes.** "This rider prevails over Homz's terms and conditions and any estimate terms where they conflict. Homz follows the RWA fit-out charter and pays any fine its crew causes. Disputes may be heard in Hyderabad."
11. **Protect the finished flat.** "Homz protects all finished surfaces and fittings (floors, sanitaryware, CP fittings, uPVC frames, grilles, VRF grilles) with sheets and covers. Homz scans walls and the slab soffit with a pipe and cable detector before drilling. Ceiling fixings use depth-limited anchors only. Nothing goes through the slab. Homz makes good any damage at its cost, including paint touch-ups matched to the builder's shade."
12. **Storage and delivery risk.** "No load-holding or storage charge applies while delivery waits on a Homz-side or RWA-scheduled lift slot. Risk in all goods stays with Homz until they are installed in B4206."

**One named site engineer** sits under ask 2 or 4. Add: "Named PM and site engineer. Any change needs 7 days' notice and a written handover. Weekly dated photo report every Saturday."

#### Payment gates tied to inspections

Homz's terms name a Production Initiation Fee and an Execution Initiation Fee, with full payment before handover. Their percentages aren't published on the site. Propose this schedule:

| Gate | Cumulative % | Pays when this passes |
|---|---|---|
| Booking | 10% (or what's already paid) | — |
| Design freeze | 40% | You sign the renders, 2D drawings, itemised BOQ and the material annexure. The rider is signed |
| Material verified | 70% | (a) Before lamination, Homz sends dated photos or video of the B4206 sheets showing mill stamp, grade and ISI CM/L number, plus the mill or dealer invoice naming the order. (b) At site you inspect an exposed cut edge or hinge-bore side and keep one offcut per board type. (c) Hardware boxes match the BOQ article numbers. (d) Optional: one factory visit you book in advance |
| Installation complete | 95% | Every unit fitted and every shutter aligned. Drawers run full-extension under load. Lights work and the RCCB doesn't trip. Ceiling finished and touched up |
| Snag closure | 100% | The snag list is closed within 30 days of handover. Deep cleaning is done. No damage against the Phase 0 photo baseline |

Factory panels arrive laminated and edge-banded, so mill stamps and Century QR labels are usually covered or trimmed off by then. That's why check (a) happens before lamination. The factory is said to be in Bengaluru (**unverified**), so a surprise visit is a trip. Check (a) is the main substitution control: don't waive it.

Homz's terms say fees are "as per the quotation". Read the payment terms in your actual quotation (one Gachibowli reviewer had four stages) and map them onto these gates before signing.

#### Site rules: lift, RWA, protection, dust, insurance

- **Baseline before Homz enters.** Close the builder's snag list in writing. Photograph every room, wall, floor and fitting, dated. Get the builder's as-built MEP drawings (plumbing, electrical, conduit runs). Without these, the builder can blame Homz for defects, and your RERA defect claim weakens.
- **Drilling.** Wardrobes and wall units on bathroom- or kitchen-adjacent walls go near concealed CPVC pipe and conduit. Homz scans before every hole (rider ask 11). A punctured pipe means re-plumbing and re-tiling.
- **Lift.** Record the service-lift car width, depth and height, the door's clear opening, and the flat's entry opening. Give them to Homz in writing. Make "delivered and installed inside B4206, floor 42" Homz's obligation. Flat-packed panels fit easily. A full-length granite slab may not, so agree joint positions first.
- **RWA charter.** Get it in writing before Homz starts. Cover working hours, drilling window, deposit, debris route, lift booking and labour passes. Give Homz a copy (rider ask 5).
- **Slab.** False-ceiling hangers go into the slab about every 1.2 m, and RCC slabs carry embedded conduit. Ask the builder for the slab type. Scan the soffit before drilling, use depth-limited anchors, and put nothing through the slab. If it's post-tensioned, a hit on a tendon is a structural problem, so the scan matters more (rider ask 11).
- **VRF units.** Record the indoor-unit type and position in each room, any builder bulkhead or ceiling, and the drain-pump locations. Get the VRF installer's written OK on ceiling work near their units, so the warranty survives (A20).
- **VRF and dust.** Switch the VRF off and seal every indoor-unit grille during gypsum, cutting and sanding work. Gypsum dust clogs coils, filters and drain pumps on a system whose warranty depends on service (A20). Once the dust work ends, have the filters cleaned. Dry mode is fine after that.
- **Purifiers.** Don't run them on site during dusty work: gypsum clogs a HEPA set in days. Install them at the cure phase. Dust containment and a post-works clean are Homz's job (scope matrix).
- **Moisture.** Factory panels arrive pressed and laminated, so the old "carpentry only Nov–Feb" rule relaxes. Don't let panels sit unwrapped on site for more than a week. See A27.5.
- **Insurance and labour.** At ₹18L for your own home, the construction cess likely doesn't apply. The labour codes (in force since 21 Nov 2025) appear to exclude own-residence work costing under ₹50 lakh. PRS's summary words this test confusingly, so it's **unverified**. Rider ask 9 puts any labour liability on Homz anyway.
- **Cure.** After installation, ventilate the flat for about 2 weeks before anyone sleeps there and before the Saroop enters (A27.5).

#### What the reviews show

| Source | Score | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| Trustpilot (17 reviews, read 2026-10-01; profile unclaimed) | 2.6/5, 64% one-star | Quotes rising after deposit (₹11L → ₹19–20L, one "3x"). 45-day promises becoming 4–8 months. Site engineers changed "5 to 6 times". Delivered items not matching renders (a 4 sqft mirror for 9 sqft, a 410 mm cabinet for 450 mm). Refunds refused |
| Gachibowli review, posted 15 Aug 2026 (experience 18 Jul) | 1★ | Quote ~₹11L in four payment stages → ~₹19L; items removed, ₹13.4L paid. Soft-close hinges quoted at ~₹42,000 against ~₹15,000 elsewhere. "Supervisor was changed multiple times". Over 8 months to a July 2026 handover, skirting still pending |
| Trustpilot, Aug 2026 | 4★ | Reviewer wasn't told that "contract value must match booking value" to keep all discounts (rider ask 8) |

The failures cluster in four areas: price growth after the deposit, slipping dates, supervisor churn, and render mismatch. Rider asks 3, 5, 8 and the named-PM clause target exactly those. Treat a slip as the base case when you plan housing (A27.5).

#### History: routes no longer in play

Until 2026-09-28 this note recommended a designer plus a vetted contractor, and rejected Homz at 2.45/10. The owner chose Homz; that analysis is retired to the vault's git history.

### Design scheme

**Brief:** modern Indian minimalist for a Sikh family of three: the owner (works from home), spouse and Kabir (6). 2,045 sqft carpet on floor 42. Homz Interio builds every fixed piece. The family buys the loose pieces. The flat came finished, so the scheme works only with joinery, furniture, light, textiles and art. It needs no paint, plaster or tile. The only electrical work is Homz's electrician feeding 4 art spots and the dining pendant from existing circuits.

<img src="../../../assets/personal/olympus/flat-plan.svg" width="560">

#### Room programme

| Room | Size | Use | Window faces (from the plan) | Zone |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Drawing + foyer | 13'0" × 17'8" | Formal hosting, shoes off at the door | N, one ~5'9" window | Public |
| Living | 19'5" × 11'9" | Family room, TV | None (interior room) | Public |
| Dining | 13'10" × 11'9" | 6 daily, 8 for guests | E, via the slider to the sit-out | Public |
| Kitchen | 13'10" × 13'0" | Builder U, kept | S, over the sink | Service |
| Sit-out | 4'11" × ~8'5" | Main balcony, off the Dining | E, open rail | Public |
| Master | 13'0" × 13'0" + L nook | Couple + Kabir, dastar mirror | S, slider to balcony | Private |
| Master balcony | 13'0" × 4'11" | Two folding chairs | S, open rail | Private |
| BR-01 | 13'0" × 13'0" | Guest room + owner's WFH study | S | Private |
| BR-02 | 13'0" × 11'11" | Kabir's study + play | N | Private |
| BR-03 | 13'0" × 11'11" | Home Gurudwara, worship only | E | Sacred |
| Walk-in closet (ex-Puja) | 4'0" × 6'1" | Open hanging, drawers | None | Private |
| Store (ex-Linen) | 5'0" × 3'10" | General store | None | Service |
| Utility + service balcony | 4'11" wide | Washer, airer, brooms | E | Service |
| 4 WCs | Master 9'0" × 6'0"; 3 × 5'0" × 7'11" | Ensuites to Master, BR-01, BR-02; one common WC off the Dining lobby | Small N windows on two. The brochure also draws a west bump-out with glazing lines on the Master WC: window or vent, confirm on site | Service |

The plan gives BR-03 no attached WC. That suits a room kept only for the Saroop.

#### Sun and orientation, corrected

The owner confirmed that the sit-out and utility face east and the Master balcony faces south (2026-09-20). Read with that, the brochure plan puts north at the top. So three rooms face north, not east or south: the Drawing, BR-02 and two WCs. The Living has no window at all. Note for [[my-home]]: its line "habitable rooms face east and south" leaves out the north-facing Drawing and BR-02 windows.

| Exposure | Rooms | What it means |
|---|---|---|
| North, no direct sun | Drawing, BR-02 | Best for art, screens and study. Sheer + lined drape for privacy and night only. No solar film or blinds needed |
| East, low 7–10 AM sun | Dining slider and sit-out, BR-03, utility | The harsh load. Sheer + blackout-lined drape on the Dining slider. BR-03 gets fine sheer only (§7.2) |
| South, high midday sun | Kitchen sink window, BR-01, Master slider | Mild. Sheer plus blackout where people sleep |
| West | Lift-lobby core, no habitable glazing. Possible small Master WC window or vent (confirm) | The worst sun in Hyderabad never reaches a habitable room |

Confirm each window face on site before Homz orders any curtain track. The plan is a brochure drawing, not a survey.

#### Design language

- **One wood tone.** Light oak or ash colour on every piece. Teak (Freedom Tree sofa) sits close enough. Get the species in writing for anything sold as "oak": most Indian "oak" is a stain over sheesham or mango.
- **Two metals.** Matt black (the AGUNNARYD pendant, door hardware) plus satin brass. The pendant carries both: black outside, brass inside. No chrome, no gold plate.
- **One motif.** Vertical fluting, used on Homz shutters for the shoe cabinet and media unit. Nowhere else.
- **One hero per room.** The art wall in the Drawing, the solid-ash table in the Dining, the Palki Sahib in BR-03. Leave 30–40% of every wall empty.
- **Matte over gloss.** Matt anti-fingerprint laminate on shutters (§12). Gloss acrylic shows every handprint at a 6-year-old's height.
- **Warm light.** 2700–3000 K, CRI ≥90 for anything that lights art or a face (§10).
- **Craft once.** One Indian craft piece per room reads as considered. Carving, mirror-work or matching "ethnic" sets on every surface reads as kitsch. Pochampally ikat (Telangana) suits cushions or a runner.

#### Palette

Rule: 60% warm white, 30% wood and putty, 10% accent. Texture does the work: linen, boucle, jute, cane.

| Role | Colour | Hex | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| Base | Warm white | `#F2ECE3` | Existing walls (builder paint stays), shutters |
| Secondary | Putty / greige | `#D8CEC0` | Sofas, drapes |
| Wood | Light oak | `#C79A6B` | Furniture, media unit, shoe cabinet |
| Grounding | Soft charcoal | `#3A3733` | Frames, pendant, hardware |
| Accent | Terracotta + sage | `#B5651D` / `#9CA88E` | Cushions, ceramics, the Living rug (STOENSE pale green) |

The builder's wall paint is probably an off-white. Hold a `#F2ECE3` swatch against it on site. If it reads cold, warm the room with textiles, not paint.

#### Display strategy

- **Two anchor walls and one small piece.** The Drawing's solid south wall, above the sofa and facing the front door. The Dining's north wall, above the crockery unit. One small artwork above the foyer bench. The Drawing and bench walls never take direct sun. The Dining's north wall does: from October to March, low 7–9 AM sun enters the slider ~25° south of east and lands on the wall's east end (hand geometry, unverified). Hang a print or a ceramic or brass piece there, not an original oil or a textile. Keep the sheer drawn 7–10 AM, which also protects the Fusi table.
- **Light them from the Homz ceiling.** Four spots in all, adjustable if the sample tilts: 2 over the sofa, 1 at the bench, 1 over the crockery unit. Each is 5 W, 3000 K, CRI ≥90, 60° beam, set ~0.5–0.7 m out from the wall (§10). The pick is the White Teak Pandora 5 W J-spot (DL01-10437, ₹2,040). The round-1 Pandora 15 W, 36° spot is rejected: §10 estimates it puts 3–6× the oils limit on the art.
- **What the spots need.** A Homz false-ceiling patch over the sofa wall, the bench and the crockery wall, a separate switch leg per room and a trailing-edge dimmable driver behind an access panel. Homz's electrician feeds them from the room's lighting circuit. A Sonoff MINI-DIM inside the existing switch box dims them and runs scenes from Alexa (§1), so no spare plate slot or bigger box is needed. Confirm the ceiling, driver and switching lines in the BOQ. No false ceiling there: use surface-mount spots on the existing point. Wired picture lights need a new wall point per frame, so skip them.
- **No foyer console.** The west wall holds the shoe cabinet and bench, so §10's foyer table lamp has no surface. The foyer gets one spot over the bench art instead.
- **Security hangers** on every frame above the sofa and bench. Kabir will stand on both.
- **Rotating art** (Optional): a hanging rail fixed at the top of the wall lets frames move without new holes. It's a Homz carpentry item. Product and price are unverified.
- **No art opposite the Dining slider.** East sun fades pigment. The piece above the crockery unit is a print for the same reason.

#### What the old scheme had that is now cut

| Old idea | Why it's cut |
|---|---|
| Lime-plaster feature walls, textured backdrops | Plaster and paint work. The flat needs none |
| Honed Kota or Jaisalmer stone accents | Floors are finished vitrified tile. No re-tiling |
| A mirror to brighten a "windowless foyer" | The foyer is the NW corner of the Drawing, which has a north window |
| Art on the "north gallery" wall of the Drawing | The north wall carries the window. The art moves to the solid south wall |
| Sculptural piece "against the east glazing" of the Drawing | The Drawing has no east glazing |
| Media unit "away from the east/south glare" | The Living has no window, so glare isn't a factor |
| Quartz counter | The builder's platform is kept (§9). New stone goes in only if there's no platform or it's damaged. Then §9 picks dark granite, because Quantra's FAQ warns against direct sun and the sink sits under the south window |
| Jaali screen, carved accents | Second and third motifs. Fluting is the only one |
| Fluted-glass kitchen–dining sliders | ~170–230 kg of glass across 4.2 m needs slab anchors and a floor guide. Both break the no-civil-work rule and §12's "never drill the slab" |
| BR-03 "+ sit-out" | The sit-out opens off the Dining. BR-03's only window faces east |

### 7.1 Loose furniture — vetted shortlist

> **Editor, 2026-10-02:** rebuilt at the doubled budget. The Drawing room is Magari (Porto sofa, Rhodes chairs), the Dining is Alankaram solid teak, the Master bed is Freedom Tree teak. IKEA keeps the Living sofa, the sofa-beds and a few lamps on the merits. Josmo Biscoff (81 cm) stays rejected; the chair limit is now ≤75 cm ([7.3](#73-room-designs--public-rooms)).

Homz builds everything architectural: wardrobes, TV and display units, the foyer console, the kitchen, and (per §7.4) the Master bed. Buy loose only what needs engineered comfort or will move house with you: sofas, chairs, tables, rugs and lamps. Homz's service pages list "loose furniture" too, so ask which pieces sit inside the ₹18L before buying any of this. If Homz quotes a piece, compare it against the line below on species, fabric and foam.

The room briefs (§7.3 public rooms, §7.4 private rooms) place each piece. This list matches them. The plain list with footprints for the room plans is `brief/furnish-picks.md`.

#### What decides this section

1. **"Oak" is usually a finish.** Oak isn't grown in India. Most Indian "oak" furniture is a stain over sheesham, acacia or mango, and IKEA's "oak" is veneer or paint. Alankaram and Josmo name European ash or oak. Even then, Alankaram's Fusi page lists the material as "Ash/Oak/Beech, Veneer" while the text says "master grade solid wood". **Get the species and "solid through" written on the invoice.** Solid teak or sheesham often outlasts oak, so a teak frame (Freedom Tree) is a fine choice.
2. **Quality first, at a doubled budget; IKEA only on the merits.** Every room gets the better-made piece where one exists within ~2× the old spend. The Living takes daily wear from a 6-year-old, so washable covers and a 10-year guarantee still win there. Hyderabad makers are checked first, and their weak spots (1-year warranties, no cancellation, upholstery excluded) are written into the order.
3. **Floor-42 delivery is your risk.** Freedom Tree says items that won't pass the entry or service lift are the buyer's responsibility. Josmo charges a handling fee above floor 2. Measure the lift before ordering anything over 2 m.

**Balcony wind and climbing.** At floor 42 a monsoon squall can slide a light bench and lift cushions. Folding pieces that live indoors remove that risk and leave nothing at the rail for Kabir to climb. A freestanding table also means no drilling. A wall-mounted drop-leaf table (IKEA ASKHOLMEN wall table) needs holes in the balcony wall, which is part of the façade: an unsealed fixing can let water in, and RWAs often ban fixings there. No umbrellas.

#### Kabir in the Master

Kabir (6) sleeps with his parents until he moves to BR-02. A 72-inch (183 cm) king gives three sleepers about 61 cm each. That works for a small child but gets tight as he grows. §7.4 keeps the plain king and rejects a side-car cot or trundle, because BR-02 is ready for him. This section agrees and adds one cheap fallback: a 3×6 ft cotton floor gadda on his side at night, rolled into the closet by day. YUMESA 72×36×3 in is ₹995 on Amazon.in. Nothing in the Master changes when he moves out. A wider custom bed (84 in) would need a custom mattress and leave ~90 cm each side in a 13 ft room, so it's rejected. Mattress brands are in §11.

#### Brands checked, Oct 2026
