## A8. Safety, security and child-safety — detail

This appendix backs the §8 table. Prices are Amazon.in, Modo India or brand-store prices checked on 2026-10-01, GST included, unless marked "ex-GST". A "~" marks an estimate that wasn't confirmed.

### A8.1 Risk ranking

Ranked by severity × likelihood for this flat: floor 42, a 6-year-old, PNG cooking, VRF cooling, finished bathrooms.

| Rank | Risk | Why it ranks here | Control | Tier |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Child falls from a balcony, sit-out, service balcony or utility | Fatal at 126 m. A 6-year-old can climb a 1.0–1.2 m rail with one foothold, such as a chair or planter | Full-height vertical invisible grille + high slider lock + nothing climbable within 0.5 m of the rail | Must |
| 2 | Fire at night | Low likelihood, catastrophic outcome, slow stair escape | Keep the building's sprinklers and detectors working through the Homz ceiling. Add 6 linked in-flat smoke alarms | Must |
| 3 | PNG leak or kitchen fire | Gas is always live. A pan fire is the most common home fire | Kitchen heat alarm, methane detector mounted high, fire blanket, 2 kg ABC extinguisher, hob with flame-failure (§4) | Must |
| 4 | Furniture or TV tips onto the child | Common at this age. A child uses drawers as a ladder | Steel anti-tip straps; Homz wall-fixes all built-ins | Must |
| 5 | Fall from a window | Severe, but builder MS grills already cover the windows | Test every grill with a 100 mm ball; add a restrictor only where it fails | Must (check), Optional (buy) |
| 6 | Scald or shock in a bathroom | Four new geysers go onto existing points in wet rooms. A 6-year-old can turn a mixer to full hot | Geysers at 55 °C, 50 °C in the master; optional 60 °C hour weekly; test the 30 mA RCCB | Must (₹0) |
| — | Water leak floods the flat below | Property and liability, not life safety | 3 leak sensors where there is no floor drain | Should |
| — | Break-in | The tower has gate security (assumed). On floor 42 the main door is the only way in | Smart mortise lock | Should |
| — | Carbon monoxide | No gas geyser. The hob vents through a ducted chimney | One EN 50291 alarm | Optional |

### A8.2 Balcony and window falls

**The gap rule.** A guard opening should stop a 100 mm sphere. US residential code (IRC R312) and UK Approved Document K both use roughly this size, set to keep a small child's head and body from passing (international practice, not re-checked on 2026-10-01). The old note cited NBC 2016 Part 6 Section 8 Annex H for the same 100 mm rule "with due allowance for deflection under load". NBC 2016 couldn't be opened to confirm that clause (unverified). NBC 2005 Part 3 clause 12.11 sets balcony parapet and handrail height at 1.0–1.2 m (verified in the 2005 text).

**Which gap is right for a 6-year-old.** 100 mm stops a 6-year-old passing through. The old ≤75 mm target adds margin but isn't required. The bigger risk is going over the top. A 1.0–1.2 m rail stops an adult leaning, not a child on a chair. So the fix is full height, plus a pitch that stays under 100 mm when pushed.

**Why vertical cables at full height.**
- Vertical cables give no foothold. Horizontal cables form a ladder, which is why the old note rejected them.
- A vertical run spans only rail top to soffit, about 1.8–2.0 m. A horizontal run across the ~4 m Master balcony spans twice that, so its gap opens further under a push.
- Hyderabad sellers on IndiaMART list vertical installation as the default.
- It keeps the view, which a rigid grille does not.

**Spec to give the vendor.**

| Item | Spec | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Wire | SS316 wire rope, 3 mm, nylon-coated, 2.5 mm minimum | The Hyderabad market standard. SS316 is normal for wire rope, so no premium for grade |
| Grade proof | Mill test certificate (MTC) for the wire lot, naming SS316 and matching the reel tag | The coating hides the core. SS202 passed off as SS316 corrodes, and a snapped wire at floor 42 is the failure mode |
| Magnet check | Hold a magnet to a stripped offcut. Strong pull: reject the lot | Indicative only. SS316 is non-magnetic; SS202 is often slightly magnetic, so a weak pull proves nothing |
| Breaking load | Vendor states the minimum breaking load per wire in writing | A written number makes the vendor liable for it. Don't accept "strong" |
| Direction and pitch | Vertical, 50 mm (2 inch) centres | No foothold. Margin under the 100 mm rule after deflection |
| Bottom track | On the parapet top or the rail top, or on side-wall brackets. Never in the balcony floor slab | Protects the builder's waterproofing |
| Top track | Into the soffit, tight to it. No gap at the top | A top gap is a climb-over route |
| Rail below | If the grille starts at rail height, the existing rail or glass below must also stop a 100 mm ball | The grille only protects above its bottom track |
| Fixings | SS anchors, every hole sealed with PU sealant | Stops water tracking in |
| Extent | Master balcony (south), sit-out (east), service balcony (east, off the dining area), utility (east) | Every opening Kabir can reach. If the utility or service balcony is fully glazed, check on site and drop it |
| Acceptance test | Push a 100 mm ball hard at mid-span between two cables. It must not pass. Pluck-test tension at 3 points | Proves the gap holds under load |
| Paper | Written warranty on wire, tension, anchor pull-out and anchor leaks. MTC copy. Photos of every anchor before handover | Homz may subcontract this; the same paper applies (A8.10 ask 6) |

If a vendor insists on horizontal cable somewhere, add one test: an adult hangs ~25 kg on one cable at mid-span, and the 100 mm ball still must not pass.

**Price.** This is the single source for the grille budget; other sections cite §8. On 2026-10-01 the IndiaMART Hyderabad page showed 90 per-sqft price tags from ₹50 to ₹750, median ~₹150. Most sit at ₹110–250 per sqft. The listings don't say whether fitting or GST is included, so treat them as ex-GST. The four openings run about 40 ft of outer face in total: Master ~13 ft, sit-out ~10 ft, service balcony ~4 ft, utility ~13 ft (estimates from the floor plan). Rail top to soffit is 1.8–2.0 m, about 6–6.5 ft, so ~250 sqft. Some vendors price the full floor-to-soffit face (~10 ft, ~400 sqft), so the high end assumes that. At ₹110–250 per sqft plus 18% GST, that gives **~₹35,000–1,20,000 incl. GST**. Measure on site and get three quotes. Reject anyone who won't do the ball test.

**Who does it.** An invisible-grille specialist, not Homz's carpenters. Ask Homz whether they subcontract one. If not, hire directly and schedule it after Homz finishes the balcony ceilings.

**One vendor for grille and pigeon net.** Many Hyderabad grille sellers also list pigeon or safety nets on IndiaMART (§21). Use one vendor and one anchor plan, so the net and grille share tracks and nobody drills twice.

**Association first.** Get the facility manager's written OK. Towers often control anything visible on the elevation. Invisible grilles usually pass because they barely show (unverified for this tower).

**Rejected:**
- *Horizontal cable.* It's a ladder, and it deflects more over a long span.
- *Rigid SS304 grille to rail height.* It still leaves the top edge. A rigid grille at full height blocks the view and costs more.
- *Baby safety net on the railing* (e.g. SNOWIE net, ₹769). It's nylon, UV-degrades in a year or two, and a 6-year-old climbs it.
- *Hinged release panel per elevation.* The old note wanted one so the family could signal from the balcony in a fire. At 126 m nobody rescues from outside. A grille with 50 mm gaps still passes air, sound and a waved cloth. Dropped.

**Balcony sliders.** The grille is the barrier; the slider lock buys time. SNOWIE SOFT keyed track locks (4 for ₹699, B0B2R179HZ) clamp into the slider track with a hex key and need no drilling. They fit tracks under 15 mm wide and over 16 mm tall (seller spec), so measure the UPVC track first. Fit them on the top track, out of reach. Count the sliders on site; two packs likely cover them.
- **Mesh sash.** §11 puts a mesh sash in each slider's spare rail. Fit the lock on a rail the mesh sash doesn't run in, or it jams the mesh.
- **Key in a fire.** The fire card (A19.3) sends the family to the Master balcony if smoke fills the flat. A key on an adult's key ring can't be found in smoke at night. On every keyed slider, hang one key on a hook at 1.9 m or higher beside it. The Master slider takes a keyless bolt instead (below). A 6-year-old reaches ~1.4 m overhead, and ~1.85 m on a dragged chair, so 1.6 m is too low. Check the shorter adult reaches 1.9 m. NBC 2005 Part 4 cl. 4.7.5 wants exits openable from inside without a key.
- **Master slider: keyless.** The Master balcony is the fire refuge, so fit a slide bolt or sash stop at 1.9 m or higher there instead of a keyed lock. Nobody hunts for a key in smoke. It needs two screws into the UPVC frame (generic hardware, ~₹200–500, unverified).

**Windows.** The windows came with builder MS safety grills. Push a 100 mm ball through each one on site. If every grill stops it, buy nothing. If one fails, add a restrictor to that sash (the SNOWIE lock works on sliding sashes). Also check for rust at the grill fixings.

**Wind at floor 42.** Design gusts here are near 54 m/s (old note's IS 875-3 estimate, not re-checked). Keep nothing loose on a balcony: no umbrellas, light planters or hanging chimes. Strap anything over 3 kg to a wall eye-bolt. A pot falling 126 m can kill someone below.

### A8.3 Fire: building systems first, then in-flat alarms

**The building already has fire systems.** NBC 2005 Part 4 Table 23 requires, for apartment houses above 60 m: extinguishers, a hose reel, a wet riser, a yard hydrant, automatic sprinklers, a manual fire alarm, and automatic detection and alarm (verified in the 2005 text). Expect sprinkler heads, and possibly detectors, on the flat's ceilings.

**The Homz false ceiling is the risk.** A false ceiling below a sprinkler head blocks its spray. Paint or tape on the bulb stops it opening.

**Cheapest: keep the ceiling clear of the heads.** Limit the false ceiling to perimeter coves that stay clear of every sprinkler head, so nobody alters the fire line. If a ceiling must pass under a head, budget the fire contractor's drop extension per head (price unverified; get a per-head quote, and count the heads first) and get the facility manager's written OK.

The Homz contract wording is A8.10 ask 1. Also: detectors are relocated to the new ceiling, not buried above it.

If the flat has no detectors inside, the in-flat alarms below matter more.

**In-flat smoke alarms.** US Fire Administration guidance: an alarm inside each sleeping room and outside sleeping areas, interconnected if possible, tested monthly, replaced every 10 years. For this flat, 6 smoke alarms: Master (Kabir sleeps here), BR-01, BR-02, BR-03 (Gurudwara), the Living on its Dining side (within ~3 m of the kitchen door, outside all bedrooms), and the Drawing near the main door. The Drawing is 13'×17'8" and open to the Living, but its far corner sits ~7 m from a central Living alarm (floor-plan estimate), and it is the exit path. One more alarm costs less than arguing the coverage. The Gurudwara plan (A07e) has no flame or incense, so a standard smoke alarm suits BR-03. If a jyot or dhoop is ever added there, swap that alarm for a heat alarm.

**Kitchen heat alarm.** A smoke alarm false-alarms on cooking, so the kitchen gets a **FireAngel FA6720-R heat alarm** (BS 5446-2, 10-year battery, ₹5,611, B09WB44VSK). Amazon lists it as a kitchen heat alarm. Fix it to the ceiling centre, away from the chimney hood. **It is unlinked.** Aqara sells no heat alarm, so this one sounds only in the kitchen. The linked Living alarm by the kitchen door is the backup for a kitchen fire.

| Model | Standard (evidence) | Interlink | India service | ₹ | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Aqara Smart Smoke Detector**, India model JY-GZ-03AQ, Zigbee, CR17450 10-year battery | EU sibling SD-S01E/SD-S01D: EU Declaration of Conformity cites EN 14604:2005+AC:2008, SGS-CSTC Guangzhou report GZES240500945131. Same size and battery as the India model. India SKU's own certification unconfirmed | Yes, through the §1 Hub M2 ("Device Alarm Grouping") | Modo India | 7,110 | **Pick**, ×6. Linked sirens plus phone alerts |
| FireAngel FA6620-R optical, 10-year sealed | BS EN 14604:2005 (Amazon listing) | No | None; UK import | 5,274 | **Alternative**, ×6 (₹31,644). Choose it if you want no hub dependency |
| IFITech 10-year photoelectric | Claims EN 14604 and UL 217 (listing) | No | Indian importer, WhatsApp support | 2,690 | Value, unlinked. The title ends "(6)" but the spec table says 1 item, so the per-unit price is either ₹2,690 or ~₹450. Check before ordering. Replaceable CR123A battery, not sealed |
| X-Sense SD19-W, RF interlinked | UL 217 (US) | Yes, no hub | None; grey import | 5,508 | Rejected: no India channel |
| Ei Electronics Ei650 | EN 14604 | No | None | 7,923 | Rejected: dearer than FireAngel for the same job |
| Notofire Valuecon | None stated | No | Noida maker, 1-year warranty | 3,199 / 5 | Rejected: no standard stated |

**The interlink trade-off.** USFA recommends interconnected alarms. Everyone sleeps in the Master, so the fire that matters starts elsewhere: Living, Drawing or kitchen. An unlinked alarm there may not wake a family behind a shut Master door until smoke reaches the Master alarm. Aqara links all six and sends a phone alert, for ~₹11,000 more than six FireAngels (₹42,660 vs ₹31,644). The Hub M2 is already a §1 Must, so it adds no new part. The cost of the pick: the India SKU's EN 14604 status is unconfirmed, though the EU sibling holds an SGS test report. If the hub fails, each Aqara should still sound in its own room from its built-in siren, which is the FireAngel's normal behaviour (hub-offline behaviour unverified: unplug the hub once and test).

**Set-up.** Grouping isn't automatic, despite Modo's copy. In the Aqara Home app, open each detector, then More > Device Settings > Device Alarm Grouping, and switch on every other detector (Aqara's product page). Then press test on one alarm and check all six sound. Before ordering, ask Modo for the India SKU's EN 14604 test report.

**The hearing test.** Shut every bedroom door. One adult lies in the Master. The other presses test on the Living alarm, then on the kitchen heat alarm. Check the Master alarm sounds too: that proves the link. The real question is the unlinked heat alarm. If it isn't heard, rely on the linked Living alarm by the kitchen door and the phone alert, and keep the phone's alert on loud at night. Don't add a hub-siren fallback: the Hub M2 sits on a Living shelf (§1), so it's no louder in the Master.

### A8.4 Kitchen: PNG detector, the valve question, fire kit

**Detector.** Natural gas is lighter than air, so the detector goes high. Mount it within ~30 cm of the ceiling and 2–4 m from the hob. Ceasefire gives the 2–4 m spacing for its own detector. Never mount it directly above the hob.

**The kitchen ceiling.** Methane rising into an open false-ceiling void passes a detector mounted below it. Best: no false ceiling in the kitchen. If Homz fits one, it must be sealed, with no open cut-outs or gaps around lights. Without a false ceiling, mount the detector on the structural ceiling or the top 30 cm of the wall.

**Power.** The Aqara runs on a 12 V DC adapter (Modo spec: 12.0 V, 0.5 A). Homz runs the low-voltage lead through the false ceiling or a painted raceway to the existing chimney or fridge socket. That's carpentry on a 12 V cable and needs no electrician. The adapter's lead length isn't published; buy a 12 V DC extension lead if it's short (unverified). The flat has 100% DG backup, so the detector loses power only for seconds at changeover. The old advice to add a UPS is dropped.

| Model | Gas | Power | Alerts | ₹ | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Aqara Smart Gas Detector** JT-BZ-03AQ/A | Methane (CH4) | 12 V DC adapter, no battery | 85 dB siren + phone + §1 Hub M2 siren | 8,210 (Modo) | **Pick.** Wall- or ceiling-mounts high |
| Firlarm BRJ-502 | NG, LPG; claims EN 50194 | Plug-in, European plug | 85 dB siren | 3,477 | **Value, with a catch.** It plugs straight into a socket, so it sits at socket height, too low for methane, unless a socket is high on the wall |
| Ceasefire ICR01 | LPG + NG | Not stated | Siren | 6,372 | Indian brand with service; spec sheet thin |
| VERiShield RB-GH1 | LPG/PNG/CNG | AC cord | Siren | 1,899 | Rejected: no standard stated |

**The automatic shut-off valve.** Skip it. Any change to the PNG line is the gas company's job. Megha Gas says its own technicians do installation and appliance conversion. BGL's published dos and don'ts say nothing about customer fittings. A DIY solenoid on a gas line is a liability. Ask the gas company once: "Do you fit or approve a detector-linked shut-off valve?" If they say no, rely on the detector, the hob's flame-failure devices and the meter valve. The Aqara Valve Controller T1 is rated for water only, so never put it on a gas valve.

**Know the meter valve.** Find the PNG meter and its isolation valve on day one. Show both adults. That valve is step 1 of every leak (A19).

**Which gas company.** BGL's Hyderabad area list includes Gachibowli but not Nanakramguda or the Financial District. Megha Gas lists Rangareddy district as a service area. Read the operator off the meter or the bill (A19).

**Fire kit.**
- **Fire blanket.** 2-pack, claims EN 1869:2019, ₹349 (B0CJM47CYH). Hang one at the kitchen exit and one in the utility, next to the hob side. It's the first response for a pan or oil fire.
- **Extinguisher pick.** VariEx 2 kg ABC powder, ₹945 (B07JDCWJR8). The listing claims ISI and BIS marks, refillable, with a wall bracket. Check the ISI mark (IS 15683) on the cylinder when it arrives. The listing doesn't state the powder grade. Cheaper Indian ABC units often use a lower MAP percentage than Ceasefire's 90% (unverified for VariEx). Mount it at the kitchen exit, not beside the hob.
- **Premium alternative.** Ceasefire ABC MAP90 2 kg, ₹7,434 at Ceasefire's shop. Same job, a known Indian brand with a service network. Choose it if you want one call for refills and annual checks.
- **Refill.** Powder extinguishers need a refill after any use and a check every year. Find a Hyderabad refill dealer before you need one; get the number onto the A19 card.
- **Rejected:** Ceasefire MultiMax 2 L (₹14,160) and clean-agent 2 kg (₹27,895). Both are overkill for one kitchen.

### A8.5 Tip-over, scalds and small child-proofing

- **Anti-tip straps:** stainless steel, rated 181 kg, ₹347 per 2 (B0DNT73M73). Fit them to every loose bookcase, chest of drawers and TV unit, and to the TV if it isn't wall-mounted. Screw into masonry with the plugs supplied. A screw into gypsum board pulls out.
- **Homz built-ins:** wardrobes, lofts, the TV wall and the study unit. Put "every tall unit fixed to masonry at the top" in the Homz contract.
- **Scalds.** Set each geyser to 55 °C, the temperature §11 sizes the tanks for, and the master's to 50 °C for Kabir's bath. Check the dial on each new geyser; factory settings vary by brand (unverified, so look). Teach Kabir to turn the cold on first and off last.
- **Legionella.** Stored water below 60 °C can grow Legionella, and geysers sit lukewarm between uses (general public-health guidance, not re-checked for India). Optionally run each geyser at 60 °C or above for an hour once a week (A11.2): an app schedule on the Wi-Fi models, the dial on the others. After a week away, run each shower hot for a minute before anyone stands under it.
- **Medicines and cleaners.** Store them above 1.5 m, in a cabinet Kabir can't open.
- **At age 6, skip:** socket covers, corner guards, door-pinch guards, cabinet straps.
- **Blinds:** cordless or motorised only (§7.2), because a looped cord can strangle a child.

### A8.6 Handover checks (₹0)

| Check | How | If it fails |
|---|---|---|
| RCCB | At the flat's distribution board, find the 30 mA RCCB (also called RCD or ELCB). Press its test button. It must trip at once. Reset it | If there's none, or it doesn't trip, the building electrician fits or replaces it. About a 1-hour job on the board, before the geysers go in |
| Sprinklers and detectors | Count heads on every ceiling; photograph them before Homz starts | Homz drawings must show them (A8.3) |
| Window grills | 100 mm ball test on every grill | Restrictor on that sash |
| Main door | Ask the facility manager whether the teak door is a rated, self-closing fire door | See A8.7 before the lock goes in |
| Inlet valves | Check whether each water inlet has a reachable quarter-turn lever valve | Decides the optional T1 valve (A8.8) |

### A8.7 Main-door lock

| Lock | Door thickness | Body | Paths | Connectivity | ₹ | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Godrej Catus Connect** (4216/4217) | 35–65 mm (Godrej page) | Mortise, 3 bolts (in-box list) | Fingerprint, PIN, RFID, app, key | Built-in Wi-Fi | 12,074 | **Pick.** Godrej installs free; 3-year warranty; 803 reviews at 4.3 |
| Yale Zuri S | 35–65 mm (listing) | Not stated | Fingerprint, PIN, RFID, app, key | Bluetooth (listing title) | 12,999 | Alternative at about the same price |
| Yale Kyra Pro | Not stated on Yale India's page | "Fully-auto lock case" | Fingerprint, PIN, RFID, app, key | Needs a "Yale Home kit", sold separately | ~25,000–32,000 (unverified) | Only if the leaf is over 65 mm. Confirm fit with Yale (1800-103-5606) first |

**Measure the teak leaf before you order.** The old note said a premium teak leaf is often 70–90 mm. No source supports that, so measure the door.

**Fire rating.** If the main door is a rated fire door, cutting a new mortise can void the rating. Ask the facility manager for the door's rating before Godrej cuts it. If it's rated, ask Godrej whether the Catus fits the existing mortise without enlarging it.

**Fire egress.** NBC 2005 Part 4 clause 4.7.5 says exit doors must open from the inside without a key. Confirm at the showroom that the inside lever retracts every bolt in one move, with no PIN.

**Alexa.** Godrej doesn't list Alexa support. Use the app for status. Never enable voice unlock.

**Order of work.** The lock cuts the teak leaf. Fit it after Homz finishes any polish on the main door.

### A8.8 Water leak sensors

The bathrooms are finished and have floor drains, so a geyser or WC leak drains away. The risk sits where there's no drain:

| Position | Why |
|---|---|
| Kitchen sink base | RO and sink trap |
| Washer in the utility | Hose bursts are the classic flood |

**Aqara Water Leak Sensor**, IP67, ₹3,080 each at Modo; needs the §1 Aqara Hub M2 (₹7,400), whose siren sounds in the Living. Pair each sensor where it will sit and check range first (A1).

**Optional auto shut-off with no plumbing.** The Aqara Valve Controller T1 (₹10,410, Modo) clamps onto an existing lever or butterfly-handle ball valve, DN15–DN25, and turns it on a leak alert. It runs on 4 AA batteries. It only works if each inlet has a quarter-turn lever valve you can reach. This flat has two water sources, municipal and bore, so you may need two. Check the inlets at handover (A8.6) before buying.

**Dropped:** 10 sensors, the motorised inlet valve and the flow-sensing valve on the main. Each needs a plumber to cut pipe, and the owner has ruled out plumbing work.

### A8.9 Optional items

- **CO alarm.** WOLF SHIELD BJ-826W, ₹5,250 (B0CL4FKND6). The seller claims EN 50291-1:2018; it has a 10-year sealed battery and an 85 dB siren at 50 ppm. Mount at ~1.5 m in the dining area. Risk is low: the flat has electric geysers and a ducted chimney. Never install a gas geyser indoors.
- **Indoor camera.** TP-Link Tapo C225. TP-Link's India page confirms a 4 MP sensor, a physical lens-down privacy mode, microSD to 512 GB, RTSP and ONVIF, and 2.4 GHz Wi-Fi only. It isn't on Amazon.in on 2026-10-01. TP-Link links Myntra and Croma; price ~₹3,500–4,500 (unverified). Buy one only to see the helper's hours or the entrance; the §1 doorbell covers the door. Put it on the IoT network, never in a bedroom or the Gurudwara. The Tapo C220 is rejected because it has a software privacy mode only.

### A8.10 Homz contract asks from this section

*Copy asks 1–6 word for word into the §7 Homz contract list. The owner signs from §7.*

1. Show existing sprinkler heads and detectors on the ceiling drawings. Keep false ceilings to perimeter coves clear of the heads where possible. Where a ceiling passes under a head, the building's fire contractor extends the drop, with the facility manager's written OK. Nobody paints or tapes a head or detector.
2. Fix every tall built-in to masonry at the top.
3. Run the gas detector's 12 V DC lead through the false ceiling or a painted raceway to the existing chimney or fridge socket. Low-voltage carpentry only; no new socket and no electrician.
4. No false ceiling in the kitchen. If there must be one, seal it with no open cut-outs, so methane reaches the detector.
5. Don't drill the balcony floor slab for anything.
6. State whether Homz will subcontract the invisible grille and pigeon net. If it does, the A8.2 spec applies: vertical SS316 at 50 mm, a mill test certificate for the wire lot, a stated breaking load, the ball test, and a warranty covering anchor pull-out and leaks.

## A9. Kitchen worktop, sinks and taps — detail

*Main body: [§9](#9-kitchen-worktop-sinks-and-taps). Rewritten 2026-10-01 for the Homz Interio turnkey contract and the rule that no plumbing, gas or electrical point moves. Revised the same day: the builder's platform and sink stay by default, because removing them is civil and tiling work. Second revision: hob cut-out and gas-feed checks added, the utility sink swapped for a top-mount-capable model, and the Bosch spacer marked unlisted in India.*

### A9.1 What changed from the old note

| Old recommendation | Now | Why |
|---|---|---|
| New quartz worktop on the main run, granite only in the utility | **Keep the builder's platform and sink.** A new top (dark granite) only if there's no platform or it's damaged | The builder spec puts the dado tile "2 ft above platform" ([[my-home]]), so a platform exists. Removing it means breaking brick or RCC and the dado tiles: civil work the owner ruled out. If a new top is needed, granite (8.4) beats quartz (7.2): no trivet rule, no UV issue under the south window |
| Counter height = cook's elbow minus 10–15 cm | **Height is whatever the builder set.** A pull-out board at ~750 mm fixes it for rolling and kneading | Changing the height means rebuilding the platform |
| Tall unit holds the fridge and a built-in oven | **Fridge, pantry and oven.** If no 16 A point exists in that zone, Homz adds one | Run it through the false ceiling and inside the tall unit, so no finished wall is chased |
| Worktop thickness unstated | **20 mm with a 30 mm built-up granite edge round the hob cut-out** | The §4 Bosch PNH7O6G20I needs 30 mm of worktop, or 12 mm "with spacer, order separately" (Bosch spec page). Bosch India lists no spacer part, so treat it as unavailable until Bosch service gives a part number (same rule as §4 and A4) |
| Full-height slab backsplash, seamed | **Keep the builder's ceramic dado** | The builder tiled 2 ft above the platform ([[my-home]]). Re-tiling is out of scope, and a slab backsplash adds seams and lift trips |
| Inset granite or sintered hot-pan strip beside the hob (~₹4k) | **Cut** | With a granite top the strip has no job. With quartz, it adds two seams beside the hottest zone. A loose stone trivet does the job |
| Carysil Waltz 780 quartz workstation sink | **Kohler Strive 30×18×10 SS304** | The Waltz 780 wasn't found on Amazon.in or in a dealer listing. Quartz-composite sinks show limescale in hard water |
| Kohler Malleco "562IN-SD" at ₹15,519 | Malleco kept as an upgrade at **₹13,189**, code **562IN-SD-VS** | The Amazon.in listing title ends "562IN-SD-VS" (Vibrant Stainless) |
| Kohler Atom 25981IN-4 "high-arc gooseneck" at ₹9,939 | **Kohler Atom 25981IN-4-CP pull-down, ₹7,559** | The listing is a pull-down with mechanical docking, not a gooseneck |
| Hindware pull-out mixer 526512 for the utility | **Jaquar Aria wall sink cock, ₹2,280** | The Hindware SKU couldn't be verified. The utility most likely has a cold-only wall outlet, and a mixer needs a hot line |
| Jayna Crown utility sink; "avoid Cera 202" | **Kohler Strive 21×18×10, 38778IN, ₹13,019** (value: Carysil Quadro Ultra 24×18×8 QS24188, ₹10,679) | Jayna couldn't be verified. The first-round pick, Hindware Domingo Neo (B0CW9BSXT3), lists "Installation Type: Undermount" and 1 mm steel, so it can't drop into a builder hole. The Strive lists "universal" and the Carysil "Top Mount" |

### A9.2 Check the kitchen as built (before the Homz design meeting)

The brochure plan shows a U-shaped platform, a sink under the window, a hob next to the utility and a fridge marked "F" by the door. The builder spec lists a ceramic dado to 2 ft above the platform, a chimney provision, an RO provision, and separate municipal and bore water. Confirm each of these on site, because they fix the design:

| Check | Why it matters | If… |
|---|---|---|
| Is there a builder platform (stone on brick or RCC) and a sink? | Keeping both is the default. It saves the worktop and sink cost and avoids civil work | Sound: Homz prices drawers and shutters under it. Cracked or missing: only then buy the A9.4 granite |
| Gaps between the platform's support walls, and the depth under the slab | The supports fix every drawer width. A drawer box needs a clear rectangular bay | Measure each bay. Ask Homz to draw drawer widths to the bays, not to a catalogue module |
| Stone thickness at the front edge, what the stone rests on, any wall under the hob bay, and any hob cut-out already made | The Bosch hob needs 30 mm of stone (or a spacer Bosch India doesn't list) and a 555–557 × 475–477 mm opening with open space below. A cut in place can crack the slab (see below the table) | Have the stone vendor inspect before you buy the hob. Under 30 mm: ask Bosch service for a spacer part number, else a 30 mm built-up granite edge. Support wall or sub-slab under the hob bay, or the vendor won't guarantee the cut: keep a tabletop hob |
| Dado tile: height of its top edge (and bottom edge if there's no platform) | Wall cabinets should start at or below the top edge, so no bare strip shows | New top only: if the counter sits below the tile's bottom edge, add a 75–100 mm granite upstand |
| Water outlet: on the wall above the counter, or angle valves under it? | Decides wall tap or deck tap | Wall outlet: wall tap. A deck tap would mean chasing new pipe, which is plumbing work |
| Drain position under the sink | The sink bowl needs to reach it | Fit the sink to the drain, not the drain to the sink |
| PNG gas point (above or below the counter?) and chimney cutout | They fix the hob position. A built-in hob takes its gas inlet under the counter. A platform built for a tabletop stove usually has the point on the wall above it | Ask the flat's PNG operator (BGL or Megha Gas: read the meter) whether the existing point can feed a built-in hob with an approved hose. If it needs re-routing, that's the operator's work and out of scope: keep a tabletop hob (§4) |
| Sockets: fridge, chimney, RO, microwave, any 16 A | They fix the tall unit and the appliance garage | No 16 A point where the oven goes: Homz adds one through the false ceiling and inside the tall unit. Chasing a finished wall stays out of scope |
| Window sill height above the counter (south window) | A tall pull-down tap can foul a low sliding sash | Sill less than ~450 mm above the counter: pick a lower spout (unverified rule of thumb) |

**A hob cut-out in the existing top.** A 475–477 mm opening in a ~600 mm-deep slab leaves ~120 mm of stone, front and back together. A cut made in place can crack it. The risk rises if the stone sits on an RCC or kadappa sub-slab, or on a brick wall under the hob bay. A crack forces a new hob-run slab, which is the civil work this note avoids. So the order is: stone vendor inspects, BGL confirms the gas feed, then you buy the built-in hob. If either says no, buy a tabletop hob instead (§4 lists the Elica FLEXI as the on-top option). These are practitioner risks, not tested here.

**Ask Homz what the quote assumes.** Turnkey modular-kitchen quotes often price a new granite top and assume the builder platform comes out. Ask Homz, in writing, whether its kitchen line keeps the platform and fits under it, and what countertop the ₹18L includes. If Homz's line can't work under a builder top, that's a change order to price before signing, not on site.

### A9.3 Layout: the simplest good plan

```
            N (Dining side, entry)
   +--------------------------------------+
   | F  TALL   |                          |  <- door from Dining
   | fridge +  |                          |
   | pantry    |                          |
   |-----------+                          |
   | base run  |                          | door to Utility ->
   | (dry      |                          |  E
   |  store,   |                  HOB at  |
   |  pantry)  |                  gas pt, |
   |           |                  chimney |
   |           |      PREP  ----->  above |
   |           +----------+---------------|
   |                SINK (under S window) |
   +--------------------------------------+
            S (window)        Utility beyond the east wall
```

*Sketch from the brochure plan, not to scale. Confirm every point on site.*

- **Zones in the order you cook.** Fridge, then a dry-store run, then the sink, then a prep run, then the hob. The prep run is the stretch between the sink and the hob, so keep it at least 900 mm long, with no tall unit or appliance garage on it.
- **Work triangle.** Measured off the brochure plan, fridge to sink is ~3.7 m, sink to hob ~2.1 m and hob to fridge ~3.9 m: ~9.7 m in total. The usual rule of thumb is 1.2–2.7 m per leg and under ~7.9 m in total (a common US kitchen-design guideline, not checked against the primary source). The fridge leg runs long. Accept it, because the fridge socket is at "F" and moving it is electrical work. The upside is that people can reach the fridge without walking through the cooking zone.
- **Tall unit at "F".** Fridge niche plus a pantry pull-out. Put the microwave in the pantry tower only if a socket already sits there. Size the niche from the fridge you actually buy: width plus ~50 mm, plus the maker's rear vent gap. The §4 Bosch KCP88E256I is 842 mm wide and 594 mm deep (Bosch spec), so the niche is ~900 mm. The Samsung RF57A alternative is 817 mm wide but 765 mm deep (Amazon.in listing), so it stands ~165 mm proud of a 600 mm counter. The crated Bosch is 2,018 × 902 × 656 mm, so check the lift door before delivery.
- **Counter height.** It's whatever the builder built. The usual rule is the main cook's elbow minus 10–15 cm, and India's default is ~850 mm. If the platform is too high for the main cook, fit a pull-out board at ~750 mm in one base unit for rolling and kneading. If it's too low, a chopping block on the counter helps more than rebuilding. Only a new top (no platform) lets you choose the height.
- **No dishwasher (owner, 2026-10-02).** The bay beside the sink takes drawers and the two-bin pull-out (§12). No T-valve is needed.
- **Out of scope:** removing or rebuilding the platform, moving the sink, the hob, the gas line or the drain, and chasing finished walls. Flag any Homz drawing that does.

### A9.4 Worktop

**This section applies only if there's no builder platform or it's damaged.** Bottom line: one dark granite, leathered or polished, for kitchen and utility. Use 20 mm with a 30 mm built-up edge round the hob cut-out, or 30 mm on the hob run. Quartz only if you want warm-white and accept two rules: trivets always, and a blind on the south window.

Inversion first. These are the top reasons to reject each material for this kitchen (PNG gas, curry cooking, hard water, south sun on the sink run, a 6-year-old, a residential lift):

| Material | Top reason to reject | Stain / Heat / Scratch / UV | ₹/sqft installed | VFM | Func | Dur | Style | Score | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Granite, dark** (Absolute Black, Black Galaxy, Steel Grey) | Limited colour story; honed black shows water marks | Good / Excellent / Excellent / Stable | ~150–450 | 9 | 9 | 9 | 6 | **8.4** | **Pick** |
| Quartz (engineered), e.g. Pokarna Quantra | Its maker says no direct sun and no pans above 150 °C. Cutting it creates silica dust | Excellent / Weak / Good / **Not UV-stable** | ~500–1,500 | 6 | 7 | 7 | 9 | 7.2 | Optional style pick |
| Sintered stone (Dekton, Neolith) | Edges chip on impact and can't be patched invisibly; few Hyderabad fabricators do it well; costs the most | Excellent / Excellent / Excellent / Stable | ~low thousands | 4 | 8 | 6 | 9 | 6.8 | Skip: Homz's materials page offers granite and quartz only, so sintered needs a separate fabricator on site |
| Porcelain large slab | Thin 12–15 mm domestic slabs chip, and a chip shows the body | Excellent / Very good / Very good / Stable | ~150–450 (material) | 7 | 8 | 6 | 8 | 7.2 | Skip |
| Quartzite | Often mislabelled (dolomitic marble sold as "quartzite"); needs sealing | Good if sealed / Very good / Excellent | ~800–2,500 | 4 | 7 | 8 | 9 | 7.1 | Skip |
| Marble | Lemon, tomato and tamarind etch it, and sealing doesn't stop etching | Poor | ~150–3,000 | 4 | 3 | 5 | 9 | 5.0 | Reject |
| Solid surface (Corian) | A hot pan scorches it next to a gas hob | Excellent / Poor / Fair | ~700–2,500 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 7 | 4.7 | Reject |

*Scores are this note's BIFL judgement. Prices are the old note's indicative ranges (unverified, not Hyderabad-quoted). Get 2–3 yard quotes.*

**Why granite beats quartz here, not in general.** Quantra's FAQ says not to use it where it's directly exposed to sunlight, because its unsaturated polyester resin degrades in UV. It also says not to place pots above 150 °C on it. The sink run sits under the south window, and Hyderabad's winter sun comes in low through that glass. Granite has neither problem. Most Homz-style quotes include granite as standard (confirm), so quartz is an upgrade you'd pay extra for, with two house rules attached.

**Granite details.**
- **Colour and finish:** dark or mid-tone flecked stone hides turmeric and oil. Leathered or polished, not honed: honed black shows every hard-water ring (practitioner view, unverified).
- **Test before buying:** put a few drops of water on the actual slab for 5 minutes. If the stone darkens, it's porous. Reject it, or seal it and re-test yearly. Dense black granites usually pass with no sealing.
- **Edge:** a small eased (1–3 mm) radius on every exposed corner. That matters at a 6-year-old's head height.
- **One yard lot** for kitchen and utility, so the colour matches.

**If you choose quartz:** Pokarna Quantra is made in Secunderabad. Ask for its honed or matte finish in a warm-white or greige. Fit a sheer or roller blind on the south kitchen window (§7.2). Keep two stone trivets beside the hob. Have every cutout wet-cut at the yard, not dry-cut in the flat. Engineered-stone fabrication releases respirable crystalline silica, and Australia banned engineered stone in 2024 over silicosis (not re-verified in this pass).

**Slabs, seams and the lift.**
- Quantra slab sizes, from its FAQ: Regular 3,050 × 1,400 mm (12, 20 or 30 mm thick), Jumbo 3,300 × 1,650, Super Jumbo 3,470 × 2,010. Granite slabs vary by yard.
- The kitchen's long leg is ~4.2 m (13'10"), so it has at least one seam whatever the lift.
- Every piece must pass the service lift (the five lift numbers live in [A12.9](#a129-the-service-lift-and-delivery-terms)). The lift isn't measured yet. In an assumed 1,100 × 1,300 mm car with a 2,300 mm-high cabin, a 600 mm-deep strip fits up to ~2.3 m long, stood on edge in the floor diagonal (geometry: 1.70 m diagonal × 2.3 m height). The ~800 × 2,100 mm door may cut that further, because the piece has to turn as it goes in. So the ~4.2 m leg needs at least two seams, and the ~2.8 m leg needs one. Measure the car and door before ordering.
- Ask for a seam drawing. Seams go at the U corners and at least 150 mm from any cutout, never through the hob or sink cutout.
- Hob cutout: 555–557 × 475–477 mm for the Bosch PNH7O6G20I (Bosch spec page), radiused inside corners. Put it on the seam drawing.

**Rough quantity (new top only).** Three runs at ~600 mm deep: ~4.2 m + ~2.8 m + ~2.4 m, less two corner overlaps. That's ~4.9 m², or ~53 sqft, plus ~10 sqft in the utility. Call it ~65 sqft. At ~₹230–540/sqft installed for granite that's ₹15k–35k. At ~₹550–1,400 for quartz, ₹35k–90k. Both are estimates: measure on site.

**GST.** Finished stone slabs carry 18% GST (the old note's finding, carried forward unverified). Yard quotes often exclude it, so ask. The Homz quote is tax-inclusive.

**Care.** Wipe up lemon and tamarind spills within an hour, even on granite. Never cut directly on stone. Use a board.

### A9.5 Sinks

**Keep the builder's sink if there is one.** Buy only if the builder fitted none, or fitted a thin or double bowl you dislike. On a builder top, buy a top-mount sink sized to the existing cut-out: an undermount needs a new, polished cut in the stone. On a new granite top, undermount is fine. Either way, pick one large, deep SS304 bowl. The dishwasher takes the rinse load a second bowl would justify. A 10-inch (254 mm) bowl swallows a kadai and a pressure cooker.

| Use | Pick | Verified listing | ₹ (Oct 2026, GST incl.) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kitchen | **Kohler Strive 30×18×10 in, 1.2 mm SS304, 38796IN-NA** | [B0F5Y2ZMG6](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0F5Y2ZMG6) | 16,319 | Listing: top, flush or undermount; "lifetime warranty up to 25 years". Needs a 900 mm sink base |
| Kitchen, smaller base | Kohler Strive 24×20×10 in, 38795IN-NA | [B0F5XJX43D](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0F5XJX43D) | 13,949 | For a 750 mm sink base |
| Kitchen, alternative | Kohler Strive 24×18×10 in, 38779IN-NA | [B0F5Y2FNC3](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0F5Y2FNC3) | 14,576 | Narrower front-to-back than the 24×20 |
| Kitchen, zero-radius look | Carysil Quadro Ultra 30×18×8 in SS304, QS30188 | [B00PQ7WWQC](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B00PQ7WWQC) | 13,708 | 8 in deep only; 7-year warranty. Sharp corners are harder to clean |
| Utility | **Kohler Strive 21×18×10 in, 1.2 mm SS304, 38778IN-NA** | [B0F5X8MQ2L](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0F5X8MQ2L) | 13,019 | Listing: "Installation Type: universal". Sold by Clicktech Retail, the same seller as the kitchen Strive |
| Utility, value | Carysil Quadro Ultra 24×18×8 in SS304, QS24188 | [B00PQ7WTL0](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B00PQ7WTL0) | 10,679 | Listing: "Installation Type: Top Mount"; 7-year warranty. Zero-radius corners, 8 in deep |

**Rejected for the utility:** Hindware Domingo Neo 24×18×9 (B0CW9BSXT3, ₹9,130): the listing says "Undermount" and "Hindware uses 1mm thick steel". Nirali Orbis 24×18×8 (B0FJYBD9X5, ₹8,765): listed "Undermount". A Franke 24×18 listing (B0H7N4G71Z) says "Top Mounted" in its title and "Under Mount" in its specs, from a third-party seller: skip.

**Rejected:** double bowls (they split the volume a big vessel needs); fireclay (cracks under a dropped kadai); SS202 or thin pressed sinks (they dent and drum); quartz-composite sinks (limescale films on dark colours in hard water, and none were verified at a usable price); a workstation ledge sink (a nice-to-have, but no verified SKU).

**Hard water.** Hyderabad bore water leaves white rings on steel too. A satin finish hides them better than polished. Wipe the bowl dry at night.

### A9.6 Taps

**Rule: match the outlet you have.** The RO drinking tap is separate (§5), so the sink tap picks for reach, not filtration. In hard water, buy a brass body with a ceramic cartridge. Homz's plumber swaps the tap on the existing outlet or angle valves: a 30-minute visit, not re-plumbing. Name it in the BOQ.

| Outlet | Pick | Verified listing | ₹ | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Valves under the counter | **Kohler Atom pull-down, 25981IN-4-CP** | [B0BT7VT364](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0BT7VT364) | 7,559 | Listing: solid brass, ceramic cartridge, mechanical docking, single hole, 10-year warranty |
| Valves under the counter, upgrade | Kohler Malleco pull-down, Vibrant Stainless, 562IN-SD-VS | [B07RNXS7WW](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B07RNXS7WW) | 13,189 | Includes a soap dispenser and a 10-inch escutcheon; 10-year warranty |
| Wall outlet, kitchen | **Kohler Fore Arc wall tap, cold only, single lever, swivel spout** | [B0CL9ZMCS6](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0CL9ZMCS6) | 2,839 | No pull-out. A wall pull-out would need re-plumbing |
| Wall outlet, kitchen, value | Kohler Beam wall tap, cold only | [B09DCKZ3KT](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B09DCKZ3KT) | 1,839 | Brass per the listing |
| Utility | **Jaquar Aria wall sink cock, swinging spout, ARI-CHR-39347S** | [B08RBNTYZX](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B08RBNTYZX) | 2,280 | Cold only |

A single-lever mixer works on cold only. But there's no point buying one unless a kitchen hot line exists. Adding one (an under-sink instant geyser) needs a socket under the sink: check for it before you buy.

**Hard-water care.** Unscrew and descale the aerator every 2–3 months in vinegar. Pull-down spray heads with rubber nozzles rub clean.

## A10. Lamps and decorative lighting — detail

Portable lamps and art lighting only. Ceiling lights, coves and downlights come from Homz's false-ceiling scope (§7). Palette target: 2700–3000K in lamps, warm white and brass shades, oak and putty.

#### Contents (A10)
- [The rule](#the-rule)
- [Corrections to the old note](#corrections-to-the-old-note)
- [Lamps by room](#lamps-by-room)
- [Lighting the art](#lighting-the-art)
- [Kid safety](#kid-safety)
- [Dropped from A10](#dropped-from-a10)
- [Sources for A10](#sources-for-a10)

#### The rule

Buy a plain lamp with an **E27 socket**, then fit a **Philips WiZ E27** bulb set to 2700K. The bulb gives warm light, dimming and Alexa control with no hub. The lamp can change later without touching the smart layer. Three exceptions:

1. **No stacked dimmers.** A lamp with its own dimmer fights a smart bulb and flickers. Leave the lamp's dimmer at full, or skip it.
2. **Integrated-LED lamps can't take a smart bulb.** Treat their own touch control as the smart layer, or put them on a Tapo plug (§6).
3. **E14 lamps take E14 bulbs.** IKEA's RÖDFLIK desk lamp is E14. Use a plain 2700K E14 LED there.

Smart bulbs here are CRI 80. That's fine for lamps. It isn't fine for art, which wants CRI ≥90 (see below).

**One smart layer for lamps.** WiZ bulbs are it. §6's Tapo plugs cover only the Gurudwara light and any integrated-LED lamp, so no lamp carries both. A lamp's own inline switch cuts power to the bulb and takes it offline: leave it on.

| RÖDFLIK floor lamp has a "5 kg base" | 5.04 kg is the packed weight. The base is reinforced EVA plastic, 25 cm across | IKEA India product page |
| KUNGSHULT/SKAFTET is "weighted" | Base weight is reinforced EVA plastic, 48 cm across. No base weight published | IKEA India product page |

#### Lighting the art

**Use the Homz ceiling for art light.** This is the one item in this section that needs an electrician. Homz's electrician feeds the spots from the room's existing lighting circuit inside the false ceiling, while the ceiling is open. No new wall point and no new circuit. **Confirm the count, the drivers and the switching are in the ₹18L BOQ before the ceiling is boarded.**

**Where and how many.** About five spots in all: the foyer console wall (1–2), the Drawing's solid south wall over the sofa (2) and the Dining backdrop on the BR-03 wall (1). One spot per artwork up to ~1.2 m wide, two for anything wider. Place each spot ~0.5–0.7 m out from the wall so it hits the art at about 30°. That keeps glare off the glass and shadows short.

**Switching.** Each room's spots get their own switch leg and a Sonoff MINI-DIM inside the existing switch box (§1), fitted by Homz's electrician. That gives dimming, a sunset scene and a timer without a new plate or a bigger box.

**Spec for the BOQ.** Adjustable (tilting) recessed spot, **3–5 W, 3000K, CRI ≥90 with R9 ≥50, 60° beam** for canvases (36° only for a small work), driver included. Low wattage replaces dimming, because a dimmer means a new switch and a dimmable driver.

| Candidate | W | Beam | CRI | Dimmable | Tilts | ₹ | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| [White Teak Pandora 5 W J-spot, trimless, matt white, 3000K, 60°](https://www.whiteteak.com/pandora-5w-matt-white-3000k-60-degree-beam-angle-j-spot-trimless-recess-mounted-dl01-10437/) (DL01-10437) | 5 | 60° | ">90%" | No | **Not stated: check a sample** | 2,040 | Pick, if the sample tilts ≥25°. 5.7 cm cut-out. R9 not published: ask |
| [Same, 36°](https://www.whiteteak.com/pandora-5w-matt-white-3000k-36-degree-beam-angle-j-spot-trimless-recess-mounted-led-cob-downlights-dl01-10433/) (DL01-10433) | 5 | 36° | ">90%" | No | Not stated | 2,040 | Small works only; brighter in the centre |
| [White Teak Altair 6 W "PC Body Tiltable COB"](https://www.whiteteak.com/altair-6w-white-chrome-3000k-36-beam-angle-pc-body-recess-led-cob-downlights-dl01-10874/) | 6 | 36° | ">80%" | No | Yes | 590 | Rejected for art: CRI 80. White Teak's "Tiltable Downlights" range lists only Altair |
| [White Teak Pandora 15 W](https://www.whiteteak.com/pandora-15w-grey-white-3000k-36-beam-angle-recess-led-cob-downlights-dl01-10469/) (round 1 pick) | 15 | 36° | ">90%" | No | Not stated | ~3,500 | **Rejected.** About 1,350 lm in a 36° cone. That's ~500–1,000 lux on the art from a ceiling spot, 3–6× the oils limit |

If the Pandora sample won't tilt, ask Homz's lighting supplier for any adjustable spot that meets the same spec, with its datasheet.

**Lux estimate, not a measurement.** A 5 W, 90 lm/W, 60° spot gives roughly 100–150 lux at ~1.2 m on the wall. The same spot at 36° gives roughly 250–300 lux. These are hand calculations from the spec sheets. After install, measure at the centre of each artwork with a phone lux-meter app (accurate to roughly ±20%). If it reads over the limit, move the spot back from the wall or swap to the 60° version.

Wall-mounted wired picture lights are rejected: each needs a new point above the frame, which means chasing the wall and repainting.

**For works on paper, rotating art or a wall Homz misses:** a cordless rechargeable picture light such as LYTARA (₹3,999, 3000/4000/6500K, remote with timer, 5,000 mAh). Its own timer and low output suit paper. LYTARA publishes no CRI, so treat its colour as **unverified**.

**Lux limits.** Visible light fades pigment even with UV filtered. CCI's traditional limits:

| Medium | Max lux |
|---|---|
| Works on paper, watercolours, photographs, textiles | 50 |
| Oils, acrylics, painted panels, furniture | 150 |
| Stone, metal | 300 |
| UV, any | below 75 µW/lm |

CCI now treats these as starting points. It notes red lakes in many oils are as sensitive as paper, so 50 lux is the safe default for anything you value. Damage is cumulative, so **hours on matter as much as level**. Switch the art spots off when nobody is in the room, and rotate works on paper twice a year.

**Framing.** Tru Vue Optium Museum Acrylic for valuable, large or kid-reachable work (99% UV, anti-reflective, shatter-resistant). Conservation Clear glass is the value choice on sun-free walls. Use acid-free mats and reversible hinges. The old note lists Ahuja Framers, Delhi, as India's only listed Tru Vue distributor (**unverified** this round). Keep originals off any wall that takes direct sun through east or south glass.

**Where to buy art (old note, not re-checked):** Kalakriti and Srishti (Banjara Hills), State Gallery of Art (Madhapur), Artisera online, Saffronart, AstaGuru and DAG for higher-value work.

#### Kid safety

- Lamps within Kabir's reach need a wide base or a spot behind furniture. Neither IKEA floor lamp has a cast base.
- No glass shades in BR-02 or the Master.
- Run cords along walls. Don't leave a loop he can catch a foot in.
- A cordless lamp on a reachable surface removes the cord altogether.

#### Dropped from A10

- **Dyson Solarcycle Morph.** ₹37k for a desk lamp when a ₹14,490 light bar does the WFH job (§14).
- **Balcony lanterns.** At floor 42 a lantern is one more loose object near the rail.
- **Hue Bridge route.** WiZ needs no hub. Add Hue only if you want Zigbee scenes.
- **Havells smart lamps.** Closed app, not retrofit bulbs (old note).
- **Wired picture lights from Fos or White Teak.** They need a wall point per frame.
- **Art-light timers on a separate timer switch.** Not needed: the Sonoff MINI-DIM runs schedules (§1).
- **White Teak Pandora 15 W as the art spot** (round 1). It over-lights art; see the candidate table.

## A11. Bathrooms, geysers, glass partitions and ventilation — detail

**What changed from the old A11.** The builder handed over finished bathrooms: sanitaryware, CP fittings, tiles and waterproofing are done. VRF is installed. Homz Interio does the interiors. So this appendix covers only what the bathrooms still lack (geysers, glass partitions) plus ventilation, insect mesh and the utility. Plumbing, re-tiling, repainting and waterproofing work are out. The old A11 also carried art framing, mattresses, paint, bins, pest control, the mini-UPS, the dehumidifier and a long "shorter adds" list. Those are not bathroom items and are not carried here (see A11.8).

### A11.1 Geyser sizing: the physics

**Short answer to "do I need a 25 L?" (owner, 2 Oct: no).** A 15 L Wi-Fi geyser in every bathroom. The physics below still holds: the same 2 kW element puts the same heat into either tank each minute, so a 25 L doesn't delay the first hot water, but it does take ~32 min to fill with heat against ~19 for a 15 L. The owner's reasons win on this flat: Hyderabad's winter is mild (December–January daily mean ~22–23 °C) so the geyser runs hard only four months a year, the 15 L recovers faster, and four bathrooms for three people means a back-to-back shower uses the next scheduled tank. A schedule hides the 19 minutes (A11.2).

**The one condition everything hangs on: shower flow.** At floor 42's likely ~1 bar (≈6 L/min), a 15 L gives ~5 min and a 25 L ~8 min of 40 °C water in January. If a booster pump is ever fitted, flow rises to 8–9 L/min. Then a 15 L gives ~3 min and a 25 L ~5–6 min, so set the tanks to 60 °C in winter. The ~1 bar figure is unverified: measure it (§20).

**Assumptions.**

| Input | Value used | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Winter inlet (Dec–Jan) | **20 °C** | Hyderabad 1991–2020 normals: Dec–Jan daily mean 22.2–22.8 °C, mean minimum 13–14 °C. The roof tank sits ~3 floors up and cools overnight. Inlet not measured: unverified |
| Shoulder inlet (Nov, Feb) | 25 °C | Daily mean 24.1 °C (Nov), 25.4 °C (Feb) |
| Summer inlet (Mar–Oct) | 28–35 °C | Estimate. The geyser barely runs in these months |
| Tank setpoint | 55 °C | Scald and Legionella trade-off, A11.2 |
| Water at the shower head | 40 °C | Comfortable winter shower |
| Usable fraction of the tank | 85% | Mixing in the tank; estimate |
| Element efficiency | 95% | Standing losses during heat-up |
| Shower flow | 6 L/min (likely at ~1 bar), 8–9 L/min (with a booster pump), 12 L/min (rain head) | US EPA: standard heads 9.5 L/min, WaterSense ≤7.6 L/min. Floor-42 pressure lowers flow (see below) |

Energy = litres × 4.186 kJ/kg·K × ΔT. Time = energy ÷ (watts × 0.95).

**Heat-up from cold, minutes.**

| Inlet → setpoint | 10 L, 2 kW | 10 L, 3 kW | 15 L, 2 kW | 15 L, 3 kW | 25 L, 2 kW | 25 L, 3 kW |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 20 → 55 °C (Jan) | 13 | 9 | **19** | 13 | **32** | 21 |
| 20 → 60 °C | 15 | 10 | 22 | 15 | 37 | 25 |
| 25 → 55 °C (Nov, Feb) | 11 | 7 | 17 | 11 | 28 | 18 |
| 30 → 55 °C (summer) | 9 | 6 | 14 | 9 | 23 | 15 |

**Check against the maker.** AO Smith's Elegance Prime manual quotes a "reheating time for 35 °C rise" of **15 min (15 L) and 29 min (25 L)** at 2,000 W. Physics gives ~18 min and ~31 min at 100% efficiency. The 25 L figure is plausible; the 15 L figure needs ~2.4 kW, so treat it as optimistic.

**Minutes of 40 °C shower from a full tank** (element keeps heating during the shower).

| Tank | 6 L/min | 8 L/min | 12 L/min (rain) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 10 L, 2 kW, Jan | 3.2 | 2.2 | 1.4 |
| 15 L, 2 kW, Jan | **4.8** | 3.4 | 2.1 |
| 15 L, 2 kW, Jan, tank at 60 °C | 5.5 | 3.8 | 2.4 |
| 25 L, 2 kW, Jan | **8.0** | 5.6 | 3.5 |
| 25 L, 2 kW, Jan, tank at 60 °C | 9.2 | 6.4 | 4.0 |
| 25 L, 3 kW, Jan | 9.4 | 6.2 | 3.7 |
| 25 L, 2 kW, Nov/Feb | 10.2 | 6.9 | 4.2 |

Method: a 55 °C tank mixed with 20 °C cold gives 1.75 L of 40 °C water per litre of tank. A 2 kW element adds ~1.4 L/min of 40 °C water while you shower (3 kW: ~2.0 L/min).

**What this means per bathroom.**

| Bathroom | Likely use | Size | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| Master, 9'×6' | Two adults, back-to-back winter showers | **15 L, Wi-Fi** | ~5 min of 40 °C water at ~6 L/min in January (~5.5 min at 60 °C). The second person uses the next bathroom, whose tank is already hot on its own schedule, or waits ~19 min |
| Kabir (sleeps in the master) | Bucket bath, ~15–20 L of warm water | **Master's 15 L at 50 °C** (Children Care Mode) | At 50 °C a 15 L gives ~19 L of 40 °C water, just enough; at 55 °C ~22 L, with an adult mixing at the bucket |
| Three others | Occasional, guests | 15 L | Short winter showers (~3–5 min); guests may run out. 10 L saves ~₹1–2k but gives ~3 min even at 6 L/min, so it's rejected. Wi-Fi is +₹1,940 each if you hate waiting |

**A rain head defeats any tank.** At 12 L/min a 25 L lasts ~3.5 minutes. If you want long rain showers, the only fixes are a bigger tank (35 L+) or a hand shower. Neither needs plumbing work.

**Instant geysers can't run a shower.** A 3 kW instant heater raises ~2.0 L/min from 20 °C to 40 °C (4.5 kW: ~3.1 L/min). AO Smith's 3 kW "MiniBot" is a 3 L mini-tank, MRP ₹5,600. Fine at a basin, useless for a shower.

**Pressure works in your favour here.** Flow through a shower head scales with the square root of pressure. If floor 42 really gets ~1 bar (old §20 estimate, unverified), a head rated 9.5 L/min at 3 bar gives ~5.5 L/min. That stretches the tank. If a booster pump is added later, flow rises and tanks run out sooner. Measure the pressure before deciding on rain heads or a pump. The ~5.5 L/min figure is a square-root estimate, not a measurement.

### A11.2 Not waiting, load and scald safety

**How not to wait.**

| Method | Cost | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Built-in Wi-Fi schedule** (all four baths, 2 Oct) | 15 L: +₹1,940 over the non-Wi-Fi tank (₹13,899 vs ₹11,959) | Racold NET app: remote on/off, temperature setting, "schedule a shower for later", Alexa and Google voice. Racold says the app is "compatible with Omnis DG Wi-Fi and Omnis Slim Wi-Fi", so buy only those families. Stagger the four schedules 20 minutes apart so only one element heats at a time. **Single point of failure:** the schedule needs the app, Racold's cloud and Wi-Fi inside a tiled bathroom. Check signal at each geyser point. Whether the schedule runs on the device during an outage is unverified; only AO Smith HeatBot Wi-Fi has a confirmed on-device timer |
| Leave the geyser on Dec–Jan | ~₹100 a month per tank | AO Smith 25 L standby loss is 0.511 kWh/day at a 45 K difference, so ~0.35 kWh/day at 55 °C. Tariff assumed ₹8–10/kWh: unverified |
| 3 kW element | ~₹0–1,000 extra | Cuts a 25 L heat-up from 32 to 21 min. Needs a 16 A circuit: check the MCB. No Wi-Fi 3 kW model was found in India |
| **Not allowed:** a plug-in timer or smart plug on the geyser supply | n/a | AO Smith's manual: "this Water Heater must not be supplied through an external switching device, such as a timer". Racold's Omnis Slim manual has the same warning, citing "inadvertent resetting of the thermal cut-out". The Omnis DG manual was not read. Use the geyser's own scheduler |

**Load.** Four 2 kW geysers peak at 8 kW if all heat at once. The old note's 11 kW included a 3 kW kitchen MiniBot, now dropped. Scheduled, the realistic peak is the master plus one other: ~4 kW. B4206's sanctioned load is still unknown (§20 owns that question).

**Scald versus Legionella.**
- AO Smith's own manual: "Water temperatures over 52 °C can cause severe burns instantly". Every listed geyser goes to 72–75 °C if set there.
- WHO: Legionella grows at 20–50 °C, so hot water should leave the heater at ≥60 °C.
- **Setting:** 55 °C for adult showers. **Drop to 50 °C for Kabir's bath** (in the master, since he sleeps there), using Racold's "Children Care Mode" or the app. If you want the WHO margin, schedule one 60 °C cycle a week when nobody is bathing. Whether the Racold app combines a schedule with a temperature is unverified.
- This replaces the old thermostatic-mixer recommendation for a house whose mixers are already fitted.

Prices are Amazon.in on 2026-10-01, GST included. The brand installs free on the existing provision point in Hyderabad. Racold supplies pipes free; AO Smith says buy hoses separately (rated ≥8 bar).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| Racold Omnis DG 15L (4012024) | 15 | 2 | 7.5 (listing) | 7 / 4 / 2 yr | Touch display | 11,959 | [B0BZHL5YCW](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0BZHL5YCW) | No Wi-Fi, so no schedule. Dropped 2 Oct |
| **Racold Omnis DG Wi-Fi 15L** | 15 | 2 | 8 (manual) | 7 / 4 / 2 yr | Wi-Fi, NET app, Alexa, Google | **13,899** | [B0BZHJN4MW](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0BZHJN4MW) | **Pick for all four baths (2 Oct).** Titanium Plus enamel, BEE 5-star, Children Care Mode, free install and pipes.
