| AO Smith SGS-GREEN-015 | 15 | **3** | 8 | 7 / 2+1 / 2 yr | None | 11,799 | [B07RVVY5HS](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B07RVVY5HS) | 13 min from cold, but no schedule, and four 3 kW tanks at 7 AM risk the DG cap |
| **Racold Omnis Slim Wi-Fi 25L**, horizontal | 25 (25.6 real) | 2.5 | 8 (manual) | 7 / 7 / 2 yr (Racold page) | Wi-Fi, NET app, Alexa, Google | **15,807** | [B0DBQ949QW](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0DBQ949QW) | **Fallback if the geyser point sits above the false ceiling.** 678 W × 358 H mm (listing); manual table gives 678 / 358 / 358 mm, so depth is likely ~358 mm (unverified axis). 2.5 kW heats a 25 L from 20 to 55 °C in ~26 min. Manual: keep ~350–400 mm free on the left for servicing. Wi-Fi model image: [<img src="https://m.media-amazon.com/images/I/51S9mCzYwFL._SL300_.jpg" width="96">](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0DBQ949QW) |
| AO Smith SGS-GREEN-025 | 25 | **3** | 8 | 7 yr tank, 2 yr comprehensive | None | 13,099 | [B07RVVW31D](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B07RVVW31D) | **Value alternative for the master.** Fastest heat-up found; no schedule |
| AO Smith Elegance Prime 25L (VAS3252KWQ222) | 25 | 2 | 8 | 7 yr tank | None | 12,990 | [B0CMD3FQMP](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0CMD3FQMP) | The old pick. 25–75 °C dial, glass-lined, 0.511 kWh/day standby |
| AO Smith HSE-SHS-015 | 15 | 2 | 8 | 5 yr tank, 2 yr comprehensive | None | **7,299** | [B0C5MXF7GF](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0C5MXF7GF) | **Value alternative for the secondaries.** Shorter tank warranty |
| AO Smith SDS-GREEN-015 | 15 | 2 | 8 | 7 yr tank, 2+2 yr element | None | 10,649 | [B07RPLT5T6](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B07RPLT5T6) | Middle option |
| AO Smith HeatBot SZS-015-DG | 15 | 2 | 8 | 7 / 4 / 2 yr | Remote + schedule | 15,999 | [B07VDK3LGP](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B07VDK3LGP) | BEE 3-star only. Brand page lists a "HeatBot Wifi" at MRP ₹20,000 |
| Havells Adonia-i 25L (GHWCAITWH025) | 25 | 2 | 8 | n/a | Wi-Fi, app, Alexa | 16,490–17,850 | [B0CP9KK21P](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0CP9KK21P) | Credible Wi-Fi rival. Costs more than the Racold |

**Look around corners.**
- **8 bar is not a differentiator.** Every model above is rated 7.5–8 bar. A near-top flat sees ~1 bar, unless the tower has a booster system. Either way any of these is fine. Each ships with a safety valve. Never plug its drip: route it to the floor trap.
- **Racold's Omnis DG page gives no bar figure.** It says only "suitable for high-rise"; the 7.5 bar figure comes from the Amazon listing. The Omnis Slim manual states 8 bar maximum, a 7.5–8.5 bar safety valve and "approximately 1.5 bar" recommended at the inlet. Minimum inlet is 0.05 bar, so ~1 bar on floor 42 works.
- **Hard water.** Hyderabad bore water is hard. Ask the builder which line feeds the bathrooms. If it is bore water, have the anode and scale checked every 12 months. AO Smith's manual offers other anode options for highly conductive water.
- **Placement.** Mount at the existing point; don't move it, because that's electrical and plumbing work. If the point is below the false ceiling, wall-mount a vertical unit with room to pull the element. If the point is in the ceiling void, use the horizontal Omnis Slim (~358 mm tall, so check the void depth first). Homz must leave a removable access panel big enough to lift the unit out, ~750 × 450 mm (estimate). Racold's manual says that for units "above a false ceiling", the customer must dismount the product for service. Never seal a geyser into a ceiling: the anode, element and safety valve need access.
- **Bracket holes hit concealed pipes.** The hot and cold lines run vertically from the geyser point to the mixer. Scan each bracket hole with a pipe and cable detector, and use the builder's as-built drawings. Keep holes ≥150 mm off the vertical line through the point and the mixer (rule of thumb). A punctured pressurised line means breaking tile and waterproofing.
- **Register the warranty** on the day of install, with the invoice and installer's name. A 7-year tank warranty is worth nothing unregistered.
- **Don't buy a used geyser.** The tank's condition can't be seen.

### A11.4 Glass shower partitions

**The four bathrooms need glass and nothing else.** One rule for all four. Fit a walk-in panel only where the measured shower zone is **≥1.2 m deep and the floor trap sits inside it**. Otherwise fit a fixed panel plus a hinged door, or a panel with a 90° return flipper. In a 5'0"×7'11" bath a shower zone at one end is likely only ~0.9 m deep (estimate from the room size), so expect doors there too. Measure before ordering.

| Option | Image | Best for | Trade-off | Hyderabad price (indicative) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Fixed panel + hinged door** (default) | [<img src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Frameless_Glass_Doors.jpg/330px-Frameless_Glass_Doors.jpg" width="140">](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Frameless_Glass_Doors.jpg) | **Any bath whose shower zone is under 1.2 m deep.** Likely all four | Contains spray fully. Hinges and seals wear; door swing needs ~700 mm clear | ₹550–800/sqft listed; ~₹18–30k for a 5' bath, ~₹22–40k for the master, branded hardware, excl. GST (estimate) |
| Fixed panel + 90° return flipper | (same hardware family) | A shallow zone where a full door would hit the WC | A short hinged return closes the gap. Fewer seals than a door | Close to the door price (estimate) |
| **Fixed walk-in panel** (no door) | [<img src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/Dusche_ohne_Tuer_Walk_in_Dusche_Glasprofi24.jpg/330px-Dusche_ohne_Tuer_Walk_in_Dusche_Glasprofi24.jpg" width="140">](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Dusche_ohne_Tuer_Walk_in_Dusche_Glasprofi24.jpg) | **Only a zone ≥1.2 m deep with the trap inside** | No moving parts, no seals, no pinched fingers, cheapest, easiest to clean. In a shallow zone spray soaks the WC side | ₹380–650/sqft listed; ~₹10–15k per panel excl. GST |
| Sliding door (framed) | [<img src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Shower_Cubicle.jpg/330px-Shower_Cubicle.jpg" width="140">](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Shower_Cubicle.jpg) | Only where a hinged door would hit the WC | Rollers and bottom guides collect limescale and fail first. More parts. **Rejected as default** | ₹380–510/sqft listed |

Prices: IndiaMART Hyderabad listings, 2026-10-01, ₹350–1,800/sqft (median ~₹450). Listings rarely state GST; assume they exclude the 18%. Get three written quotes.

**Glass total for four baths:** three small at ~₹18–30k plus the master at ~₹22–40k is ~₹0.76–1.3 L excl. GST, or ~₹0.9–1.5 L with GST. With geysers (~₹52–54k incl. GST), §11's two Must buys come to ~₹1.4–2.1 L. Ask Homz in writing whether glass sits inside the ₹18 L quote.

**How each bathroom might lay out** (illustrative; confirm on site). The secondary sketch shows why a walk-in panel fails there: a ~0.9 m zone is shallower than the 1.2 m the rule needs.

```
Secondary bath, 5'0" x 7'11"           Master bath, 9'0" x 6'0"
+---------------------+                +-------------------------------+
|  SHOWER (~3' deep)  |                | SHOWER      |                 |
|      head on wall   |                | ~3'6" x 6'  |   WC    basin   |
|===========|door|    |  <- 900 mm     |             |                 |
|  fixed     600 mm   |     fixed      |  fixed |door|                 |
|  panel     door     |     panel      +--------+----+                 |
|   WC        basin   |                |                    door  ---> |
|        door         |                +-------------------------------+
+---------------------+
```

**Specification to put in the BOQ.**

| Item | Specify | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Glass | **10 mm clear toughened**, IS 2553 (Part 1) test certificate plus the processor's toughening stamp on each pane, polished edges | 10 mm stays rigid as a frameless panel. 8 mm is fine only for a framed or fully clamped door. Toughened glass breaks into small cubes, not shards |
| Heat-soak | **Ask for heat-soaked glass (EN 14179)**; accept unsoaked only with a written replacement promise | Nickel-sulphide inclusions make toughened glass shatter on its own, sometimes years later. Heat-soaking screens most of them out. India availability and price premium unverified |
| Weight check | 10 mm glass weighs 25 kg/m². A 700 × 2,000 mm door is ~35 kg | Choose hinges rated above the door weight |
| Hardware | **SS304 or brass-bodied**, with a mill test certificate. Brands: Ozone, Dorma (dormakaba), Hafele | Indian listings sell SS202 hinges side by side with SS304 at similar prices. SS202 rusts in a wet room |
| Door swing | **Opens outward, or a 180° pivot hinge that swings both ways** | Someone who falls inside can be reached, and drips land inside the shower. Check it clears the WC and the bathroom door |
| Stabiliser bar | **Wall-to-glass or wall-to-wall only, never into the false ceiling** | A builder or Homz false ceiling can't carry the sideways load |
| Seals | Clear PVC bottom wiper and magnetic or bubble side seals on any door | Stops the drip line onto the floor |
| Water bar | **Bonded with neutral-cure silicone**, not screwed | Needed if the shower floor is level with the rest. Keeps water inside without a floor hole |
| Finish | Clear for a small bath (looks bigger). Fluted or frosted hides hard-water spots | Clear glass shows every spot from bore water |
| Coating | Ask for a factory hydrophobic coating if offered | No India-stocked brand was confirmed (EnduroShield lists no India channel). DIY sprays exist on Amazon.in but are unproven |

**Fixing without breaching the builder's waterproofing.** The bathroom floor membrane is the one you can't repair without lifting tile. Drilling it gives the builder a causation defence under RERA §14(3), which obliges the promoter to fix defects for five years from possession.
1. **Find the pipes first.** The walls carry concealed CPVC/PPR hot and cold lines and the mixer body. Get the builder's plumbing and electrical as-built drawings. Have the vendor scan each hole position with a pipe and cable detector. Keep holes ≥150 mm off the vertical line above or below any mixer, spout or geyser point (rule of thumb). A punctured line means breaking tile and waterproofing, the work the owner has ruled out.
2. **No floor holes.** No floor pivots, no screwed floor channels. Bottom profile or water bar is bonded with silicone.
3. **Walls only.** Wall profile or wall hinges, and a wall-to-glass or wall-to-wall stabiliser bar for the sideways load. Never fix the bar into the false ceiling.
4. **Wet-set every wall hole.** Drill through the tile with a tile bit, fill the hole with silicone or PU sealant, then insert the SS plug and screw. Water then can't track along the screw.
5. **Photograph every hole** and get the vendor to warrant its own penetrations in writing.
6. **Ask the builder in writing** whether wall fixings in the shower zone affect the waterproofing warranty. Some builders keep a list of approved glass vendors. If they do, the simplest path is to use one.
7. Let the silicone cure 24 hours before the first shower.

**Who installs.** Glass is a specialist trade. Homz would subcontract it anyway. Two clean options:
- **Inside Homz's ₹18 lakh scope**, with the spec above as a BOQ line and the glass vendor named. One contract, one snag list. Confirm in writing that the quote includes it.
- **Direct from a Hyderabad glass vendor**, priced per sqft. Usually cheaper (no turnkey margin) and the vendor's hardware warranty is direct.
Get one direct quote either way, so you can judge Homz's line. Install glass last, after Homz's dusty work, to avoid scratches.

**Upkeep with hard water.** Squeegee after every shower (~₹349, e.g. [AIR O Matic](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B08FTGCPRK)). Wipe weekly with diluted white vinegar. Never use scouring powder or a steel scrubber on glass or hinges.

**A toughened panel that fails looks like this**: the whole pane drops as small cubes. That's the safe failure mode, and the reason to insist on the IS 2553-1 certificate and toughening stamp.

[<img src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cd/2024-02-14_21_57_40_A_shattered_glass_shower_door_inside_a_house_in_Ewing_Township%2C_Mercer_County%2C_New_Jersey.jpg/330px-2024-02-14_21_57_40_A_shattered_glass_shower_door_inside_a_house_in_Ewing_Township%2C_Mercer_County%2C_New_Jersey.jpg" width="160">](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:2024-02-14_21_57_40_A_shattered_glass_shower_door_inside_a_house_in_Ewing_Township,_Mercer_County,_New_Jersey.jpg)

### A11.5 Exhausts and ceiling fans

**Exhausts: buy only what's missing.** The builder probably fitted exhausts or at least cutouts. All four toilets sit on an outside wall on the plan, so each likely has a ventilator. Check each one for a ventilator or exhaust cutout.
- A 5'×7'11" bath with a false ceiling at ~8'6" holds ~9.5 m³. 6–8 air changes an hour needs 57–76 m³/h delivered.
- **atomberg Studio 150 mm BLDC**: 250 m³/h free air (listing), 6.8 W, needs a 153 mm round cutout, ₹1,799. Ducted delivery will be well below the free-air figure. Whether it still clears 76 m³/h is unverified, and several Amazon reviews report weak suction. Test it with a tissue at the grille after fitting.
- **atomberg Efficio 200 mm**: 650 m³/h free air, 20 W, needs a 245 mm square cutout, ₹1,649. Oversized for a bathroom; use it only to fill an existing 245 mm cutout.
- **Rules:** match the existing cutout so nobody cuts tile. Confirm it ducts to the building shaft, not into the void above the false ceiling. Run it 20–30 minutes after a shower. An electrician swaps a fan on an existing point in under an hour.

**BLDC ceiling fans: Optional.** VRF already cools every room. A fan earns its place in Feb–Mar and Oct–Nov, when the VRF is off and windows are open.
- **atomberg Renesa Elite Smart 1200 mm**, Alexa support, 5-year warranty, ₹4,999 ([B0DV5CT8NH](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0DV5CT8NH)). Base "Renesa" and "Studio" fans are remote-only; check the listing says Alexa.
- **Fit only where the slab already has a fan hook and a wired fan point.** Tell Homz before the false ceiling closes. The ceiling must be cut around the hook, and the downrod sized to the finished ceiling height. A Homz ceiling lowers the room, so the old "12–18 in downrod for 10 ft" advice no longer holds. Measure the finished height.
- If there's no hook, skip the fan. Adding one means drilling the slab and running a new circuit.

### A11.6 Insect mesh

**First, count the rails.** The builder's spec says the balcony sliders have a "mosquito mesh track provision". Open both glass panels and count the raised rails in the empty sill. Three rails, or a shallow half-height third rail ("2.5-track"), take a mesh sash at no fixing cost. Two rails take none. Don't judge by frame depth; it misleads. Photograph every sill.

| Opening | System |
|---|---|
| Balcony slider, 3-track or 2.5-track | **Mesh sash in the spare rail.** Cheapest, wind-proof, washable |
| Balcony slider, true 2-track | Fixed or hinged framed screen in the reveal. A clip-on outer track is the alternative |
| Casement or tilt-turn window | Framed screen on the **outside** face, magnet or clip mounted, removable |
| Sliding window | Sash in its own track if 3-track, else an outside framed screen |
| Teak main door | Nothing. The internal corridor brings no insects |

**Aperture decides exclusion or reduction.** Standard 18×16 fibreglass has a 1.13 × 1.31 mm hole (pitch 1.41 × 1.59 mm minus ~0.28 mm yarn). The old note put a female *Aedes aegypti* thorax at 0.96–1.03 mm, citing Gunathilaka et al., BioMed Res Int 2019. The paper's abstract confirms it measured thorax width; the figure itself is not re-checked. So standard mesh only reduces mosquitoes. **Specify a hole ≤0.9 mm in writing.** A finer mesh cuts airflow by roughly half, which matters only with windows open.

**Material.** Stainless wire gives a small hole with more open area than fibreglass. Use **SS316 for mesh sitting in a never-rain-washed track**; SS304 is fine for an exposed frame. Get a mill test certificate: Indian sellers list SS201 and SS202 mesh at the same price. Avoid nylon; it embrittles in two Hyderabad summers. Buy Phifer fabric through [Phifer India](https://phiferindia.com/) (Chennai subsidiary), not a marketplace listing that uses the name.

**Cost** (Mr Mosquito, Hyderabad, May 2026 guide, GST included):

| System | ₹/sqft | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Fixed frame | 80–140 | Fibreglass. **SS304 adds 80–120/sqft** |
| Roller | 120–200 | |
| Pleated | 150–250 | Rejected below |
| Zip-track balcony | 380–550 | ₹22–35k per balcony |
| Sliding sash in an existing track | ~170–350 | Old-note rate; not in the 2026 guide. Unverified |

On a 34 sqft balcony opening a sash is ~₹5,800–11,900. A 4'×5' window with framed SS304 is ~₹3,200–5,200. Whole-flat total depends on the opening count: ~₹45–80k is an estimate.

**Rejected: pleated (plissé) screens.** The fabric can't be had in stainless or heavy pet-grade polyester. Isotra's own manual describes the failure chain: dust wears the rail, the cord ruptures and the system is scrapped. You can't remove the pack to wash it, and floor-42 wind lifts it from its shallow bottom track.

**Layer order in each opening, inside to outside:** child-safety grille, then glass, then mesh. Blinds sit inboard in the pelmet. Never put a mesh between the grille and the glass: you can't reach it to clean. Draw one section through a typical opening before ordering; three trades each assume they own the reveal.

**Cleaning is the main upkeep.** Dirt clogs more airflow than UV ageing, and it's reversible. Vacuum fabric mesh dry before wetting it; rinse metal mesh with water only. Monthly in the Mar–Jun and Oct–Nov dust, deep clean before the monsoon. No ammonia, solvents or hard scrubbing.

### A11.7 The utility: do not enclose it

**Leave the east utility open.** Three reasons, worst first.
1. **The building permission rests on an undertaking not to enclose balconies.** Telangana G.O.Ms.No.168 (2012) checklist item 26 requires a joint undertaking "not to enclose balconies". Appendix-VII binds successors and tenants. Rule 26(d) allows compounding only for non-high-rise buildings, so there is no regularisation route for a 44-storey tower. Ask the builder for Tower B's filed undertaking.
2. **A sealed utility can't absorb one wash.** At ~9 m³ and 30 °C, saturated air holds ~0.27 L of water. A 7 kg load leaves the spin carrying ~3.5 L. That's a ~13× deficit, so it condenses on walls and cabinets ([A27](#a27-assumptions-and-derivations)).
3. **It becomes a sun-heated box** on an east face, and deletes the kitchen's best make-up-air path.

**Washer check.** The drain hose goes into the builder's trapped standpipe at the maker's hose height, never loose into the floor drain (sewer gas, back-siphon). Put anti-vibration pads under the machine (~₹500–1,500, unverified) to protect the flat below.

**If driving rain on the machines is the problem**, glaze partially: fixed glazing over the lower part, with a permanently open band or top-hung vent above. That likely keeps it a balcony; confirm with the builder or GHMC (unverified). Rough cost ₹22–34k installed with GST (old-note figure, 2026-09; not re-quoted). Use 6 mm toughened glass and no anchors into the balcony floor slab. Bid list: Aparna Venster, VEKA, Prominance. If you ever enclose it fully, the old note's minimum spec still applies: EN 12210 wind test report, ≥110 m³/h ducted extract plus a permanent inlet, and a structural engineer's anchor letter. That route is not recommended.

| Old item | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Thermostatic anti-scald mixers, ₹0.8–3.6 L | **Dropped** | The fitted mixers are almost certainly concealed bodies; replacing them means chasing finished tile. Use the geyser thermostat instead (A11.2). If a bath has an **exposed** wall mixer on two 150 mm-centre inlets, an exposed thermostat swaps in with one plumber visit (Hansgrohe Ecostat exposed ~₹33k on Amazon.in): Optional |
| "The cold line arrives scalding" | **Corrected** | A roof tank in May reaches roughly 35–40 °C (estimate). Warm, not a scald. The scald risk is the hot line |
| Sanitaryware split, Geberit cistern | Dropped | Builder fitted TOTO/Jaquar/Cera |
| Anti-skid re-tiling, DIN R-ratings | Dropped | Bathrooms tiled with anti-skid ceramic |
| Paint (Royale Aspira etc.) | Dropped | No painting in scope |
| RCBO per wet circuit | Dropped from §11 | Electrical work is out of scope; §20 can raise it if wanted |
| Kitchen MiniBot 3 kW | Dropped | Needs a hot line at the sink and a new point. 3 L only |
| Utility uPVC minimum-spec table, wind-class essay | Cut to two lines | Not recommended route |
| Mesh wind-certificate essay | Cut | The conclusion stands: choose captive or framed geometry |
| Mini-UPS for router and hubs (DG changeover gap 10–30 s) | **Moved to §20 / network** | Not a bathroom item. Flagged to the orchestrator to confirm an owner |
| Mattresses, drapery, art framing, acoustic panels, plants | **Moved to soft furnishings and decor** | Flagged to the orchestrator |
| Kid-safe pest control before move-in, bins | **Moved to move-in** | Flagged to the orchestrator |
| Home safe, door hardware, wardrobe internals | **Moved to Homz BOQ / storage** | Flagged to the orchestrator |
| Dehumidifier, VRF noise | **Moved to §20 / climate** | Flagged to the orchestrator |
| RO reject water | **Moved to kitchen / water** | Flagged to the orchestrator |
| Parapet height, EV charging | **Moved to safety / building** | Flagged to the orchestrator |
| Anti-fog LED mirror with a heated demister pad (₹1.5–45k) | **Demoted to a check** | Builders often skip mirrors. If none is fitted, add one as a Homz BOQ line. A true demister pad needs a wired point at the mirror: buy it only where one exists |
| Health faucet, TOTO Washlet | **Dropped** | Health faucets usually come with the CP fittings: confirm each bath has one. A Washlet needs a new RCD point: electrical work, out of scope |
| Towel rails, robe hooks, heated towel rail | **Demoted to a check** | Confirm the builder fitted them. If not, plain SS304 rails go in the Homz BOQ. A heated rail needs a new point: skip |
| Raised threshold at the master bath door | **Kept as a check** | The master has laminate; a shower-water track ruins planks. Check the builder left a step or saddle; if not, Homz fits a marble or SS saddle without touching the bath's waterproofing |
| Washer standpipe and anti-vibration pads | **Cut to one check in A11.7** | Existing plumbing; no new work |
| Ceiling pulley drying rack in the utility (~₹3–9k, old estimate) | **Moved to storage / utility** | A12 covers the utility's competing uses |
| Bathroom door locks | **Kept as a check** | NBC 2016 Part 4 cl 6.1.1.1(b) wants bathroom locks openable from outside in an emergency without a special key (old-note quote, not re-checked). Check the builder's locks; swap any that fail |

### P1. Contract Rider — Homz Interio engagement

**Why a rider.** The ₹18L turnkey has wrong board-grade incentives and missing smart infrastructure. A rider locks: board grade, hardware brand, smart wiring, and a fixed-price add-on schedule.

#### Rider structure — attach to LOI and BOQ; both parties sign; stamp 2 %.

| Clause | What Homz supplies | Owner supplies | Penalty if missed |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 — Board grade | **Century Club Prime** 19 mm BWP everywhere: kitchen, shutters, utility, wet-zone. Every sheet carries ISI + Century QR | None | ₹15,000 per cabinet line; Homz replaces |
| 2 — Hardware | **Blum or Hettich** every hinge, drawer channel, corner mechanism | None | Generic on site → replace within 10 days; ₹8,000 per line |
| 3 — Smart wiring | Cat6: Living→AP-1, Dining→AP-2, BR-01 desk→AP-2 edge; 36 mm pull-wire to every switch/fan; 16 A socket at TV; 50 mm PVC duct | None | ₹5,000 per missing line; Homz runs at no cost |
| 4 — Change orders | Written change-order within 24 h | Owner approves in writing before work. No verbal | Work without approval = unpaid |
| 5 — Cure rule | 7 days from written notice to correct any spec deviation | WhatsApp OK | After 7 days: owner pays nothing for item + claims penalties |
| 6 — Payments | 30-25-25-15-5 tranche schedule (5 % held till snag-clearance) | Site photo walk-through before every tranche | No photo = no payment |
| 7 — Warranties | Carpentry 5 yr; Hardware per mfr; Edge-banding 3 yr | Appliances/electronics: owner | Homz responds within 48 h to claims |
| 8 — Insurance | CAR policy on file during construction | None | No CAR page before final payment |
| 9 — Snag list | Owner walks through day 3 of completion; Homz closes every line in 14 days | Owner visits site with photos | Unclosed snags → 5 % hold-back forfeit pro-rata |
| 10 — Delivery | Board stacks on dunnage (no bare tile); Cat6 Belden only, no bare-copper | None | Substandard → full line penalty |
| 11 — MEP wiring | 2.5/4/6 mm Finolex/Polycab; stiff conduit; owner written approval for changes | None | Non-ISI wire → replace + ₹10,000 per circuit |

#### Rider add-on schedule — items not in the ₹18L base.

| Item | Description | Fixed price (GST) | In BOQ? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Smart switch wiring kit | 10 × MINIR4M ports; 1 × Aqara Hub M2; pull-wires | Included in clause 3 | No |
| Cat6 runs | Belden 8290E × ~100 m (3 runs × 30–40 m). Owner buys; Homz routes | ~₹1L total | Owner purchase |
| TV 16 A socket | Behind media unit; new circuit from DB | ₹2,500 | Yes |
| Ply backing + duct | 18 mm BWP ply, 50 mm PVC duct at TV height | ₹1,600 | Yes |
| Mini-UPS | APC Back-UPS 650VA; 10–30 s bridge over DG | ₹6,500 | Owner purchase |
| Smart lighting add-on | 15 × Sonoff ZBMINI-L; Homz tags wiring | ₹4,500 | Owner purchase |

#### Homz engagement checklist — before Homz starts.

| # | Action | Who | When |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Sign the rider; stamp both copies | Both | Before site visit |
| 2 | Homz delivers signed BOQ with all clause 3 items priced | Homz | Before material order |
| 3 | Homz uploads CAR insurance page to shared folder | Homz | Before final payment |
| 4 | Homz walks through with tape measure; notes every dimension on spec | Homz | Before carcass start |
| 5 | Homz posts photos of every board delivery (ISI marks visible) | Homz | At delivery |
| 6 | Owner walks through before ceiling board closes | Owner | After wiring, before board |
| 7 | Homz closes every snag line | Homz | Within 14 days |
| 8 | Owner releases final 5 % | Owner | After snag clearance |
| 9 | Homz hands over warranties, certificates, QR codes | Homz | At handover |
| 10 | Homz provides AsBuilt wiring diagram (photos of every switch + pull-wire) | Homz | At handover |

#### Top rider failure modes.

| Risk | Probability | Impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Homz swaps Club Prime to cheaper BWR | Med | Water damage in wet zones | Clause 1: QR code at every delivery |
| Generic hardware on Blum/Hettich lines | Med | Squeezing, early wear | Clause 2: replace within 10 days |
| Wiring after ceiling boards (no pull-wires) | High | Can't add smart switches | Clause 3: owner present before board |
| Verbal change orders → cost blow-up | High | ₹50k+ overruns | Clause 4: written every change |
| 5 % hold-back lost | Med | No snag leverage | Clause 6: tranche schedule |
| Owner pays 80 % before hardware | High | Can't insist on Blum | Clause 6: 30-25-25-15-5 |

**Decision: sign the rider and base BOQ together, or ask for a revised base BOQ before signing?**

**Recommendation: sign both in the same meeting.** The base BOQ captures 90% of the work. The rider locks the remaining 10% (board grade, hardware, smart wiring) at zero-cost change orders. If Homz refuses the rider, do not sign.

**Skip:** soil termite treatment. **Detail →** [A12](#a12-joinery-storage-and-materials-spec--detail)

**Execution history, two lines.** The old note scored Homz Interio 2.45/10 and recommended a designer-plus-contractor route (III Studio and others). Homz is now contracted at ₹18 lakh including tax, so that analysis is obsolete. Its findings survive only as the contract asks in A12.1.

### A12.0 Spec sheet to hand Homz

"Or equivalent" needs your written approval. Attach this sheet to the Homz agreement as the materials annexure.

| Item | Write into the BOQ | Proof at delivery |

| Carcass, all rooms | 19 mm IS 303 BWR grade (stamped BWR or BWP), product named | ISI stamp; licence checked in the BIS Care app |
| Backs | 9 mm, same grade and emission class | ISI stamp |
| Shutters | 18–19 mm BWP ply or HDHMR (Action Tesa or named equal), laminated both faces | Sample shutter |
| Emission | E0 or E1 (EN 717-1), or CARB Phase 2 / TSCA VI | Test report naming the product |
| Plinth and sink base | PVC or aluminium plinth on legs; liner tray under the sink | Sample |
| Edge banding | 2 mm ABS/PVC visible, 0.8 mm hidden, machine-applied | Sample corner |
| Hardware | Brand and article number per line; 3 hinges per door over 900 mm, 4 over 1,600 mm | Sealed cartons, dealer invoice |
| Finishes | 1 mm anti-fingerprint matt laminate, code named; no membrane, acrylic or pale PU | Signed 200×200 mm sample |
| Wet walls | Laminate back and 25 mm air gap on the BR-02 east wardrobe and BR-03 almirah | Site check |
| False ceiling | Gyproc on GI, extent per room, hatches at every VRF unit, driver and junction | Signed ceiling plan |
| Paperwork | Invoices and warranties in your name; Club Prime Total Cover registered within 90 days | Copies before final payment |

### A12.1 Turn the old Homz risks into contract asks

The old audit read four risks off Homz's own published terms. Each one becomes a spec-side ask. §7 owns the full contract; these are the parts that touch materials.

| Old risk (Homz terms, read 2026-09-27) | Ask | Check |
|---|---|---|
| The materials page names no brands, no thicknesses and no emission class | Attach the §12 spec sheet as a signed annexure. Every BOQ line names brand, product, thickness and article number. "Or equivalent" needs your written approval | Compare the delivered labels and invoices with the annexure before each milestone payment |
| Homz is "deemed the author and owner of the Designs" | Ask for the final working drawings (plans, elevations, BOQ) as PDFs, licensed to you for repair and future changes | Drawings received before the final payment |
| The warranty states no duration and replaces manufacturing defects only, against badges reading "10/25/30* years" | A per-component warranty schedule in the contract. Plus manufacturers' warranties in your name: Blum 15 years, Century per product | Invoices and warranty cards in your name. If you take Club Prime, register its Total Cover within 90 days (only the Club Prime page documents this) |
| Substitution risk | Signed 200 × 200 mm sample of every laminate code and the board, kept on site; signed renders binding (the Sep 2026 Hyderabad consumer-commission case in old A7) | Spot-check shutters against the samples at delivery |
| Nobody has seen what the ₹18L assumes. Homz's materials page lists "MR 303" as a carcass option, and turnkey kitchen lines often assume a new granite top | Ask Homz, in writing, which board grade, hardware brand, corner fittings and countertop the ₹18L includes, and whether its kitchen line keeps the builder platform. Treat BWP everywhere, Blum or Hettich, and Club Prime as possible change orders (§7 estimates ~₹1–2L, unverified) | Every "confirm in quote" line in §12 answered before signing |
| No civil, plumbing or electrical scope | Mostly moot now: the flat needs none. Name who does the small jobs that remain (the RO feed on an existing angle valve, ceiling lights and smart switches on existing circuits). No rewiring. A missing 16 A point is added by Homz's electrician, priced in the BOQ (A9.2) | Each named in the BOQ, or listed as excluded |

### A12.2 Board: what the standards actually say

| Standard or label | What it covers | What it doesn't | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **IS 303** (plywood for general purposes) | Two bond grades in clause 4.1: **BWR** (boiling water resistant) and **MR** (moisture resistant). Mills and dealers usually say "BWP", and sheets may be stamped either way. Amendment 1 (Nov 1992) changed "BWR" to "BWP" in one Foreword line only; the body and the later amendments still say BWR | No formaldehyde limit, even after Amendment 2's optional Eco Mark criteria. The copy read here runs to Amendment 6 (Aug 2007); a later BIS revision wasn't checked | [IS 303:1989 with amendments](https://law.resource.org/pub/in/bis/S03/is.303.1989.pdf) |
| **IS 710** (marine plywood) | For "marine and river craft, pontoons and the like". Named face species only, no gaps over 0.5 mm, pressure preservative treatment, a 72-hour boiling test | No emission limit. More than a kitchen needs | [IS 710:2010](https://law.resource.org/pub/in/bis/S03/is.710.2010.pdf) |
| **"Sainik 710"** (Century trademark) | Century's own disclaimer on the product page: "Asli" means BWP quality "in compliance with IS 303 BWP (General Purpose Plywood)". ₹122 per unit (929 sq cm, ~1 sqft) including GST; the page doesn't say which thickness the price is for. Stocked in 4–25 mm including 19 mm. "Borer and termite proof" with a 10-year warranty | It isn't IS 710 marine. Century's plywood hub page contradicts this by calling Sainik 710 "IS 710 certified", so trust the product-page disclaimer | [Sainik 710](https://www.centuryply.com/plywood/sainik-710) |
| **Century Club Prime** | "Premium BWP marine-grade", "ISI 710 compliant", "E0 emission compliant", fire retardant to IS 5509, 30-year warranty, first 10 years under Total Cover (register within 90 days). ₹171 per unit including GST, stated for 19 mm: ~40% more than Sainik 710's ₹122 | No termite or borer claim on the page. No emission figure or test standard | [Club Prime](https://www.centuryply.com/plywood/club-prime) |
| **Century Sainik MR** | Commercial MR ply, "borer and termite proof", 5-year warranty. Stocked in 18 mm, not 19 | No emission class or price stated | [Sainik MR](https://www.centuryply.com/plywood/sainik-mr) |
| **HDHMR** (e.g. Action Tesa) | A dense fibreboard ("over 850 kg/m3") sold for shutters. Flatter than ply, so tall shutters warp less. Commonly sold at 18 mm in India (trade practice; the Action Tesa page lists no thicknesses, so unverified) | A product category, not an IS grade. No emission claim on the page | [Action Tesa](https://www.actiontesa.com/products/plain-hdhmr-boards/) |
| Homz's materials page | Offers "MR 303", "BWP ISI 710" and "HDHMR" shutters | Says "ISI 710 certification guarantees exceptional water resistance". It conflates BWP with marine, so name the product, not the number | [Homz materials](https://homzinterio.in/materials/) |

**Spec per zone.**

**One board for the whole flat.** It's the simplest rule, and nothing can be mixed up on site. MR would save money in the bedrooms, but it brings a second grade, an 18 mm thickness and a wet-wall exception to police. The MR saving is unverified, because Century publishes no Sainik MR price.

| Zone | Board | Why |
|---|---|---|
| All carcasses: kitchen, utility, wardrobes, TV unit, storage | 19 mm IS 303 BWR grade (stamped BWR or BWP): Century Sainik 710. Upgrade: Club Prime | Spills and steam in the kitchen; condensation and leaks on wet walls elsewhere. IS 710 marine also passes but adds nothing a home needs |
| Shutters | 18–19 mm BWP ply or HDHMR (Action Tesa or a named equal), laminated both faces | HDHMR stays flatter on tall doors. Allow 18 mm so a 19 mm-only spec doesn't give Homz a reason to substitute |
| Back panels | 9 mm, same grade and emission class as the carcass | Thin backs bow; backs off-gas too |
| Bathroom storage, if added | BWP or PVC/WPC board | Bathrooms are finished, so only for add-ons |

**Sainik 710 or Club Prime?** Sainik 710 states no emission class. Club Prime claims E0 but names no test standard or figure, so its claim is marketing too. Ask Homz for a test report naming the product either way. Club Prime costs ~40% more per unit (₹171 at 19 mm against ₹122; Century doesn't say which thickness the ₹122 is for). Ask Homz for that delta on the whole-flat sheet count. Club Prime's real gains are the 30-year warranty and the fire-retardant claim, not proven lower emissions.

**Check the ISI stamp.** Fake ISI marks on plywood are a known problem (practitioner reports, unverified). The BIS Care app has a "Verify Licence Details" function for checking a product's ISI licence ([BIS Care](https://www.bis.gov.in/bis-care-app/)). Enter the licence number printed with the stamp (the CM/L format is unverified) and check it names the right mill and IS 303.

**Emission class: the line that protects Kabir.** Neither IS 303 nor IS 710 sets a formaldehyde limit. Name one of these and ask for the mill's test report naming the product:

| Label | Limit | Test basis |
|---|---|---|
| CARB Phase 2 = US TSCA Title VI | Hardwood plywood 0.05 ppm; MDF 0.11; thin MDF 0.13; particleboard 0.09 | ASTM E1333 large chamber ([40 CFR 770.10](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/40/770.10)) |
| E0 (as Century uses it) | ≤ 0.5 mg/L | Desiccator method (Century's page; the test standard isn't named) |
| E1 | ≤ 0.1 ppm, i.e. ~0.124 mg/m³ | EN 717-1 chamber (European convention; not re-verified in this pass) |

A brand's "E0" claim without a test report is marketing. Ask for the report.

**Cure before anyone sleeps here.** New board, laminate glue and edge-band adhesive off-gas most in the first weeks. Keep the windows open and the fans running for 2 weeks after installation, 4 if the flat still smells (the old note's rule; no primary study checked). Two events wait for that: move-in, and the Saroop's arrival in BR-03 (§7.3C). Run the purifiers (§5) on high during the cure.

### A12.3 Edge banding, plinth and sink base

- 2 mm ABS or PVC on every visible edge; 0.8 mm on hidden edges.
- Machine-applied hot-melt at the factory. Ask whether Homz's Bengaluru factory runs PUR (polyurethane) glue. It resists water better on sink-base edges. If it doesn't, EVA hot-melt is acceptable.
- No hand-applied edge tape with contact glue in the kitchen.
- Check one sample corner: no gap, no glue line, radiused edge.
- **Kitchen plinth:** PVC or aluminium kick-board on adjustable plastic legs. No ply plinth: daily wet mopping rots it within a few years (practitioner view).
- **Sink base:** an aluminium or PVC liner tray on the cabinet floor under the drain and the RO. It catches slow leaks before the board swells.

### A12.4 Hardware: brands, article numbers, fakes

**Rule:** each BOQ line names the brand and the catalogue article number. Hardware arrives in sealed brand cartons, and Homz gives you a copy of the authorised distributor's invoice.

**Why the invoice matters.** Blum India's warranty statement covers all products sold after 1 Apr 2022 for 15 years (Servo-Drive 3). It says Blum products reach India "through Blum authorized distributors only", and that the warranty "is only valid if the original sales receipt is provided". It excludes rust and improper installation. So a Blum hinge with no authorised receipt is, in effect, unwarranted. Amazon.in shows the grey channel: the Hettich Sensys 8645i listing (B07KMCTYLX) carries a "Visit the Hettich Store" link but ships from and is sold by a third party, STYLE WITH VISION. Don't use Amazon prices as proof of an authorised channel. Blum lists a Hyderabad experience centre: **EN Vision Solutions, Sudarshan Nagar Colony, Serilingampally** (from Blum's distributor page). Use it to check a quote or see samples.

| Item | Pick | Value | Spec note | ₹ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hinges | Blum CLIP top BLUMOTION 110°, full overlay, 71B3550 plus its mounting plate | Hettich Sensys 8645i, eShop article [9071205](https://shop.hettich.com/in_EN/p/9071205) | Integrated soft-close. 3 per door over 900 mm tall, 4 over 1,600 mm (rule of thumb; follow the maker's chart for heavy doors) | ~₹500–650 per hinge (Amazon.in third-party listings; not an authorised price) |
| Drawers | Blum MERIVOBOX or LEGRABOX (Blum: 40 kg and 70 kg classes) | Hettich InnoTech Atira or ArciTech; Blum TANDEMBOX (30 and 65 kg) | 70 kg class on pot and pan drawers. Drawers, not doors, on base units | ~ ask Homz (unverified) |
| Lift-ups | Blum AVENTOS HK top ("stays in any position" per Blum) or HK-XS for small lofts | Side-hung doors | Reject gas-strut "hydraulic" lift-ups: no stop-anywhere claim | ~ ask Homz (unverified) |
| Inner dividers | Blum AMBIA-LINE / ORGA-LINE for spice and cutlery | Ebco inserts | Use drawer inserts, not door-mounted wire racks, which rust in humid months | — |
| U corners (two) | A branded pull-out corner with the article number named: Kessebohmer LeMans type (the Homz materials page shows one under "Blind corner"), or the Hettich or Häfele equivalent Homz stocks | A blind-corner fixed shelf behind a door (₹0 extra) | The costliest and most-substituted joinery line. Generic "magic corner" units on Amazon.in (e.g. DBR B08XMKMN1V, ₹12,999) need a 900–1,050 mm-wide, 500 mm-deep bay. Under a builder top, a brick support often closes the corner: measure the clear opening first | ~₹10k–35k per corner (generic units ₹8.5k–15.5k on Amazon.in; branded price unverified) |
| Sink-base bins | Two-bin pull-out (dry and wet) on full-extension soft-close runners, e.g. DBR 40 L dual, 400 W × 500 D × 450 H mm ([B0FPR6793S](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0FPR6793S)) | Two loose bins on a liner tray | India's Solid Waste Management Rules 2016 ask households to segregate wet and dry waste (from memory; not re-read in this pass). The bins must clear the trap and the RO unit. Blum's sink-unit page shows the drawer version: a U-shaped LEGRABOX or TANDEMBOX pull-out round the sink, recommended for cabinets 900 mm or wider | 6,555 (Amazon.in, sold by DBR Kitchens) |
| Wardrobe fittings | Häfele or Ebco pull-out trouser rack and valet rod; Hettich cam lock for one drawer | Ebco | Spec them as line items, or you get a plain-shelf carcass | ~₹10–20k per wardrobe (unverified) |

**Counterfeit checks you can do yourself:** the brand logo is moulded into the hinge cup and the arm; there's an article number on the carton; the invoice comes from a distributor on Blum's list. Hettich's own authenticity scheme couldn't be verified, so buy through Hettich's eShop (shop.hettich.com/in_EN) or an authorised Hettich dealer, and check the seller name on any marketplace listing. The Ebco hologram claim is unverified.

**Where the money goes.** Blum or Hettich on kitchen drawers and lift-ups (daily, heavy use). Soft-close Hettich or Ebco is fine for wardrobe hinges. The upgrade over a no-name hardware pack is ~₹40k–90k for the flat (an estimate from roughly 100 hinges, 12 drawers and 4 lift-ups; unverified).

### A12.5 Finishes

| Finish | Where | Pick | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Anti-fingerprint ultra-matt laminate, 1 mm** | Kitchen and wardrobe shutters | Greenlam AFX ("ultra-matt… anti-fingerprint", electron-beam cured); Century Anti-Fingerprint Plus; Merino Feather Touch (anti-fingerprint claim unverified) | **Pick.** Name the code per shutter run |
| Light laminate, 0.8 mm | Carcass insides | Any of the three brands | Pick: easier to clean than a paper liner |
| Natural veneer plus matt PU | TV unit, foyer console | Oak veneer; Asian Paints Emporio PU or WoodTech PU Palette (acrylic-based opaque PU per Asian Paints) | Optional. Ask for a non-yellowing (aliphatic) hardener in writing |
| PU lacquer, pale colours | — | — | Only with an aliphatic hardener. Aromatic isocyanates yellow in light, which is why coatings use aliphatic ones |
| Acrylic (high gloss) | — | — | Skip: shows fingerprints, scratches, wrong for a matte brief |
| Membrane (PVC foil) | — | Offered by Homz | Skip: the foil can lift near heat and steam |
| Glass shutters | — | Offered by Homz | Skip in a home with a 6-year-old |

**Corrections:** the old note named "Century Sainik Suede" (not found; Century's anti-fingerprint line is Anti-Fingerprint Plus) and "Asian Paints Duco + UV inhibitor" as the PU. Duco is a lacquer trade name, not PU, and Asian Paints' wood-finish page lists Emporio PU and WoodTech PU Palette. The old line "PU yellows in 3–5 years on warm-white" holds only for aromatic-hardener PU. Rubio Monocoat's India distribution wasn't verified, so it's dropped.

### A12.6 Handles, child safety and reach

- Aluminium J-profile on base units and lift-ups on wall units. Name the profile finish (anodised champagne or black). Push-to-open on drawers next to the hob only invites accidental opening, so avoid it there.
- Kabir is 6: magnetic child locks on the chemicals cabinet and the knife drawer. Anti-tip straps on loose furniture (§8).
- Ceiling height is unverified (~10 ft assumed). Measure it. Make tall units' top shelves for rarely used items only, and keep a folding step in the utility. Pull-down shelf mechanisms (Kessebohmer iMove) are optional.

### A12.7 Wardrobes and storage

**Wardrobes.** Split shutters at ~2,100 mm with a loft above. One shutter to the ceiling warps and is hard to reach.

**Check the loft fits before drawings.** Loft clear height = measured floor-to-ceiling − 2,100 mm shutter − ~100 mm plinth − ~60 mm of carcass top and bottom panels − any false-ceiling drop over the wardrobe. At a true 3,050 mm (10 ft) with no drop, that leaves ~790 mm, just short of 820. So either keep the false ceiling off the wardrobe wall, cut the shutter to ~2,050 mm, or put the 32-inch cases in the store room. Measure the clear height in each bedroom first. Inside: hanging, a trouser pull-out, a valet rod, sock dividers and one locking drawer in the Master for daily jewellery (valuables go in the §16 safe). Put a motion LED strip in each loft, which is a low-voltage strip on the wardrobe's own driver.

**Storage by category** (keep what fits the drawings; none of it needs civil work):

| Category | Where | Carrier | Key dimension |
|---|---|---|---|
| Luggage, 2 large and 2 cabin | Store room (old Linen) and the Master loft | Loft flap-up | **≥820 mm clear** so a 32-inch case fits on its wheels (700 mm takes only a 28-inch). If the loft formula above gives less, the 32-inch cases go to the store room |
| Linen, seasonal | Corridor tall unit if the drawings allow, else BR-02 loft | 600 mm-deep shelves, labelled boxes | Above a child's reach |
| Robot vacuum base | An open bay with a clear floor run-out | Open cabinet floor plus an existing socket | ~450 mm deep, never behind a closed door |
| Stick vacuum dock | Corridor or utility slot | Wall dock | Near an existing socket |
| Cleaning chemicals | Utility under-counter | Locked cabinet | Child lock (leading child-poisoning source) |
| Toys (age 6) | BR-02 Homz storage bench | Drawers at his height, books face-out on the ledges above | Fixed to masonry |
| Shoes | Foyer full-height flush cabinet | Tilt-out racks plus one open cubby for today's shoes | Shoe count: also see §7.3C for sangat days |
| Foyer drop zone | Inside the entry door | Hook rail at 1,500 mm (adults) and 900 mm (Kabir); key-drop and charging shelf near an existing socket; ~400 × 300 mm parcel ledge | No free-standing shoe rack: it overflows within days |
| Sports gear | Foyer cabinet or utility corridor | Tall bay with a vertical slot for a bat and racquets, plus a peg rail | Cam-locked if it holds anything sharp |
| Ironing board | Utility wall | Fold-flat wall board (IKEA JÄLL type) | Mount high enough that the iron's cable is out of Kabir's reach |
| Tools and spare hardware | Utility drawer | Drill, hand-tool kit, a parts bin of plugs and anchors | Locked drawer: batteries and blades out of reach |

**The utility is over-subscribed.** Earlier sections assign it a washer-dryer stack, a softener, a granite counter and sink, drying racks, an ironing board, the chemicals cabinet, a tool drawer and possibly a network rack and a safe. Measure its length on site, draw a plan and elevation for Homz, and decide in writing what moves out. The network rack (§2) and the safe (§16) are the first to evict: a hot, wet room suits neither.

### A12.8 Termite: what's worth doing on floor 42

- **Board with the mill's termite and borer warranty.** Century's pages state "borer and termite proof" for Sainik 710 (with a 10-year warranty) and Sainik MR. The Club Prime page makes no termite claim, so if you upgrade, get Century's or Homz's termite cover in writing. It costs nothing extra: name it in the BOQ.
- **No soil treatment.** Soil treatment belongs at foundation stage, which is the builder's job. Ask the builder for the anti-termite certificate for the record.
- **Optional: drill-fill-seal.** A post-construction perimeter treatment by a licensed pest-control firm, along the walls the joinery will cover, before installation. Do it only if the RWA reports termites in the tower. ~₹8k–15k, unverified. Pin the warranty term in writing. Only a 3-year post-construction term is documented.

### A12.9 The service lift and delivery terms

Measure five numbers and write them into every order as a supply condition: lift car width × depth × height, door clear opening, whether the car roof has a hatch, fire-stair clear width at the tightest turn, and the flat's entry-door clear opening. Indian service cars are often ~1,300 × 1,100 mm with an 800 mm door (typical 8-passenger; unverified for this tower). Two consequences:

1. Stone arrives in pieces cut at the yard (A9.4).
2. Factory-made Homz panels ship flat, so they're fine. Any pre-assembled tall unit or 2.4 m shutter needs the car diagonal checked first.

Write "delivered and installed inside B4206, floor 42" on every loose-furniture order.

### A12.10 False ceiling

- **Board and frame:** Gyproc Gypboard (plain) 12.5 mm on Gyproc Gypframe GI; Gyproc MR board in the kitchen and utility. Homz offers gypsum and POP. Name gypsum board, not POP.
- **Start from what exists.** VRF is installed. If it's ducted, the builder has probably built bulkheads or a partial ceiling round the units and ducts. Photograph them, and draw the new ceiling from them, not from a blank slab. Keep every VRF and drain-pump hatch reachable (~600 × 600 mm, per §7).
- **Extent:** set it out from the existing VRF grilles. Perimeter cove by default, which keeps ceiling height. Use a full drop only where §3 keeps in-ceiling speakers or §2 keeps ceiling access points, because a 2 ft perimeter band leaves no plenum in the middle. Draw it per room on a signed ceiling plan.
- **Before boarding (irreversible):** anchors for any ceiling fan, heavy pendant or curtain track; access panels at every driver and junction; curtain pelmets where motorised tracks go (§7.2). The tower plausibly has post-tensioned slabs, so ask the builder for the slab type. Scan before drilling, use depth-limited anchors, and **never drill through the slab**.
- **Low-voltage cable:** if §2 or §3 keep ceiling devices, the cable goes in before boarding. Homz's site electrician or the AV or network installer pulls it, and the BOQ must say which. Otherwise skip the cable.
- **Lights in the ceiling** are wired from the existing circuits by Homz's electrician. That's a fit-out task, not rewiring.
- **Finishing:** tape, putty, primer and paint on new gypsum, matched to the builder's shade, sits in Homz's scope (§7 owns that row). The tier here is Should because the extent is a choice; once any board goes up, finishing it is a Must.
- **Cost:** inside the Homz quote. For reference, branded gypsum runs ~₹75–125/sqft installed (old note, unverified).

### A12.11 Cut from this section, and why

| Item | Reason |
|---|---|
| Resilient-channel and rockwool acoustic wall (~₹450/sqft) | It needs taping, plaster and repaint work in a finished flat. Revisit only if noise proves a problem after move-in |
| Recessed shadow-line skirting | It needs a plaster reveal: civil work |
| Laminate-to-tile T-strips | The Master already has builder laminate flooring. No new floors |
| Picture rail (Gallery System) | Art hanging, not joinery; it needs no Homz work |
| Wicker baskets | Pest and mould risk in humid months (practitioner view); use drawers |
| Spec'ing "Sainik 710" as IS 710 | It's IS 303 BWP, which is fine for a kitchen, but don't call it marine |
| Merino "Cleaf" | An Italian brand; no India channel verified |
| Hob-side inset landing strip | Not needed with granite (A9.1) |

## A13. Rooms over time — detail

Kabir is 6 and the only child. He sleeps in the Master with his parents for now: the 72-inch king, plus a ₹995 floor gadda on his side if three gets tight ([A7 §7.1](#kabir-in-the-master)). BR-02 (13'0"×11'11", north light) is his study and play room, with a bed for naps, sleepovers and the day he moves in. Until then its sofa-bed is a sofa and a spare single; adult guests sleep on BR-01's HYLTARP. No nursery scope: no cribs, changing tables or baby monitors.

#### Kabir's room, BR-02

Layout: sofa-bed along the solid wall, desk side-on to the north window (light from his left if he's right-handed), low storage under the window line, open floor in the middle. Keep one wall near-empty. One display wall for his own art, framed and rotated. Warm wood, muted sage and ochre, no bright plastic.

| Sofa-bed, his nightly bed | [IKEA PS 2026 sofa-bed](https://www.ikea.com/in/en/p/ikea-ps-2026-3-seat-sofa-bed-skiftebo-light-grey-beige-90620350/), Skiftebo light grey-beige | 59,990 | 204×102, open or closed | One 92×204 mattress, 24 cm, pocket springs under HR foam, on solid-pine slats. Bed 41 cm high. Nothing folds, so there is no pinch point |
| Sleepover bed | [IKEA KOMPISHÄNG](https://www.ikea.com/in/en/p/kompishaeng-mattress-pouffe-beige-red-60628767/) fold-out mattress | 12,990 | 80×200 unfolded; 80×68 folded | 13 cm pocket springs. Stands as a pouffe in the play corner by day. Takes standard 80×200 sheets |
| Desk | IKEA BERGLÄRKA, tiltable (2 Oct) | 14,990 | 120×70 | Crank 52–75 cm, tilts 10/20/30°, EN 17191. "Last chance to buy" |

| Footrest | Grin Health foam foot-rest, flat top, 10/15 cm ([B08L6XB7WN](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B08L6XB7WN)) | 999 | — | Until his feet reach the floor. Not IKEA BOLMEN: at 25 cm it's too tall |
| Desk lamp | IKEA TERTIAL work lamp, E27, clamps on | 1,490 | shade Ø17 | See [A10](#a10-lamps-and-decorative-lighting--detail) |

**Total now:** ~₹96,109 (₹59,990 + 12,990 + 14,990 + 999 + 6,490 + 650) plus the Homz bench, ₹6,990 for VIMUND at age 7 and ₹1,490 for the TERTIAL lamp. Straps are extra (§8). Colour: the Harbour palette ([BR-02](#br-02-kabirs-room), [A28.3](#a283-br-02-kabirs-room)).

#### Sleep piece options

The old note ranked daybed with trundle above platform bed, floor gaddi, sofa-cum-bed and Murphy bed. The owner asked for a just-in-case sofa-cum-bed in BR-02, since Kabir sleeps in the Master, and invited better ideas (19 Sep). PS 2026 fits that brief. It gives a real one-piece mattress and reads as a bench by day. When he moves in full time, a proper single bed with a trundle beats it as a nightly bed, and it adds no moving mechanism. A Murphy bed is a pinch hazard for a 6-year-old.

| Option | ₹ | For | Against |
|---|---|---|---|
| **IKEA PS 2026 sofa-bed** (pick) | 59,990 | Buy and use this month. One 24 cm pocket-spring mattress on solid-pine slats. No fold, no hinge, bed 41 cm off the floor. A UK owner says the mattress can be swapped for a normal single later | IKEA sells it for "occasional sleep" and it has 6 reviews, so nightly use rests on the spec (thin evidence). The mattress is also the seat, so it wears first: re-check for dips at 12 months |
| **IKEA KOMPISHÄNG fold-out mattress** (pick, for a friend) | 12,990 | 80×200, 13 cm pocket springs. Folds to a pouffe, so it earns its floor space | No frame: it sleeps on the floor. Too new to judge, 8 reviews |
| IKEA HEMNES daybed + 2 mattresses (baseline) | 53,970 | Mattresses included at the right size. 3 drawers | The owner chose sofa-cum-beds; HEMNES stays the baseline, see §7.6. Only the bottom rail is solid pine, the pull-out is stiff, and the ~56 cm seat is high for a daily sofa |
| Homz-built daybed with trundle, BWP ply, laminate to match the wardrobes | ~45,000–75,000 (**unverified**, ask Homz) | Matches the room. Sized to Indian mattresses. Homz's warranty | Adds to the BOQ and the schedule. Specify locking castors on the trundle |
| Freedom Tree Andaman teak pull-out bed | 1,04,300 frame only | Seasoned solid teak, 206×86 closed, 206×157 open | Fretwork back is decorative. ₹1L for occasional use |
| Alankaram daybeds (Santai, Sohva) | 1,10,000+ | Solid wood, Hyderabad showroom | No trundle |

**Mattress sizes.** PS 2026's bed measures 92 × 204 cm and IKEA publishes no separate mattress size, so measure it in store. A standard 90 × 200 protector or sheet may fall 4 cm short. Buy a deep-pocket protector that fits 24 cm. KOMPISHÄNG takes standard 80 × 200 sheets. Indian singles are 91×190 or 91×198 cm. If Homz builds a daybed instead, size it to an Indian mattress (§11 covers mattresses).

#### Desk and chair fit

A 6-year-old is about 115–120 cm tall. EN 1729-1 (school chairs and tables) sizes furniture by stature. From memory of the standard, **not fetched this round (verify)**:

| EN 1729-1 size mark | Stature | Seat height | Table height |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2 | ~108–121 cm | 31 cm | 53 cm |
| 3 | ~119–142 cm | 35 cm | 59 cm |
| 4 | ~133–159 cm | 38 cm | 64 cm |

So Kabir is size mark 2 now (~53 cm desk). BERGLÄRKA's 52 cm minimum matches it, which PÅHL's 59 cm couldn't. Until 7, a fixed chair at the desk with the foot-rest under his feet. From 7, raise VIMUND's seat until his elbows clear the desk, and lower the foot-rest as he grows. Crank BERGLÄRKA to ~59 cm at 7–8, ~64 cm at 9–10, and consider an electric frame (≥62 cm) then. Check the fit each school year.

The old note rejected the Stokke Tripp Trapp as a desk chair. That's a judgement call: it has an adjustable seat and footplate, but no castors and no swivel. It costs several times the IKEA pair (India price **unverified**). Not needed.

#### Safety

- **Anti-tip straps on every unit over 60 cm,** into masonry. Homz's wardrobes should be fixed by Homz; ask for it in writing.
- **Window restrictors and grilles:** the builder fitted MS safety grilles on UPVC windows (my-home). Check every grille and balcony lock at handover (§8).
- **No cord loops.** Curtains on tracks, no chain rollers (§7.2).
- **Rounded corners** on anything at head height: the VIMLE footstool in the Living, the oval coffee table in the Drawing.
- **No camera in BR-02 or the Master.** See §1 for voice devices.
- **Balconies:** nothing he can drag to the rail. Folding chairs live indoors (§7.1).

#### Guests, BR-01

**Guests sleep in BR-01.** The HYLTARP 2-seat sofa-bed stands on the north wall and opens to a one-piece 140 × 200 cm double (₹97,980, 182 × 93 cm closed, 182 × 240 cm open). BR-01 stays the WFH room and library by day.

**BR-02 is overflow, not the guest room.** Kabir sleeps in the Master for now, so PS 2026 is a sofa and a spare single. Two parties can stay at once, one on HYLTARP in BR-01 and one on PS 2026 in BR-02. When Kabir moves in, PS 2026 becomes his nightly bed and BR-01 still takes guests, so nothing has to be bought twice.

Guest kit:
- Two sets of 200–300 thread-count cotton percale sheets, a light quilt and a winter comforter.
- A bathroom tray: toothbrushes, paste, razor and sanitary pads.
- A bedside charger and a universal travel adapter.
- A printed card with the guest Wi-Fi name and password, and the tower security number. Update it when the password changes (§2).

#### Aging in place, revised

The bathrooms came finished, so the old plan to **cast ply blocking behind tile** is gone. Without blocking, a grab bar goes through the tile into the masonry with proper anchors.

- **Optional:** SS304 60 cm grab bars (Simon's Flexy, pack of 2, ₹2,938) by the WC and at the shower entry, **outside the shower spray zone**. A plumber fits them with a diamond tile bit and seals each hole with silicone. Drilling a wet wall can breach the waterproofing behind the tile, and the owner has no waterproofing scope.
- **Check the wall first.** Ask the builder whether each bathroom wall is RCC (Mivan-style shear wall), brick or AAC block. RCC takes a standard expansion anchor. AAC block needs an anchor made for aerated concrete, or the bar can pull out under load. Find the pipes too: the plumber should check the builder's plumbing drawing before drilling.
- **Lever handles:** check what the builder fitted before buying anything. Replace only knobs.

#### Checks at handover

1. **Door leaves:** record what each internal door is (solid or hollow core, thickness). A hollow-core leaf won't hold a closer or an acoustic seal.
2. **VRF return air:** shut each bedroom door with the VRF running for 20 minutes. If the door whistles or the room won't cool, the room needs a return path. Ask Homz to trim the door bottom by about 10 mm, or fit a transfer grille. Don't seal bedroom doors before this test.
3. **Lift for a stretcher:** ask the facility manager which car takes a stretcher (§19).

#### Dropped from A13

- Marine-ply blocking behind tile at grab-bar positions: the tile is already laid.
- Jaquar Continental grab bars as a fit-now item: demoted to Optional, any SS304 bar.
- Night-lights per bath on new wiring: use plug-in motion night-lights if wanted.
- Gurudwara incense venting via a new duct fan: that's new ventilation work. See §7.3C for the Gurudwara.
- Nursery items, baby monitors, cribs.
- HEMNES daybeds in BR-01 and BR-02: the owner chose sofa-cum-beds; HEMNES stays the baseline, see §7.6.

- IKEA India: [IKEA PS 2026](https://www.ikea.com/in/en/p/ikea-ps-2026-3-seat-sofa-bed-skiftebo-light-grey-beige-90620350/), [KOMPISHÄNG](https://www.ikea.com/in/en/p/kompishaeng-mattress-pouffe-beige-red-60628767/), [HYLTARP](https://www.ikea.com/in/en/p/hyltarp-2-seat-sofa-bed-gransel-natural-s29489599/), [NYHAMN](https://www.ikea.com/in/en/p/nyhamn-3-seat-sofa-bed-with-pocket-spring-mattress-knisa-grey-beige-s69306362/), [LYCKSELE MURBO](https://www.ikea.com/in/en/p/lycksele-murbo-2-seat-sofa-bed-knisa-light-grey-s89387041/), [HEMNES bundle](https://www.ikea.com/in/en/p/hemnes-day-bed-w-3-drawers-2-mattresses-white-afjaell-medium-firm-s29521504/) (rejected baseline, §7.6), [PÅHL](https://www.ikea.com/in/en/p/pahl-desk-height-adjustable-white-s69619344/), [ÖRFJÄLL](https://www.ikea.com/in/en/p/oerfjaell-childrens-desk-chair-white-vissle-light-green-30541431/), [BOLMEN](https://www.ikea.com/in/en/p/bolmen-step-stool-white-50574430/), [TROFAST](https://www.ikea.com/in/en/p/trofast-storage-combination-with-boxes-light-white-stained-pine-white-s29584544/), [TIPHEDE](https://www.ikea.com/in/en/p/tiphede-rug-flatwoven-natural-black-00456759/), [VIHALS](https://www.ikea.com/in/en/p/vihals-day-bed-w-trundle-bed-2-mattresses-white-vannareid-extra-firm-s89580623/)

## A14. WFH ergonomics — detail

The owner works from home about 8 hours a day in BR-01 (13'×13', south window). The sit-stand desk and Steelcase chair are owned. So this section buys little and mostly sets the room up.

#### Contents (A14)
- [Siting](#siting)
- [What to buy](#what-to-buy)
- [Call acoustics, honestly](#call-acoustics-honestly)
- [Reference: if the chair or desk is ever replaced](#reference-if-the-chair-or-desk-is-ever-replaced)
- [Dropped from A14](#dropped-from-a14)
- [Sources for A14](#sources-for-a14)

#### Siting

- Put the desk against the east wall, facing it, with the south window on your right (§7.4). Nothing bright sits behind you on video, and no window glares on the screen.
- The Homz library on the west wall (§7.4), with acoustic panels in its centre bay, is the call backdrop, about 8 ft behind you. Fill it; a full shelf breaks up echo.
- Keep the VRF fan-coil's airflow off the desk and the back of your neck.
- Network: see §2. Wi-Fi from a nearby access point is the fallback.
- One plant (areca palm or calathea) about 60 cm back from the window, out of the June sun.

#### What to buy

| Tier | Item | Pick | ₹ | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Must | Desk, chair | Owned | 0 | Don't re-buy |
| Should | Task light | BenQ ScreenBar Pro | 14,490 (Amazon.in) | Clamps on the monitor and lights the desk, not the screen. BenQ's clamp has a sawtooth top for non-flat and curved monitors. USB-C, 5 V / 1.7 A, from the monitor or a charger. Its ultrasonic sensor sits ~50 cm above the desk and detects movement 60±10 cm in front of the screen (BenQ India), so it may switch off while you sit very still. BenQ quotes Rf >96 and Rg ≈100 and "flicker-free", but no CRI number |
| Optional | Monitor arm | Jin Office single gas-spring arm (B07YKZ7VTS) | 2,988 (Amazon.in) | Buy only if the stand can't put the top of the screen at eye height. VESA 75×75 or 100×100, 13–32", C-clamp or grommet, 1-year warranty, distributed by Jin Office Solutions, Mumbai. 4.7/5 over 683 ratings. The title says 10 kg but the specs say 6 kg max: weigh the monitor first. Long-term sag is **unverified**; the old note warned that ~₹2,000 arms sag within six months |
| Optional, upgrade | Monitor arm | Ergotron LX (45-490-216) | 35,566 (Amazon.in import listing) | The old note's ₹15–18k is out of date. An import listing has no clear India warranty. An authorised Ergotron India reseller price was **not checked** |
| Optional | KVM switch | UGREEN 4K HDMI 2-port | ~5,500 (old note, **unverified**) | Only if you run two laptops on one monitor |
| Optional | Laptop lock | Kensington keyed lock | ~3,000 (old note, **unverified**) | House-help and deliveries come through the flat |

#### Call acoustics, honestly

Most echo on calls comes from hard, bare surfaces. The flat's bedrooms have 800×800 vitrified tile and painted walls. The fixes, cheapest first:

1. **Fill the shelves.** A full bookcase behind you breaks up reflections.
2. **Close the curtain** (sheer plus lined drape, §7.2) during calls.
3. **Add a rug** under or beside the desk.
4. **Only then, fabric panels.** 4–5 fabric-wrapped panels behind the camera, about ₹2,500 each (old note, **unverified**). Skip if calls already sound fine.

**Door seals help little.** The old note said a full seal set on a solid-core leaf reaches about 31 dB Rw, and no seal improves the leaf itself (old note, source not re-checked: **unverified**). Genuine mechanical drop seals had no Indian retail channel; what sells at ₹92–225 is adhesive draft strip. Check whether the BR-01 door is solid-core before spending (A13 handover checks). Don't seal the door before the VRF return-air test in A13.

#### Reference: if the chair or desk is ever replaced

Kept from the old note, **not re-verified** this round:
- Herman Miller Aeron size B, ~₹1.1–1.6L, 12-year warranty. Steelcase Series 1 ~₹52k. Featherlite Contessa ~₹19k as value.
- Reject mesh chairs under ~₹15k and chairs without lumbar depth adjustment.
- Sit-stand desks: dual-motor with memory presets. IKEA's own electric desks were rated poorly on controls.

#### Dropped from A14

- **Dyson Solarcycle Morph** (~₹37k, price unverified): the light bar does the job for less and takes no desk space.
- **Phase-1 Cat6 drop as "the only irreversible part":** network belongs to §2, and any cable through the Homz ceiling is a §2 decision.
- **Full studio treatment:** overkill for calls.
