## A15. Home insurance and asset inventory — detail

**Two layers, two owners.** Homz insures its site while it works. You insure the flat. Buy building cover on the shell before Homz labour enters. Raise Contents at the first owner-bought delivery. At Homz handover (about April 2027, per A27.5), raise the declared construction rate so Building covers the joinery.

### A15.1 What changed from the old note

| Old claim | Now | Why |
|---|---|---|
| You buy Contractors' All Risks, workmen's compensation and contractor liability before labour enters | **Homz buys CAR and employees' compensation.** Liability cover only if you add it to ask 9 (A15.2). You collect the certificates | Homz is the turnkey contractor. Rider ask 9 puts every worker and every labour-law duty on Homz (A7) |
| BOCW §45(2) makes the owner liable for unpaid worker compensation | **Dropped as a citation** | The BOCW Act was folded into the labour codes in force since 21 Nov 2025. At ₹18L the own-residence threshold very likely doesn't bite (PRS's summary says ₹50 lakh; exact wording **unverified**) |
| An "electronics rider" in Bharat Griha Raksha covers surge damage | **Not in the standard wording.** The wording excludes damage to electrical or electronic machines from short-circuit or over-running, lightning included | Tata AIG customer sheet, exclusion 5. Surge protection is the §20 SPD, not a policy |
| Burglary is a standard Bharat Griha Raksha add-on | **Carrier-specific.** Tata AIG's sheet lists only three add-ons, and burglary isn't one | Ask each insurer. If none offers it, buy a separate burglary policy or a home package that includes it |
| Declared contents of ₹22–33L | **Re-size from the real purchase list** at move-in, likely ₹15–30L (estimate) | The old figure came from a ₹17–29L tech budget that later sections cut |

### A15.2 The fit-out layer (Homz's job)

| Cover | Who holds it | What to check on the certificate |
|---|---|---|
| Contractors' all-risk (the works) | Homz, rider ask 9 | The site address says B4206. The period runs to handover. The sum covers the ₹18L works |
| Employees' compensation | Homz, rider ask 9 | It covers every person on site, including subcontractors. Under the Code on Social Security, 2020 (wording **unverified**) |
| Third-party / public liability | Homz, **only if you add it** | Ask 9 as written in A7 says nothing about liability. Add: "and third-party liability of at least ₹50 lakh, including sprinkler leakage, naming the Association and Tower B as additional insured, valid to handover." The RWA may ask for it before it issues labour passes |

**Why the liability line matters.** Three failures cost more than the works: a Homz hot-work fire that spreads, a drill into a pipe that floods the flat below, and a crew knocking a sprinkler head while it builds the false ceiling around it. **Your own Bharat Griha Raksha policy excludes sprinkler leakage caused by "repairs or alterations in your Home"** (peril 13). So Homz's CAR and liability cover must name sprinkler-leak damage to your flat and to the flats below. The CAR policy's optional third-party section may cover these. Confirm it's on Homz's certificate, with a stated limit.

The deposit you pay the RWA covers common-area damage. It doesn't cover a fire. Ask Homz how it controls hot work: grinding, welding and adhesives near laminate and foam.

### A15.3 Your policy: Bharat Griha Raksha (verified against the wording)

Bharat Griha Raksha is the IRDAI standard home policy. Every insurer uses the same wording, so pick on price and claims service. Quote three insurers on one schedule. The facts below come from Tata AIG's wording and customer sheet (UIN IRDAN108RP0019V02202021).

| Clause | What it says | What it means for B4206 |
|---|---|---|
| Building sum insured | Carpet area in m² × the rate of cost of construction you declare, "including Fittings and Fixtures" | 2,045 sqft = 190 m². The builder's bathrooms sit here now. Homz's fixed joinery joins at handover, not under Contents |
| Carpet area definition | Balconies, verandahs and parking count at 25% of their floor area | Add a quarter of the balcony area to the 190 m² |
| Waiver of underinsurance (Clause I) | "Underinsurance does not apply." A low sum insured doesn't shrink a partial claim | You still can't recover more than the sum insured on a total loss |
| Automatic escalation | Building sum insured rises 1/365th of 10% a day on annual policies, and 10% a year on multi-year ones, up to 100% | Construction inflation is built in. You don't need a margin on top |
| Built-in contents | "20% of the Sum Insured for Home Building Cover subject to a maximum of ₹10 Lakh", only if you buy both covers | Declare a higher Contents figure and pay for it. It never rises by itself |
| Valuable contents | Optional cover on an agreed value, backed by a valuation certificate | Jewellery and art need this for fire and flood. It doesn't fix the 7-day theft rule. See "Jewellery" below |
| Theft | Covered only "within 7 (seven) days from the occurrence of and proximately caused by" an insured event | Ordinary burglary of an empty or occupied flat isn't covered. This applies to the whole policy, Valuable Contents included |
| Exclusion 5 | Electrical or electronic machines damaged by short-circuit, over-running or leakage, "lightning included" | TV, AV and appliances aren't covered for surge |
| Exclusion 11 | Structural changes adding over 10% carpet area, unless you pay more | Not relevant. Homz makes no structural change |
| Add-ons (Tata AIG) | Temporary resettlement, personal liability, accidental damage to general contents | Take **personal liability**. It covers a leak from your flat that floods the flat below |
| Optional personal accident | ₹5 lakh per person, self and spouse | Optional. Term life and existing cover may already do this |

**Sizing (estimate, confirm with each insurer).**

| Line | Basis | Rough figure |
|---|---|---|
| Building, now | (190 m² + 25% of balconies) × the insurer's accepted rate | ~₹55–75L. The rate per sqft is **unverified**; ask each insurer what it accepts for a Hyderabad high-rise |
| Building, at Homz handover | Ask the insurer to raise the declared construction rate per m² so it includes the fixed joinery (the formula is carpet area × rate, so the sum moves only through the rate) | ~₹70–90L |
| Contents | Replacement cost of loose items: appliances (§4), AV (§3), furniture (§7.1), clothes, kitchenware | ~₹15–30L, sized from invoices at move-in |
| Valuables | Jewellery and art, each with a valuation certificate | Your figure |
| Personal liability | Add-on | ₹10–25L limit is a sensible ask (judgement) |
| Premium | All of the above | **Unverified.** The old note's ~₹8–20k looks high for a policy sold as low-premium, so this note plans ₹3–20k. Get 3 quotes |

**Timing.**
1. Now: building cover on the shell and builder fittings. Tell the insurer the flat is under fit-out and unoccupied until about April 2027. Get its answer in writing. During fit-out the joinery is Homz's risk under its CAR policy, so don't pay for it twice.
2. Before the first owner-bought delivery (§4's Must appliances, ₹2.7–3.1L, plus the Should oven and dryer, to ₹4.4L): raise the Contents sum insured. Theft cover alone won't protect these, because it pays only within 7 days of an insured event. Also get Homz to sign a delivery receipt for each item and hold it at Homz's risk until handover. The simpler route: schedule appliance deliveries for the last fit-out week.
3. At Homz handover: ask the insurer to raise the declared rate per m² to include the joinery.
4. At move-in: add valuables and personal liability. Add burglary only if one of the 3 insurers offers it on Bharat Griha Raksha. Otherwise buy a separate burglary policy.
5. Each year: raise the contents figure as you buy.

**Jewellery.** Keep it in a bank locker, or in a bolted anti-theft safe. Bharat Griha Raksha pays for theft only within 7 days of a fire, flood or other insured event, and Valuable Contents cover doesn't change that. A locker suits pieces worn a few times a year. Bank locker rent is set by each bank by size and branch (~₹2–6k a year for a small or medium locker in a metro, **unverified**). For pieces worn weekly, use the Godrej Forte Pro Digital 40 L ([Amazon](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B07THHHWH9), ₹23,106, 16 kg). Bolt it into a wall or floor inside the locking wardrobe. Scan for conduits first (§12). An unbolted 16 kg box walks out under one arm. The safe stops a casual thief. Pair it with a separate burglary policy if no Bharat Griha Raksha insurer sells the add-on.

**Check first.** Ask your home-loan lender and the builder whether either already placed a property policy on B4206. Lenders often bundle one. Don't buy a second policy on the same building.

### A15.4 Asset inventory (one hour at move-in)

1. Film a walk-through on a phone. Open every cabinet and drawer.
2. Build one sheet: item, brand, model, serial, purchase date, invoice file, photo file.
3. Photograph jewellery on a scale card. Photograph the back of each artwork with its label.
4. Store the sheet and photos in the family cloud and on the offline drive (A16).

### A15.5 Rejected

- **Relying on the society's policy.** A tower policy covers the shell and common areas. It doesn't cover your fixtures, contents or liability.
- **Inflating Contents to cover the joinery.** Joinery is a fixture. It belongs under Building.
- **Buying your own Contractors' All Risks.** It duplicates Homz's cover. Hold Homz to rider ask 9, with the liability line added.
- **Insuring the joinery from day one.** It doesn't exist yet, and Homz's CAR carries it until handover.

### Sources (A15)

- Tata AIG Bharat Griha Raksha policy wording: https://www.tataaig.com/s3/Bharat_Griha_Raksha_Policy_Policy_Wordings_5219f40e18.pdf (fetched 1 Oct 2026)
- Tata AIG Bharat Griha Raksha customer information sheet: https://www.tataaig.com/s3/tata_aig_bharat_griha_raksha_policy_b1f007a95a.pdf (fetched 1 Oct 2026)
- HDFC ERGO Bharat Griha Raksha page: https://www.hdfcergo.com/home-insurance/bharat-griha-raksha-policy
- PRS, Occupational Safety, Health and Working Conditions Code, 2020: https://prsindia.org/billtrack/the-occupational-safety-health-and-working-conditions-code-2020
- Godrej Forte Pro Digital 40 L listing (₹23,106, 16 kg, read 1 Oct 2026): https://www.amazon.in/dp/B07THHHWH9

## A16. Documents, backups and digital ops — detail

**Answer:** Scans first, boxes second. Most property papers can be re-issued, so a good scan in two places protects more than any safe. Keep the originals in a fire- and water-resistant box inside a locking drawer.

### A16.1 What changed from the old note

| Old | Now | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Synology DS224+ NAS with 2× 8 TB drives as the primary copy | **Optional.** Default is one family cloud plus an offline drive | Fewer moving parts. A NAS needs power, a cool shelf, drive replacements and updates. DS224+ is replaced by the DS225+ (~₹47,000 on Amazon.in before drives) |
| A fire-rated safe bolted to the RCC slab in the utility | **A fire box in the locking wardrobe drawer** Homz builds (§12) | No drilling into the slab. The utility is hot and wet. The box doesn't need bolting because it guards against fire, not theft |
| "Bosch, LG, Samsung, IFB and AO Smith void the extended warranty after 30 days" | **Register each appliance in the delivery week.** Read each warranty card for its own rule | The blanket claim was never sourced (**unverified**) |
| Camera privacy rules | Moved to §8 | Not a documents topic |

### A16.2 Papers

| Paper | Re-issuable? | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Registered sale deed | Yes. A certified copy from the Sub-Registrar (Telangana registration department) | Original in the fire box. Scan in the cloud |
| Sale agreement, allotment letter, possession letter, builder receipts | Partly, from the builder | Fire box + scan |
| OC, fire NOC, approved plan, as-built drawings (§18) | Yes, from the builder or association | Scan only |
| Property-tax receipts, utility account letters | Yes, online | Scan only |
| Form 26QB challans and Form 16B (§18) | Yes, from the tax portal and TRACES | Scan only |
| Passports, birth certificates, Kabir's vaccination card | Slow to re-issue | Fire box + scan. Photocopies go in the §19 grab-bag |
| Wills (A16.5) | No | Fire box. Tell the executor where it is |

**The box.** Measure the registered deed first. Some Telangana deeds are printed on legal-size sheets (216 × 356 mm), not A4 (210 × 297 mm) (**unverified** for yours). None of the boxes below holds a legal sheet flat, so a legal deed folds once along the binding. All four are UL classified for 30 minutes at 1,550°F (843°C) for paper. All are imported listings, so India warranty support is unclear. Interiors are from each Amazon.in listing, read 1 Oct 2026.

| Box | Interior (W × D × H) | A4 flat? | Waterproof | ₹ | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **SentrySafe CHW20221** ([Amazon](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B00KOO594O)) | 12.8 × 8.5 × 4.4 in (325 × 216 × 112 mm) | Yes | ETL, 72 h submersion | 20,425 | **Pick.** A4 lies flat. A legal deed folds once |
| SentrySafe HD4100 ([Amazon](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B00GE57DFK)) | 12 W × 8.1 D × 11.5 H in (305 × 206 × 292 mm), upright | Upright in a hanging file | ETL, 72 h submersion | 19,380 | Alternative if you want hanging files. Taller, so check the wardrobe drawer depth |
| SentrySafe H0100CG ([Amazon](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B00GE586CY)) | 12 × 7 3/8 × 3 3/8 in (305 × 187 × 86 mm) | **No.** The listing says "holds A4 flat", but 187 mm is less than A4's 210 mm | ETL submersion | 14,030 | Rejected. A4 must fold |
| SentrySafe 1200 ([Amazon](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B008HZUI1G)) | Not on the listing; smaller than the H0100CG | No | No | 9,387 | Rejected. Papers fold and it isn't waterproof |

Add a sachet of silica gel and change it each monsoon. The box resists fire, not theft. The Amazon Basics 59 L safe (₹12,999) was rejected: its fire claim is a "modified UL 72" test for 20 minutes at 648°C, weaker than a UL classification. If the flat has a home loan, the lender holds the original deed; keep its acknowledgement and a certified copy in the box instead.

### A16.3 Digital copies (3-2-1, kept simple)

| Copy | What | Cost | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Phones | Photos and scans | 0 | The working copy |
| 2. Family cloud | Google One's 2 TB tier shared with family, or iCloud+ 2 TB. Pick one | ~₹6,500/yr. Google has renamed tiers (one region now sells 2 TB as "Google AI Plus"), so the India plan name and price are **unverified** | Auto-upload photos. A shared folder for house papers |
| 3. Offline drive | Seagate Expansion 2 TB HDD (₹12,499), or the cheaper Toshiba Canvio Basics 2 TB ([Amazon](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B07DNDLHH8), ₹11,696) | One-time | Copy every quarter, then unplug. It survives a hacked account or a mass deletion |
| Optional NAS | Synology DS225+ (~₹47,000) + two NAS drives | ~₹1L total (estimate) | Only if you want local photo serving. Back it up to cloud with versioning. Synology's third-party drive support changed in 2025; check its compatibility list before buying drives (**unverified**) |

### A16.4 Warranties

At each delivery:
1. Photograph the serial plate or QR code.
2. Register on the brand's site that week.
3. Save the invoice and registration PDF to `Warranties/<Brand>-<Model>/` in the cloud.
4. Add a calendar reminder 30 days before the warranty ends.

Racold (geysers, §11), Bosch (kitchen, §4) and Godrej (lock, §8) carry the most value.

### A16.5 Accounts, access and succession

| Item | Do this | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Passwords | Bitwarden Families, $47.88 a year for 6 users before tax (bitwarden.com, checked 1 Oct 2026). With 18% GST on foreign digital services that's ~₹5,100 a year. 1Password Families is the alternative | One shared vault for utilities, RWA and school. A personal vault per adult |
| Emergency access | Set Bitwarden's emergency access between the two adults. Print the recovery codes into the fire box | A forgotten master password otherwise locks the whole vault |
| Society apps | Use a unique random password on MyGate or the tower's app | Treat them as low-trust |
| Smart home | Give your spouse admin rights on the Godrej lock app, the Alexa household, Aqara Home and Tapo | One unavailable account holder otherwise locks the family out |
| House-help | A revocable PIN or card on the lock. Never the master code | Revoke it the day they leave |
| Nominations | File them on the flat (with the association), bank, demat, insurance and EPF | Quick, and it speeds transfer |
| Wills | One will per adult | A nominee holds assets for the legal heirs and doesn't become the owner (Sarbati Devi v. Usha Devi, SC 1984, on insurance; reaffirmed for shares in Shakti Yezdani v. Jayanand Salgaonkar, SC 2023). Sikhs come under the Hindu Succession Act, 1956 (s.2(1)(b)). Registration of a will is optional |

## A17. Health, first-aid and poison control — detail

**Answer:** One locked medicine compartment above 1.5 m, a short kit list, and two verified numbers on the §19 card. The grab-bag is §19's. This section adds only the medical items.

### A17.1 Numbers for the §19 card (verified 1 Oct 2026)

| Who | Number | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Emergency (police, fire, ambulance) | 112 | National emergency number |
| Ambulance | 108 | Telangana ambulance service |
| **AIIMS National Poisons Information Centre**, 24×7 | **1800 116 117** (toll-free). Landlines 011-2658 9391 and 011-2659 3677 | aiims.edu NPIC page |
| **Rainbow Children's Hospital & BirthRight, Financial District**: a children's hospital open 24 hours, with 24/7 emergency staff. The centre page lists a PICU and a Level 3–4 NICU (60 beds) | **1800 2122** (emergency and ambulance) first, then **79970 79970** (24×7), then the hospital line 080 3783 6515. Survey No 74, Financial District, Nanakramguda 500008 | rainbowhospitals.in centre page: the three tel: links and the PICU/NICU entries in its structured data. The header shows the first two with ambulance and 24×7 icons but no text label. Put 1800 2122 first on the §19 card |
| Adult ERs | AIG, Continental (numbers on the §19 card) | §19 |

Read the drive time to Rainbow off a map at 9 pm on a weekday. It's in the same district, so expect under 15 minutes (estimate).

### A17.2 Where it lives

Ask Homz for a **lockable top compartment** in a kitchen tall unit or the utility, above 1.5 m. A cam lock costs a few hundred rupees and needs no extra trade. The value fallback is a Plantex lockable metal wall box (₹1,379), screwed into masonry above 1.5 m. Keep cleaners and the dishwasher tablets locked too.

### A17.3 The kit

| Item | Pick | ₹ | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Thermometer | Omron MC-246 digital stick | 231 | Underarm or oral. Infrared forehead guns are faster but less accurate |
| Paracetamol, child dose | Syrup or suspension by weight (e.g. Calpol 250) | ~100 | Dose by weight, not age. Keep the oral syringe it comes with |
| Ibuprofen, child dose | Suspension | ~100 | **Never give it for a fever that could be dengue: any fever from June to November, or one with a rash or body ache. Use paracetamol and see a doctor.** WHO says ibuprofen and aspirin raise the bleeding risk in dengue. Not on an empty stomach. Ask the paediatrician before first use |
| ORS | **WHO low-osmolarity ORS sachets** ×20 | ~400 | Check the pack says WHO formula. "Electrolyte" drinks aren't ORS |
| Antihistamine | Cetirizine syrup | ~100 | For hives and itch |
| Saline nasal drops | Any | ~80 | |
| Antiseptic | Povidone-iodine (Betadine) solution | ~150 | |
| Dressings | Sterile gauze, cohesive bandage, plasters, micropore tape | ~500 | |
| Tools | Tweezers, blunt scissors, disposable gloves, cling film | ~400 | Cling film is the burn cover (A17.4) |
| Hydrocortisone 1% cream | Any | ~100 | Insect bites and rashes |
| Pulse oximeter | Beurer PO 30 | 1,670 | Adult clip; may misread a small finger |
| BP monitor (optional) | Omron HEM-7120, cuff 22–32 cm | 1,849 | Only if an adult tracks BP |
| CPR pocket mask | Any with a one-way valve | ~300 | |

**Door card for the medicine compartment:** "Fever June–November, or with rash or body ache: paracetamol only, no ibuprofen or aspirin. Call the doctor." Add the weight-based paracetamol dose for Kabir and the date you weighed him.

Kit total: about ₹5,000–6,500 with the devices (estimate). Check expiry dates every 6 months (A22).

### A17.4 Corrections and rejected items

- **Burns: cool running water for 20 minutes, then cling film.** No cream on a fresh burn. The old note stocked silver sulfadiazine, which is a prescription cream for a doctor to decide on. It also called Burnol "obsolete". Burnol is still sold, but no cream belongs on a fresh burn.
- **Infrared forehead thermometers** were rejected as the only thermometer. Keep a digital stick.
- **"Electrolyte" powders and drinks** were rejected as ORS. Only the WHO formula is ORS.
- **Refrigerated medicine plan** was cut from the main body. Nobody in the household needs it today. If that changes, a thermal pouch and two frozen gel packs cover an outage longer than the DG switchover.

### A17.5 Conditional

- **Asthma diagnosis:** a spare reliever inhaler and spacer in the kit and the §19 grab-bag.
- **Food allergy diagnosis:** adrenaline auto-injectors are prescription-only in India. Ask the paediatrician.

## A18. Handover, snag and change-of-address — detail

**Answer:** Possession is done, so the old "don't sign until" advice is moot. Three things remain. Chase the 21 Sep handover snag list before Homz covers the walls. Settle the TDS forms today. Then move every owner record into your name. The handover-day checklist itself is [[u-handover]].

### A18.1 What changed from the old note

| Old | Now | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Get a paid snag walk before signing the possession letter | **Chase the 21 Sep list ([[u-handover]]).** Re-walk only the walls Homz will cover. Hire a firm only if you find damp or cracks | Handover happened in Sep 2026. Bathrooms, wiring and paint are finished and out of scope for new work |
| RERA §19(10) two-month possession clock, and "the OC date may show a deadline has run" | **Moot.** You have possession and the deed is registered (31 Aug 2026) | §19(10) is a duty on the buyer to take possession. You've done it |
| "§17(2)'s 30-day document duty runs from the completion certificate" | **Wrong.** The Act says "within thirty days after obtaining the occupancy certificate" | RERA 2016, §17(2) proviso, read in the Gazette text |
| "An emailed complaint is not a notice" under §14(3) | **Overstated.** The Act says only "brought to the notice of the promoter". Send it in a form you can prove | Email plus registered post or a stamped acknowledgement |
| Form 26QB "due 30 Sep 2026" | **Overdue as of today** if the August instalment is unfiled. Act today (A18.4) | Today is 1 Oct 2026 |
| Form 26QD at ₹50L and BOCW cess at ₹25–40k | **Not triggered at ₹18L** (A18.4) | Homz replaces the ₹25–40L self-managed fit-out |
| Ponding test, plumbing pressure test, earth-loop on every socket | **Cut as tests you run.** Ask instead for the builder's ponding-test report and endorsed waterproofing warranty (A18.3) | No plumbing, electrical or waterproofing work is planned, so you now rely on the builder's |
| Tank first-fill flush by the owner | **The facility drains and cleans the tanks.** You run each tap for 5 minutes before the water test (§5) | The overhead tank is the building's, not yours |
| Anti-vibration mounts if a machine room sits above | **Kept as an ask.** Listen at night in the top-floor-facing rooms | Floor 42 of 44 |

### A18.2 RERA, verified text (Act 16 of 2016)

| Section | Text (verbatim or close) | Use it for |
|---|---|---|
| §14(3) | A defect "brought to the notice of the promoter within a period of five years by the allottee from the date of handing over possession" must be fixed "without further charge, within thirty days" | The snag list. The five years run from Sep 2026 |
| §19(5) | The allottee "shall be entitled to have the necessary documents and plans, including that of common areas, after handing over the physical possession" | The document request in A18.3 |
| §17(2) | Documents and plans go to the association "within thirty days after obtaining the occupancy certificate" | Ask the association whether it got them |
| §19(6)–(7) | You pay municipal taxes, water, electricity and maintenance on time, with interest on delay | Set up NACH for maintenance (A21) |
| §19(9) | Every allottee "shall participate towards the formation of an association" | Join it (A21) |

### A18.3 The snag list (by ~21 Oct, before Homz installs anything)

**Start from the 21 Sep list.** [[u-handover]] planned a snag list signed by the builder's rep at handover, with acceptance "subject to attached snag list dated 21-Sep-2026". If you served it, §14(3)'s 30 days end about **21 Oct 2026**. Chase it in writing that week. If you didn't serve one, serve it now; the clock starts on your notice.

**Then re-walk only what Homz will hide.** Once a wardrobe sits on a wall, you can't show the damp was there first. Photograph every wall Homz will cover. Use the table below.

| Check | How | Pass |
|---|---|---|
| Damp or cracks | Look at every wall base, ceiling corner and the walls behind future wardrobes | No stains, no hairline cracks across plaster |
| Tiles | Tap floor and wall tiles with a coin | No hollow sound over large areas |
| Bathroom drains | Run the shower for 2 minutes | Water reaches the drain with no ponding |
| Silicone and grout | Look at basin, WC and shower joints | Continuous, no gaps or mould |
| Sockets | A plug-in socket tester (~₹300) at each socket | Correct wiring lights on every socket |
| RCCB | Press its test button | It trips |
| Geyser points | Photograph each point. Touch a ₹150 non-contact voltage pen to it with the switch on. Note whether it's a socket or a wire outlet, and which MCB feeds it | Live at every point, on its own labelled MCB. The geyser brand's installer connects to it (§11) |
| VRF | Run each indoor unit 15 minutes | Cools, no drip, no rattle |
| Doors and sliders | Open, close and lock each one | No binding, all locks work |
| Window MS grills | Look for rust and loose anchors | Sound |
| Balcony | Pour a bucket near the drain. Measure the parapet | Drains away; parapet ≥1.0 m |
| Noise | Stand in each room at night | No machine hum from above |

**Serve it.** Send one numbered list with photos. Email it to the builder's CRM, and also send it by registered post or hand it over for a stamped acknowledgement. Note the date. If nothing is fixed in 30 days, write again citing §14(3). Ask the builder in writing whether fixing a wardrobe to a bathroom-adjacent wall affects the waterproofing warranty.

**Documents to request (§19(5)):** OC, fire NOC, lift certificate, approved plan, as-built electrical and plumbing drawings, sanitaryware and CP warranty cards, VRF make and warranty, and the slab-stage termite certificate (A7). Add four that matter more now that you rely on the builder's bathrooms and Homz will fix joinery to wet walls:

- the **waterproofing warranty**, endorsed to you;
- the **ponding-test report**, dated with photos (its absence is itself a finding);
- the **PNG commissioning certificate**;
- the **LPS and earth-pit resistance report**.

Give Homz the electrical drawing before anyone drills. [[u-handover]]'s red list has the full set.

**Meter photos.** Photograph the electricity and gas meter faces and serials now. Homz's tools will run on your prepaid electricity meter, so keep a standing balance or the site stops.

### A18.4 Tax and labour, settled

**Form 26QB (TDS on buying the flat).** The buyer deducts 1% of the higher of price or stamp-duty value once it crosses ₹50 lakh. No TAN is needed.

| Rule | Detail |
|---|---|
| Due date | Within 30 days from the end of the month you deducted. An August deduction was due **30 Sep 2026** |
| Late fee | ₹200 a day until filed, capped at the TDS amount (old-Act §234E) |
| Interest | 1.5% a month or part month if you deducted but didn't pay. 1% a month if you never deducted |
| Penalty | Under the old Act's §271H: ₹10,000–1,00,000, not levied if you paid the TDS, fee and interest and filed within **one month** of the due date (window cut from one year on 1 Apr 2025). The new Act's equivalent is **unverified**, so file today rather than counting on a 30 Oct grace |
| Form 16B | Download from TRACES and give it to the builder within 15 days of the due date, so **15 Oct 2026** |

**Do this today.** Log in to incometax.gov.in, open e-Pay Tax, and check the payment history for each builder instalment. File any missing 26QB with the interest and late fee. Download each Form 16B from TRACES once it appears. Scan everything (A16). If a lawyer or the bank filed for you, ask for the challan numbers rather than assuming. Check that the 26QB amounts cover every sum paid to the builder: parking, club, electricity, water and advance maintenance, not only the base price. The old Act's §194-IA counted these charges as consideration; the new Act's wording is **unverified**.

*Caveat:* the Income-tax Act, 2025 replaced the 1961 Act from 1 Apr 2026, so an August 2026 deduction falls under the new Act. Under it, TDS on buying property from a resident seller sits in **s.393(1), Table Sl. No. 3(i)**, with the ₹50 lakh threshold in Note 3 (Finance Bill 2026 memorandum, "Correction of referencing error"). The builder is resident in India, so this is your section. s.393(2) Sl. No. 17 covers non-resident sellers only. The same memo adds a fee as s.427(3), which suggests s.427 is the statement-fee section; its TDS sub-clause is **unverified**. The penalty section and amounts under the new Act are **unverified**. Whether the portal still names the form "26QB" is **unverified**. Check the portal's own fee and penalty figures when you file. The memo's TAN relaxation (from 1 Oct 2026) attaches to s.393(2) Sl. No. 17, the non-resident-seller entry, so it doesn't affect you.

**Form 26QD (TDS on contractor payments).** Applies only when an individual pays one contractor over ₹50 lakh in a year. Homz at ₹18L doesn't trigger it (A0).

**Building-worker cess and labour registration.** The labour codes replaced the BOCW Act from 21 Nov 2025. PRS's summary puts the cost threshold at ₹50 lakh. The own-residence wording is **unverified**, but ₹18L sits well below it. Rider ask 9 puts any labour duty on Homz anyway.

GHMC was split on 11 Feb 2026; CMC's HQ is the National Academy of Construction, HITEC City | Phone the zonal office and ask, in writing, which office holds B4206's record. Ask whether a 2026-27 half-year bill is already pending on B4206, and whether you or the builder owes it until mutation. Arrears attract monthly interest (rate **unverified**). Then file the mutation to get a PTIN in your name. Nanakramguda's ward mapping is **unverified**. Expect glitches: The Hindu reported trifurcation problems in the 2026 early-bird tax drive |

1. Aadhaar for both adults, online. Book **Kabir's biometric update** at an Aadhaar centre this month. UIDAI asks a child enrolled under 5 to update biometrics at 5. It's likely free at Kabir's age (**unverified**; the UIDAI page checked gives no fee; worst case ~₹125).

3. Employer HR and payroll.

5. Voter ID.

Keep the checklist in the cloud. A missed one bounces mail a year later.

- Book the service lift through the facility. Lay floor and lift protection.

## A19. Emergency preparedness on floor 42 — detail

This appendix backs the §19 table. §8 covers detection and prevention. This section covers what the family does when something goes wrong.

### A19.1 The plan in one line

No fire-service ladder reaches floor 42 (~126 m). Plan around three things: your own flat door, the pressurised stair and the refuge floors. Don't plan for a rescue from the balcony. The old note also found no reachable source for Hyderabad's aerial-ladder reach, so don't print a number.

### A19.2 Handover checks (all ₹0)

| Check | How | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Refuge floors | Read them off the tower's fire-safety drawing or the Telangana fire NOC. Ask the facility manager for the file. Then walk to the nearest one and count floors | A tower sanctioned around 2021 falls under NBC 2016. The old note quotes NBC 2016 Annex E-4 as putting refuges at 60 m and every 30 m above it, including for balconied towers (unverified). NBC 2005 Part 4 cl. 4.12.3 exempted balconied flats (verified). So expect refuges below you, but take the floors from the NOC drawing, not from this note |
| Refuge floor condition | Look for clear floor, railings, emergency lights, signage, an extinguisher and drinking water | Builders trim these. Report gaps to the association in writing |
| Stair | Confirm the fire stair is pressurised. Doors should self-close and stay shut. Feel the air push out when you open a stair door | NBC 2005 Part 4 cl. 4.10.2 requires stair pressurisation in high-rise buildings (verified). A propped-open door defeats it |
| Lifts | Confirm each car has an automatic rescue device (ARD) that levels the car on power loss | An ARD prevents entrapment between floors in an outage |
| Stretcher | Ask: "Which lift car is at least 2.1 m deep?" Write the answer on the card | The code's fire-lift minimum (old note: 1,000 kg, 2.35 m²) allows a nearly square car, about 1,500 × 1,570 mm, which takes no stretcher. A 2.35 m² car could also be ~1,100 × 2,140 mm, which does. If no car fits one, a medical emergency means a chair-carry down 42 floors |
| Sprinklers in the flat | See A8.3 | The Homz ceiling must not block them |
| Main door | Ask whether the teak main door is a rated, self-closing fire door, and check that it closes on its own | "Stay in" (A19.3) relies on it. If it isn't rated, lean towards leaving early when the corridor is clear |
| Tower fire plan | Ask for the tower's written evacuation plan: stay-put or full evacuation, and who gives the call | The card below must match it |
| Fire warden | Get the tower fire warden's name and number | Goes on the card |

### A19.3 The fire card (tape inside the front door)

> **Fire in our flat**
> 1. Shout "Fire". Get everyone out. One adult holds Kabir. The other takes the grab-bag.
> 2. Close the flat door behind you. Don't lock it.
> 3. Take the stairs. Never the lift.
> 4. Call 101 or 112 from the stair. Say: "B4206, The Olympus, Nanakramguda, floor 42."
> 5. Meet at the muster point. Nobody goes back in.
>
> **Fire elsewhere in the tower**
> 1. Follow the PA or the fire warden.
> 2. If the corridor is clear, leave by the stairs. Never the lift.
> 3. If the corridor is smoky, stay in. Shut all doors. Call 101 and tower security.
> 4. If smoke comes under the door, put wet towels along the gap.
> 5. If smoke fills the flat, go to the Master balcony (south) and close the door behind you. **The balcony-lock key hangs on the hook beside the Master slider.**

Check this card against the tower's own fire plan before printing. The "stay in when the corridor is smoky" rule follows common high-rise apartment practice in the US and UK. It assumes the flat door is a self-closing rated fire door and the flats are properly compartmented, and neither is confirmed for this tower (see A19.2). Unverified: NFPA's page was unreachable on 2026-10-01.

**Practice.** Walk down the stairs once with Kabir in daylight and time it. Expect ~15–30 minutes for 42 floors, 84 flights (estimate, no source; adults run ~15–20 s a floor before fatigue). Write the measured time on the card. It tells you whether a 15-minute hood would last to the nearest refuge. Repeat the card drill twice a year, once with Kabir reading the steps.

### A19.4 The gas-leak card (tape inside the kitchen cabinet nearest the meter)

From BGL's published guidance for domestic customers:

1. Turn off the hob.
2. Shut the valve on the gas line next to the PNG meter.
3. Open every door and window.
4. Don't switch any light or appliance on or off. Don't use a phone near the leak.
5. Leave the flat. Call the gas company and 101 from outside.

### A19.5 Emergency numbers card (fridge)

These numbers come from the old note, which checked each against the institution's own page. They weren't re-dialled or re-checked on 2026-10-01. Dial-test every one before printing.

| Call | Number | Note |
|---|---|---|
| All emergencies | **112** | Pan-India line |
| Fire · Police · Ambulance | 101 · 100 · 108 | Still published by Cyberabad Police (old note) |
| Poison, 24×7 | 1800 116 117; 011-2658 9391 / 011-2659 3677 | AIIMS National Poisons Information Centre |
| Paediatric ER | 080 3783 6515 | Rainbow Children's Hospital, Financial District. The 080 prefix is a national Rainbow number, not a typo |
| Adult ER | +91 40 6700 0111 | Continental Hospitals, Financial District |
| Adult ER, alternate | +91 40 4244 4244 | AIG Hospitals ambulance, Gachibowli |
| Gas leak | **Read the operator off the meter or bill** | BGL Hyderabad: 1800-599-6991 (verified on BGL's site). Megha Gas: 1800 123 1803 (verified on Megha's site). BGL's area list includes Gachibowli but not Nanakramguda; Megha lists Rangareddy district. Print only the one on your meter |
| Tower security · fire warden · paediatrician | — | Fill in at handover |

Teach Kabir to dial 112 and say "B4206, The Olympus, Nanakramguda."

### A19.6 Smoke hoods (Optional, conditional)

A hood buys time to cross a smoky corridor to the stair. The pressurised stair itself should be clear, and that stair is the real protection. Carbon monoxide is the main killer in fire smoke, so a fire-escape hood must filter it. EN 403 includes a CO test (standard's summary, not re-checked).

| Hood | Standard | ₹ | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| VENUS Fire Escape Mask (B0GMQPVQPN) | Listing claims EN 403. Venus Safety & Health is an Indian PPE maker | 6,500 | **Buy ×2 only if Venus sends an EN 403 certificate showing the CO test.** Otherwise skip |
| TZL40 Emergency Fire Escape Hood | None stated | 2,999 | Rejected: no standard, no CO evidence |
| Generic "EN 140" aluminised hood | EN 140 is a half-mask standard, not an escape-hood standard | 1,711 | Rejected |

**Why conditional.** The VENUS listing's own feature list contradicts its CO claim. It names an "ABEK V-7700 cartridge" protecting against SO₂, NH₃, Cl₂, CCl₄ and H₂S. ABEK filters cover organic vapours, acid gases, ammonia and similar. They don't stop CO, which needs a catalyst such as hopcalite. Another line on the page claims CO filtering, and the item weight reads 0.23 g, so the listing is unreliable. Ask Venus (the maker, not the Amazon seller) for the certificate before buying.

Fit for a 6-year-old is unconfirmed; ask Venus before buying a third. If bought, store the hoods by the front door with the grab-bag and practise donning one once. **Correction:** the old note named Ceasefire and 3M FR57 at ₹3–4k. Ceasefire's online shop lists no hood, and no 3M FR57 turned up on Amazon.in on 2026-10-01.

### A19.7 72-hour kit and the utility box

Ready.gov advises about 1 gallon (3.8 L) of water per person per day for drinking and sanitation. For 3 people over 3 days that's ~35 L. Upper floors lose water first when the pumps stop.

| Item | Where | Note |
|---|---|---|
| ~35 L water (~20 L drinking, the rest for flushing) | Utility | Rotate every 6 months. RO needs power and water |
| One week of each person's medicines, first-aid kit | Grab-bag | Include Kabir's |
| 2 torches, 20,000 mAh power bank | Grab-bag | Top up the bank monthly |
| ₹10,000 in small notes | Utility box | ATMs and UPI fail in a grid or network outage |
| The smart lock's mechanical keys | One outside the flat: a trusted neighbour, the car or the office. One in the utility box | The lock runs on its own batteries, so a power cut doesn't stop it. The key matters when you're locked out, and then the utility box is behind the door. Smart locks of this type usually have an emergency USB power port outside: check the Catus manual and keep a power bank with a cable in the car (unverified for Catus) |
| Copies of IDs and insurance | Grab-bag, sealed pouch | Keep the originals elsewhere (Red data stays out of the vault) |
| Whistle, N95 masks ×6, ORS sachets | Grab-bag | — |
| Sturdy shoes | By the door | Stairs at night |

Cost ~₹5,000–10,000 plus the cash float (estimate).

### A19.8 Split-family plan

- One muster point Kabir memorises, for example "podium fountain, west side". Choose it with the facility manager.
- A family chat with three code words: BLUE (all clear), RED (evacuating), BLACK (need help).
- Kabir knows two adults' phone numbers by heart.

### A19.9 Leaving the flat for a week

PNG off at the meter. Geysers off at the MCB. Leak sensors armed (§8). If the T1 valve controller is fitted, close the water inlet. Fridge to eco, perishables out. Doorbell alerts on. Parcel hold with security. Top up both prepaid meters (§20). A prepaid cut-off is an outage the DG doesn't bridge.

### A19.10 Power and internet failing together

The DG keeps the flat powered, but the ISP's equipment may be down. Alexa, the doorbell and phone alerts then stop. The fixes live elsewhere: 4G fallback on the router (§2), a router mini-UPS for DG changeover (§2), prepaid auto-recharge (§20). Here, keep cash in the utility box and one lock key outside the flat (A19.7).

### A19.11 What changed from the old note

| Old | Now | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Mechanical key in the utility box | One key outside the flat, one in the box | A locked-out family can't reach the box |
| Open the hinged grille panel from the override tin to signal | Dropped | No outside rescue at 126 m; the grille passes air and signals (A8.2) |
| 6 L water per person + a 20 L reserve | ~35 L total for 3 people × 3 days | Ready.gov: ~3.8 L per person per day |
| Ceasefire / 3M FR57 hoods at ₹3–4k | VENUS at ₹6,500, Optional, only with an EN 403 CO certificate | Neither old pick was found on sale. The VENUS listing names an ABEK cartridge |
| "Defend-in-place" as the only doctrine | Two cases: fire in the flat (leave); fire elsewhere (follow the PA or warden, leave if the corridor is clear, stay in if it's smoky) | Plainer. Matches common high-rise practice (unverified). Depends on a rated door, so check it |
| BLUE/RED/BLACK codes, stair walk, vacation list | Kept, shortened | — |

### Sources (A19)

- NBC 2005 Group 1 (Part 4 cl. 4.10.2 stair pressurisation, cl. 4.12.3 refuge areas, cl. 4.15 fire lifts): https://law.resource.org/pub/in/bis/S03/is.sp.7.1.2005.pdf
- BGL emergency numbers and dos and don'ts: https://www.bglgas.com/ · https://www.bglgas.com/dos-and-donts/ · areas: https://www.bglgas.com/business-areas-in-hyderabad/
- Megha Gas areas and emergency number: https://www.meghagas.com/ · FAQ: https://www.meghagas.com/faqs
- Ready.gov emergency kit: https://www.ready.gov/kit
- VENUS escape mask: https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0GMQPVQPN · TZL40 hood: https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0H5KJRB6G · EN 140 hood: https://www.amazon.in/dp/B08RJD73JH
- Ceasefire online shop catalogue (no hood listed): https://www.ceasefireonlineshop.com/

## A20. Building systems: HVAC, electrical, surge and water — detail

*Timeline: meters and facility questions now; VRF access into the Homz drawings before design freeze; AMC after Homz leaves.*

The flat came with VRF, finished wiring and finished bathrooms. So this appendix keeps what is already installed alive and finds the facts that change a purchase. It adds no new circuits or pipework. The only trade visit is optional: an electrician at the DB for a surge device. Geyser sizing lives in §11.

### A20.1 VRF: protect it, reach it, service it

**During Homz's fit-out.** Switch the VRF off on days with sanding, cutting or gypsum work. Cover every indoor-unit grille with plastic sheet and tape. Sawdust drawn into the coil and drain tray is the cheapest damage to prevent and the dearest to clean.

**In the false-ceiling drawing.** Before design freeze, mark every indoor unit, drain pump, drain line and refrigerant joint on Homz's ceiling plan. Write into the BOQ:

- A removable service hatch of ~600 × 600 mm under each indoor unit and drain pump. Confirm the size against the unit's service manual.
- No screws or hangers through refrigerant or drain lines.
- Return-air paths kept open. A ceiling that boxes in a ducted unit's return starves it.

Then check before the ceiling closes. Pour a litre of water into each drain tray (the AMC technician can do this) and confirm it drains.

**Use the cover you already have before buying an AMC.** Get these from the builder in writing:

1. VRF brand, model and indoor-unit count.
2. The warranty card and the **commissioning date**. The warranty clock may have started when the builder commissioned the system, months before your handover.
3. The free-service schedule. New VRF systems usually come with free first-year service and a longer compressor warranty (typical; confirm on your card).

The builder also carries a legal duty. RERA s.14(3): any "structural defect or any other defect in workmanship, quality or provision of services" reported within five years of handing over possession must be fixed "without further charge, within thirty days". That covers faults, not routine servicing. Builders often route bought-in equipment like the VRF to the maker's warranty under the sale agreement. Check what yours says about the VRF. Report VRF faults to the builder in writing first, and copy the brand's service line.

**Then the AMC.** Buy a brand-authorised AMC only from the month the free cover ends. Two visits a year: filter clean, coil clean, gas-pressure check and a drain-line flush. Cost is ~₹15–30k a year for a flat this size (unverified estimate; get a quote). Wash the return-air filters yourself every ~6 weeks in the dry season.

**Condensate overflow.** A clogged drain tray over gypsum shows up months later as a ceiling stain that is your liability. Most ducted and cassette VRF indoor units ship with a drain pump and a float switch that stops the unit on high water. Ask the AMC technician to confirm yours do. If not, ask about an auxiliary float switch. Optional: two more of §8's Aqara leak sensors (₹3,080 each) inside the Living and Master hatches. §8 currently buys three, for the sink, dishwasher/RO and washer, so none covers a VRF tray. Add them to §8's count if you want this.

### A20.2 Electricity: prepaid meters, sanctioned load and the DG

**Prepaid meters.** [[my-home]]'s builder spec lists one "dual-source prepaid energy meter" (grid and DG through the same meter) and a separate prepaid PNG gas meter. A zero balance is a billing cutoff, not a grid fault. The DG doesn't bridge it. The fridge, network, VRF, leak sensors and the lock's Wi-Fi all stop together. During fit-out, Homz's tools stop too. That zero-balance behaviour is likely but unverified until the facility answers. Set auto-recharge and low-balance alerts on the electricity meter this week. Keep a standing float.

**Gas is a separate prepaid meter.** A zero PNG balance stops all cooking. PNG prepaid meters are often smart-card or token types, which can't auto-recharge (unverified for this building). Read the gas company's name off the meter (the §19 card lists it). Then ask the facility or that company how the meter tops up: app, card or token. If it's a card, keep the card in one known drawer and a float on it.

Add "top up the electricity and gas meters" to the §19 travel checklist and a balance check for both to the §22 monthly list.

**Ask the facility in writing:**

| Question | Why it matters |
|---|---|
| What is B4206's sanctioned load in kW? Is the supply single- or three-phase? | Prepaid meters may trip at the sanctioned load. It sets how many geysers can run at once. The phase decides the SPD pole count (A20.5) |
| What is the per-flat cap on DG supply, and is it lower than the grid sanction? | A low DG cap trips the flat when the VRF and geysers run together during an outage |
| What happens at zero balance? Is there emergency credit or a grace period? | Decides how large a float to keep |
| How does the PNG meter top up: app, card or token? Can it auto-recharge? | A zero gas balance stops all cooking |
| Are the domestic water pumps and the overhead-tank fill on DG? | A top-of-stack flat loses water first in a pump outage. That, not the ACs, is the real resilience gap |
| Do the top floors have a booster or hydro-pneumatic system? | Decides whether low shower pressure is normal or a fault (A20.4) |
| Does the bore supply pass through a community softener or treatment plant? | May remove the need for any flat-level softener (A5.6) |
| Fit-out rules: is a flat-level booster pump or a DB surge device allowed? | Avoids buying something the RWA will make you remove |

TGSPDCL's site gave no substantive content on meter behaviour, so zero-balance and load-limit behaviour here are unverified until the facility answers.

**Load enhancement.** If the sanctioned load is lower than the realistic simultaneous peak, ask the facility whether a flat-level enhancement is possible in a sub-metered tower. It is paperwork, not wiring. The old note's ₹11,000–25,000 figure for +5 kW is unverified. Build the peak from the heaters people forget: the front-loader washer (~2 kW; Indian front-loaders take a cold-only feed and heat internally) and a dryer (1–2.5 kW).

**Whole-house inverter: skipped.** The builder spec says 100% DG backup. A whole-house inverter means rewiring at the DB. Revisit only if the facility says ACs are shed on DG and that matters to you. Then a 1.5–2 kVA LiFePO₄ unit is the type to buy. The APC UPS in §2 covers the network either way.

### A20.3 Morning peak load

Geyser size, heat-up time and models are in §11. This section covers only the load on the meter.

§11 fits a 2 kW, 15 L Wi-Fi geyser in each bathroom (2 Oct). Four elements heating at 7 AM would draw ~8 kW before the VRF and kitchen. On DG, with a possibly lower per-flat cap, that may trip the meter (cap unverified). Staggered app schedules keep it to one element (~2 kW) at a time.

**Mitigation, no wiring:**
- Stagger the four Wi-Fi schedules ~20 minutes apart, e.g. Master 6:00, then the next bathroom 6:20 (§11).
- Schedule a bathroom only on days someone uses it. Never let all four start at 7 AM.
- Don't put a plug timer on any geyser supply. §11 notes AO Smith's manual forbids external switching.

The old note's "load-limiting contactor" is electrical work and is dropped. Installation of the geysers on the existing points is the brand's job (§11).

### A20.4 Water pressure

The flat is two floors below the top of a 44-floor tower. If the roof tank feeds it by gravity alone, the static head is only a few metres, roughly 1 bar or less. Many towers fit a booster system for the top floors for this reason. Find out which applies (A20.2 question list).

**Test it, free.** At each shower, with every other tap shut, time how long a 10 L bucket takes to fill. Under ~6 L/min (100 seconds or more) a rain shower will feel weak. Hand showers and bucket baths cope.

If it is weak, raise it with the facility first. A flat-level booster pump is both plumbing and electrical work and may need RWA permission. Don't fit a pressure-reducing valve; at the top of the stack there is nothing to reduce.

### A20.5 Surge protection

**What a Type 2 SPD does.** It sits in the flat's distribution board and clamps voltage spikes on every circuit. Type 3 strips protect single devices. The RWA's lightning protection certificate (IEC 62305 / IS 2309) protects the structure, not your electronics.

**Is it worth one electrician visit? Optional.** Do it only if Homz's electrician is already on site for the false-ceiling lights and the DB has spare modules for the SPD and its MCB (about 2 + 1 for single-phase, 4 + 1 for three-phase). Then the single-phase part costs ₹770 and the labour ~₹1–3k. Evidence that DG changeover surges kill household electronics is thin. Treat it as cheap insurance, not a proven need.

| Item | ₹ | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| [Elmex SPPV1+1T2-320N](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B09KBVKRCT), Type 2, 1+1 pole, 320 V, 20/40 kA | 770 | Indian DIN-rail brand. Single-phase only |
| Elmex Type 2, 3+1 pole, same rating | not found | Three-phase. No Amazon.in listing found on 2026-10-01. Ask Homz's electrician to quote and source it |
| Backup MCB, as the SPD's datasheet specifies | ~300–600 | Unverified price |
| Electrician time, via Homz | ~1,000–3,000 | Unverified; add to the Homz electrical line |
| Schneider Acti9 iPRD 40 kA ([listing](https://www.amazon.in/dp/B019IVQI0W)) | 32,828 | Three-pole commercial part. Overkill for a single-phase flat |

**Conditions.** A 2,880 sq ft flat with its own VRF outdoor unit may well be on three-phase supply. Confirm the phase (A20.2 question list, or count the incoming MCB poles at the DB) and buy the matching pole count. The SPD is only as good as the earth. Ask the §18 snag inspector to confirm earth-loop impedance below 1 Ω at the DB. Replace the SPD when its window shows red.

If the DB has no room, skip it. Don't add a new enclosure. Use Type 3 strips at the TV (§3) and the network gear (§2).

### A20.6 Water layer: what stays and what goes

| Item | Status | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Braided-steel inlet hoses on the washer and RO | **Keep** | The appliance installer fits them at install. No plumbing work |
| Water-hammer arrestors at appliance tees | Dropped | Plumbing work. Braided hoses handle the hose-burst risk |
| Smart flow-sensing shutoff valve on the main | Dropped to Optional, §8 | Cutting into the inlet is plumbing work. High-rise bathrooms may not share one inlet anyway |
| Pressure-reducing valve | Dropped | Nothing to reduce at floor 42 |
| Flat-level booster pump | Only after the facility answers | Plumbing plus power, may need RWA permission |

### Sources (A20)

- Builder specification and room sizes: [[my-home]]
- Elmex Type 2 SPD: https://www.amazon.in/dp/B09KBVKRCT ; Schneider iPRD listing: https://www.amazon.in/dp/B019IVQI0W
- RERA Act 2016, s.14(3): PRS India copy, checked 2026-10-01: <https://prsindia.org/files/bills_acts/acts_parliament/2016/the-real-estate-(regulation-and-development)-act,-2016.pdf> ; India Code (unreachable from here on 2026-10-01): https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/2158/1/a2016-16.pdf
- Geyser models and sizing: §11 brief and A11
- Old note A20 for the appliance-heater list and the DOE/PNNL humidity reference (not re-checked)

## A21. RWA, deliveries and building ops — detail

**Answer:** Get the fit-out rules in writing this week and hand them to Homz. Join the association and check your parking bay. Everything else is a small habit or an AGM ask.

### A21.1 What changed from the old note

| Old | Now | Why |
|---|---|---|
| "Empty AC drip trays weekly" for mosquitoes | **VRF indoor units drain through pipes.** There's no tray to empty. Empty planter saucers and check the balcony drain weekly in monsoon | Ducted and cassette VRF units pump condensate to a drain line (§20) |
| Booster pump, ERV and grille rules in the charter | **Dropped from the ask** | §20 cut the booster pump and ERV. §8 does add a full-height SS316 invisible grille on the balconies, so the charter ask is the association's written OK for it (A21.2) |
| Rooftop solar and property-tax rebates as main-body items | **Optional AGM asks** (A21.5) | They need the association, not you, and pay back slowly |
| Doorbell automation flashing a lamp | Moved to §1 | Smart-home topic |

### A21.2 Fit-out rules: get these in writing

Ask the facility office (or the association, once formed) for the written rules. Give Homz a copy. Homz's delay clause voids if site rules restrict work, so the rider must strike that (A7, rider ask 5).

| Item | Typical Hyderabad high-rise answer | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| Refundable deposit | ₹50,000–2,00,000 (estimate) | Photograph the lift, lobby and corridor before and after. That's where deductions happen |
| Working hours and drilling window | e.g. 9 am–6 pm, drilling 10 am–1 pm, no Sundays (varies) | Sets Homz's install days |
| Service-lift booking | Through MyGate or the facility desk, sometimes paid | Every panel and stone slab goes up this way |
| Debris | Per-bag fee and an approved vendor | Put it in Homz's scope |
| Labour passes | ID and sometimes police verification | Homz supplies the list |
| Balcony rules | Net colour, grille type, anything visible on the façade | Decides §8's invisible grille and any pigeon net |
| Sprinklers and detectors | Only the building's fire contractor moves them | Homz's false ceiling must keep them working (§8) |
| Who can drill the slab | Usually "no core cutting" | Matches §12's no-through-slab rule |

### A21.3 Day-one admin

- **Parking.** Check the bay number in the sale deed matches the bay painted on the floor. Collect stickers or RFID tags. Mis-allotment is common and hurts resale.
- **Association.** Register as an owner. RERA §19(9) requires you to take part in forming it.
- **Maintenance and corpus.** Set up NACH auto-debit.
- **Fit-out labour toilet.** Tell Homz which bathroom its crew may use. Default to the common bathroom by the Drawing room. Keep the Master and the bathroom next to BR-03 (the Gurudwara) off-limits.
- **Neighbours.** Introduce yourself to the two flats sharing your walls before drilling starts. They're your noise and leak neighbours.

### A21.4 Ongoing building life

| Topic | Do this | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Pigeons | Wait and watch for 4 weeks after move-in. If they nest, fit a transparent UV-stabilised HDPE net inside the opening (Solimo bird net ₹839 + ~₹15–35/sqft fitting). If the rules ban nets, use SS spikes on ledges | Ultrasonic devices don't work. A 50 mm invisible grille (§8) doesn't stop pigeons. Floor-42 wind tears plastic net clips, so use SS-wire tensioned edges, and never net a slider you'd escape through. Droppings carry a fungal lung risk, so don't let them build up |
| Mosquitoes | Empty planter saucers weekly in monsoon (Jun–Oct). Keep balcony drains clear | Aedes breeds in clean standing water at any height |
| Waste | Confirm the pickup times and wet/dry segregation | The kitchen bins are §12's |
| Deliveries | Send MyGate alerts to both adults. Leave an insulated bag tagged B4206 at the guard desk for frozen orders | Quick-commerce riders leave fast |
| Exterior glass | Ask the facility how often the gondola cleans floor-42 glass, and the charge | It's not an owner task at this height |
| Overhead tanks | Read the AGM minutes for a 6-monthly cleaning record | Industry practice, not a standard number |

### A21.5 AGM asks (Optional, low effort)

- **Group net metering for common-area solar.** TGERC Regulation 1 of 2025 allows group and virtual net metering. Per-flat solar fails on roof area: one roof over 44 floors. PM Surya Ghar pays RWAs ₹18,000/kWp for common facilities including EV charging, up to 500 kWp at 3 kWp per house. Allocation is first-come, first-served. (Carried from the old note's 2026-09 research, not re-read this round.)
- **Rainwater harvesting certificate.** G.O.Ms.No.168 (2012) requires it on plots of 300 m² and above, sized at 6 m³ per 100 m² of roof. Ask for the inspection certificate.
- **Property-tax rebates** for solar heating or lighting and for doing both waste-water recycling and RWH (10% each, per the old note's reading of the building rules, **unverified** under the new corporation).

### Sources (A21)

- RERA Act 2016 §19(9): https://up-rera.in/pdf/reraact.pdf
- PM Surya Ghar portal: https://pmsuryaghar.gov.in/
- Solimo bird net listing: https://www.amazon.in/dp/B0DY4PVPLM
- Carried from the old note without re-reading this round: TGERC Regulation 1 of 2025; G.O.Ms.No.168 MA&UD (07.04.2012) Rule 15(a)(vii)

## A22. Maintenance cadence — full table

**Answer:** One shared calendar, with each row as a repeating event. Put a laminated A5 copy inside the utility cupboard so household help can work from it. The table lists only what this guide now recommends. Rows for cut items (ERV, flow valve, booster pump, whole-flat softener unless bought) are gone. Exterior re-caulking stays with the RWA.

| Monthly | Ceiling fans (§11) | Dust the blades | Household |
