## A23. Sequencing — the full phase narrative

Homz Interio runs the build, so the sequence follows Homz's own process: measure, design sign-off, factory production, installation, handover. Your job is to fill in around it: decisions Homz needs, the few trades Homz doesn't do, and checks at each payment gate. The dates assume design sign-off by **15 Nov 2026**. The timing argument is in [A27.5](#a275-occupancy-timing--when-the-prakash-and-move-in-can-honestly-happen).

**Plan housing for the slip, not the target.** Target mid-April 2027. Keep your current housing flexible to end-August 2027 (the slip case in A27.5), because Homz's reviews show slips of months.

### The phases at a glance

| Phase | When (plan) | Homz does | You do | Gate to the next phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **0 Possession and contract** | Now to mid-Oct 2026 | Site measurement | Sign the rider ([A7](#the-contract-rider-twelve-asks-to-paste)). Close the builder's snag list, photograph every room, get the as-built MEP drawings. Record the lift size and the VRF indoor-unit types. Get the RWA charter. Check the GSTIN. Phone SGPC and a Granthi | Rider signed, baseline recorded |
| **1 Design freeze** | Mid-Oct to 15 Nov 2026 | 3D renders, 2D drawings, itemised BOQ priced against the §12 sheet | Decide appliance models (cut-outs), ceiling extent, light positions and any ceiling cable. Approve material samples. Give Homz the builder's paint brand and shade | You sign the renders, BOQ and material annexure (40% gate) |
| **2 Factory production, site prep** | 16 Nov 2026 to Jan 2027 | Panels made in the factory. On site: surface protection, ceiling frame, electrical points, boards after the 3-day notice, then ceiling finish | Any Ethernet or speaker cable goes in before boards close. Keep the VRF off and grilles sealed on dusty days | Materials delivered and checked against the BOQ (70% gate) |
| **3 Installation** | Jan to early Mar 2027 | Carcass, shutters, worktop, lights, BR-03 joinery **first**, then the rest. Touch-ups | Weekly site visit and photo check. Geyser installers on days Homz isn't in that bathroom. Glass partitions last | Installation complete (95% gate) |
| **4 Handover and cure** | Mid-Mar to early Apr 2027 | Snag fixes, post-works clean, deep cleaning | Snag walk against the Phase 0 photos. VRF filter clean. About 2 weeks' ventilated cure. Purifiers, loose furniture, blinds and devices arrive. Insurance active | Snags closed (final 5%), cure complete, BR-03 ready |
| **5 Prakash and move-in** | **Mid-April 2027** (target) | Warranty service | Prakash, then move in. Register every warranty within its window | — |
| **6 Ongoing** | From move-in | Warranty claims | VRF AMC, pest rounds, filter changes, the maintenance calendar (§20–§22) | — |

### Phase 0: possession and contract (now)

- **Sign the rider before paying anything more.** Asks 1 to 4, 6 and 10 are the minimum ([A7](#the-contract-rider-twelve-asks-to-paste)).
- **Record the baseline before Homz's crew enters.** Get the builder's snag list closed in writing. Photograph every room, floor, fitting and wall, dated. Ask the builder for the as-built MEP drawings (plumbing, electrical, conduit runs). These protect your RERA defect claims and tell Homz where not to drill.
- **Measure the service lift**: car width, depth and height, door clear opening, the flat's entry opening. Give the figures to Homz in writing.
- **Get the RWA fit-out charter in writing**: hours, drilling window, deposit, debris route, lift booking, labour passes. Give Homz a copy.
- **Get Homz's holiday schedule in writing**, with milestones set around 11 Oct (Bathukamma), 18–20 Oct (Dussehra), 6–11 Nov (Diwali), 15–16 Nov (Chhath, on the freeze date itself, so hold the freeze meeting before 6 Nov), 24 Nov (Gurpurab), 25 Dec to 1 Jan, and **Sankranti, about 14–16 Jan 2027** (exact dates **unverified**). Sankranti is Telangana's main harvest festival, and site labour leaves for up to a week.
- **Phone SGPC Amritsar and a local Granthi about the Saroop.** The lead time is unknown and it gates the move-in.
- **Ask the builder** for the slab type and the VRF commissioning date.
- **Record the VRF indoor units** in each room: type (ducted, cassette or hi-wall), position, any builder bulkhead or ceiling, and drain-pump locations. Get the VRF installer's written OK on any ceiling work around their units. This sets the false-ceiling area and hatch count.
- **Map every builder point**: each 16 A socket, the gas outlet, the sink supply and waste, the washer inlet and drain. Photograph them. Appliances and the hob cut-out are placed on these, not the other way round.
- **Write the slippage plan now.** At 3–6 months late, these move: quote validity, the Path date, the school term, and your current housing. Housing that ends before the flat is ready is the most expensive item.

### Phase 1: design freeze (by 15 Nov 2026)

Homz can't start production until every cut-out and position is fixed. So these decisions move forward into October:

1. **Appliance models and sizes** (§4). The hob, chimney, built-in oven, fridge and RO set the kitchen cut-outs. Each heavy appliance goes on a 16 A point; if none reaches, Homz adds one without chasing a finished wall. The hob cut-out sits over the existing gas outlet: only the PNG utility may move it.
2. **Kitchen platform**: keep the builder's and build under it, unless it's unsound (§9).
3. **False-ceiling extent per room**, set out from the recorded VRF units, with a hatch at every concealed unit and drain pump. Perimeter cove keeps the full ceiling height (measure it: ~2.9–3.0 m, **unverified**).
4. **Light positions**: cove runs, downlights, wardrobe profiles, art spots.
5. **Any low-voltage cable in the ceiling.** Decide whether §2's access points or §3's speakers go in the ceiling. If they don't, skip the cabling.
6. **Furniture layout**, so the TV wall, desk and bed positions match the points.
7. **Window covering per opening** (§7.2), so Homz leaves pelmet space for a battery driver (§7.2). Get Homz's quote for curtains and glass at the same time.

### Phase 2: production and site prep (Nov 2026 to Jan 2027)

- **Protection first.** Floor sheets and fitting covers go down before any tool comes in (rider ask 11).
- **VRF off, grilles sealed** on every day with gypsum, cutting or sanding work ([A20](#a20-building-systems-hvac-electrical-surge-and-water--detail)). Don't run purifiers on site yet: gypsum dust clogs a HEPA set in days.
- **Drill only after a scan** on any wall next to a bathroom or the kitchen, and on the slab soffit before ceiling hangers go in. Depth-limited anchors only.
- **Ceiling-close hold point**: Homz gives 3 days' notice before boarding each room. Pull any cable then. Ceiling finish follows: tape, putty, primer, 2 coats in the builder's shade.
- **The 70% gate:** before lamination, Homz sends dated photos or video of your sheets with mill stamp, grade and CM/L number, plus the mill or dealer invoice. At site, check a cut edge, keep one offcut per board type, and match hardware boxes to the BOQ article numbers ([A7 gates](#payment-gates-tied-to-inspections)).

### Phase 3: installation (Jan to early Mar 2027)

- **BR-03 goes first**, then gets closed off and kept clean.
- **Hookups on existing points**: sink by Homz's plumber, RO by its installer, washer and dryer by theirs, hob by the PNG utility ([A7](#plumbing-and-gas-touches)).
- **Geyser hookups** by the brand's installer on existing points. Book them for days Homz isn't working in that bathroom, so Homz has no "customer vendor on site" excuse.
- **Glass partitions go in last**, after the dusty work, whoever fits them.
- **The 95% gate:** every unit fitted and every shutter aligned. Drawers run full-extension under load. Lights work and the RCCB doesn't trip. Ceiling finished and every point touched up.

### Phase 4: handover and cure (mid-Mar to early Apr 2027)

- Snag walk with a printed list and the Phase 0 photos. The final 5% falls due when it's closed (rider ask 4).
- Post-works clean and deep cleaning by Homz.
- **VRF filter clean** once the dust work is done. Dry mode is fine from then on.
- **Ventilated cure, about 2 weeks**, windows open and fans running, before anyone sleeps there and before the Saroop enters.
- Purifiers, loose furniture, blinds, smart devices and AV go in during the cure.
- Insurance active before the first appliance delivery. Asset inventory photographed (§15).

### Phase 5: Prakash and move-in (mid-April 2027, target)

- Ask the Granthi for a date after the cure ends. Vaisakhi falls in mid-April 2027 (exact date **unverified**; confirm with the Granthi).
- Register each appliance warranty within its window. Save the Homz Warranty Letter and the Club Prime "Total Cover" registration (within 90 days of purchase).

### Phase 6: ongoing

VRF AMC, surge and water upkeep (§20). RWA, deliveries, pest and pigeon routines (§21). The maintenance calendar (§22). Homz warranty claims go to its CRM in writing, with photos.

## A24. Verification checklist — the full list

**Answer:** This is the one checklist, as of 1 Oct 2026. It's ordered by the Homz schedule: now, before Homz's design freeze, before the false ceiling closes, before move-in, after move-in. Homz runs the build, and the flat needs no plumbing, electrical, painting or waterproofing work, so every item that assumed otherwise is gone (A24.7). Each line names its section.

### A24.0 Next actions — full list

*Moved from the main body on 2026-10-02 and updated. The dated and blocking items (26QB, snag list, rider, BOQ and GSTIN, lift, smart-switch survey, sit-tests) are ticked in the main body's [Next actions](#next-actions) and not repeated here. Homz's design freeze is planned for 15 Nov.*

**Today and this week**
- [ ] Record the baseline for RERA claims: dated photos, as-built MEP drawings, every 16 A point, gas outlet, geyser point and VRF unit.
- [ ] Measure the lift car and door, ceiling heights, windows, the platform underside, shower zones and the utility. Compass every window to confirm the plan's north, east and south faces. Check the Master's west wall for a window.
- [ ] Ask the facility manager in writing: fit-out rules, fibre ISPs, intercom, sprinklers, refuge floors, supply phase, DG cap, slab type, stretcher lift, Zeus Club rooms.
- [ ] Set auto-recharge on the electricity meter; learn the PNG top-up. Read the PNG operator (BGL or Megha Gas) and its emergency number off the meter for the §19 card.
- [ ] Before labour enters: bind Bharat Griha Raksha (fit-out disclosed), get Homz's insurance certificates, and rekey the main door.
- [ ] Flush the tanks and test both water lines at IHS (₹3,800). Hardness decides the softener.
- [ ] Ask the Granthi at Gurudwara Sahib Ameerpet how a Saroop is issued and what Manji height suits a reader.
- [ ] In October: check pending property tax with Cyberabad MC, book Kabir's Aadhaar update, patch the Flint 2 to 4.9.0+.

**Before Homz freezes the design (15 Nov)**
- [ ] Get Homz's dated schedule in writing with festival weeks absorbed: Diwali–Chhath 6–16 Nov, 25 Dec–1 Jan, Sankranti ~14–16 Jan. Hold the freeze meeting before 6 Nov. Ask 5 only works against a written date.
- [ ] Fix appliance models on existing points and hand over the [A4.4 slots](#a44-slot-sizes-for-the-homz-drawings). Before buying the Bosch hob, get the stone vendor's and the PNG operator's yes.
- [ ] Hand Homz the room plans below: shoe cabinet, crockery unit, pendant point, TV duct, BR-03 shelf, closet hinge, utility overhang, robot niche, medicine and fire-box drawers.
- [ ] Add to the BOQ: VRF hatches, coves clear of sprinklers, the gas-detector lead, tall units fixed to masonry, wet-wall wardrobe backs ([A8.10](#a810-homz-contract-asks-from-this-section)).
- [ ] Put the glass spec and curtain line in the BOQ. Get a direct glass quote, and curtain quotes from Bhavani Furnishings and Neo Fabrique on one spec sheet.
- [ ] Price the 3 AP spots, Cat6 runs and 4 art spots as BOQ lines. Get the VRF installer's OK on ceiling work.
- [ ] Ask Homz to price Century Club Prime over Sainik 710, LEGRABOX by name and Kesseböhmer LeMans corners; and to draw the BR-01 library, BR-02 storage bench (Harbour laminate samples) and Master south bedside shelf.
- [ ] Decide on the Dining ceiling speakers. They need work before boarding.

**Before the false ceiling closes**
- [ ] Hold the 70% gate: photos of your sheets with mill stamp, plus the invoice. Keep an offcut per board.
- [ ] On Homz's 3-day notice: scan the slab, photograph the Cat6, fit the pendant member and gas-detector lead. Water-test each VRF drain tray; check hatches and sprinkler heads.

**Before move-in**
- [ ] Fit the grille (keep the mill certificate) and the 6 grouped smoke alarms. Hearing-test them from the Master, doors shut.
- [ ] Order appliances ~2 weeks ahead from GST-invoice sellers. Only Bosch service installs Bosch. No dishwasher.
- [ ] Fit the glass last. Register every appliance in its delivery week, with the invoice. Set geysers to 55 °C, Kabir's bath to 50 °C, and stagger the four schedules 20 minutes apart.
- [ ] Close Homz's snags against the Phase 0 photos. Air the flat 2 weeks (4 if it still smells), purifiers on high, before the Prakash.
- [ ] Book fibre 4 weeks ahead. Move jewellery to a locker. Raise Contents cover before the first delivery.

**After move-in**
- [ ] After a month, read PM2.5 and speed-test Wi-Fi in every room. Add the third AP only if a room tests under 200 Mbps.
- [ ] Walk the stairs with Kabir and time it. Read the closet hygrometers weekly in the monsoon. Start the [A22](#a22-maintenance-cadence--full-table) calendar.

### A24.1 Now (this week)

- **Form 26QB for every builder instalment.** The August one was due 30 Sep 2026. File any missing one today with interest and late fee. Check each covered parking, club and other charges paid to the builder, not only the base price. The old Act's one-month penalty window (to 30 Oct) has an **unverified** new-Act equivalent, so don't wait. Form 16B to the builder by 15 Oct (§18, A18.4).
- **Chase the 21 Sep handover snag list** ([[u-handover]]) in writing; the builder's 30 days end about 21 Oct. If none was served, serve one now by email plus registered post or a stamped acknowledgement. Re-photograph every wall Homz will build against (§18, A18.3).
- **Request documents under RERA §19(5):** OC, fire NOC, approved plan, as-built electrical and plumbing drawings, warranty cards, slab-stage termite certificate. Add the waterproofing warranty endorsed to you, the ponding-test report, the PNG commissioning certificate, and the LPS and earth-pit report (§18, A18.3).
- **Rekey the builder's main-door cylinder now.** Give Homz one logged key, signed for by the named site engineer (§8). Keep the log in the family cloud.
- **Fit-out rules in writing** from the facility office. Copy to Homz (§21, A21.2).
- **Homz's insurance certificates** before labour enters: contractors' all-risk and employees' compensation (rider ask 9). Add to ask 9 third-party liability of at least ₹50 lakh, including sprinkler leakage, naming the Association and Tower B, valid to handover. Your own policy excludes sprinkler leaks caused by the fit-out (§7, §15, A15.2).
- **Check for an existing policy.** Ask your home-loan lender and the builder whether either already insured B4206 (A15.3).
- **Bharat Griha Raksha building cover** bound on the shell and builder fittings, with the fit-out disclosed (§15, A15.3).
- **Homz rider signed** before the next payment: the 12 asks (minimum: asks 1–4 and 10), plus the liability line above (§7, A7).
- **Check Homz's GSTIN** on gst.gov.in before paying. One source showed the Telangana GSTIN as cancelled (A0, **unverified**).
- **Phone SGPC Amritsar and a local Granthi about the Saroop.** The lead time is unknown and gates the Prakash and move-in date (§7.3C, A27.5).
- **Measure the service lift:** car width, depth, height and door clear opening. Give the numbers to Homz and every furniture seller (§7, §7.1, §4).
- **Photograph every builder point:** hob, chimney, RO, fridge, utility, each geyser point, each shower zone and exhaust (§4, §9, §11).
- **Unscrew one room faceplate:** coax or Ethernet, and does the cable slide in a conduit (§2).
- **Ask the facility in writing:** sanctioned kW, DG cap per flat, which ISPs have fibre in Tower B, bore-water softening, whether the electricity is a TGSPDCL service or a facility sub-meter (§2, §5, §18, §20).
- **Prepaid meters:** auto-recharge and low-balance alerts on both. Homz's tools run on your meter (§20, A18.3).

### A24.2 October 2026

- **Kabir's Aadhaar biometric update** at an Aadhaar centre (§18, A18.6).
- **Property-tax mutation** with Cyberabad MC, Serilingampally zone. Ask whether a 2026-27 half-year bill is pending and who owes it until the PTIN moves to you. Get the office's answer in writing (§18, A18.5).
- **Electricity and gas accounts** into your name (§18).
- **Parking bay** matches the deed; stickers or RFID collected (§21).
- **Join the association;** NACH for maintenance (§21).
- **Scan every paper** to the family cloud; originals in the fire box (§16).
- **Water test** on both lines, after running each tap 5 minutes (§5).

### A24.3 Before Homz's design freeze

- **Lockable top compartment** for medicines, above 1.5 m (§17).
- **Locking wardrobe base** sized for the fire box (SentrySafe CHW20221, exterior 391 × 363 × 168 mm) and, if you keep jewellery at home, a bolted Godrej Forte Pro 40 L (§12, §15, §16).
- **Counter height** from the main cook's elbow, and the dado tile edge (§9).
- **Appliance cut-out sizes** final: hob, chimney, sink, fridge niche, microwave niche (§4, §9).
- **TV wall:** 50 mm duct, ply backing; locate the builder's TV socket (Homz extends it inside the panel only if needed) (§3).
- **Robot-vac dock niche** next to an existing socket (§6).
- **Router shelf** at the fibre entry, vented, next to an existing socket (§2).
- **VRF service hatches** in the BOQ at every indoor unit (§20).
- **Shower glass spec** in the BOQ: 10 mm toughened, IS 2553 mark, SS304 hardware (§11).
- **Board, hardware and laminate named** per line (§7, §12).
- **Borate treatment** on all joinery as a named line (§7).
- **Curtain choice per window.** Confirm each window's compass face on site: the plan reads north, east and south (§7.2).
- **Ask the builder in writing** whether fixing into a bathroom-adjacent wall affects the waterproofing warranty (§11).

### A24.4 Before the false ceiling closes

- **Homz's 3-day ceiling-close notice** received (§7, A7).
- **Cat6 to each ceiling AP** pulled, in conduit with no power cable (§2). Who: a network installer, or Homz's electrician pulling cable you supply. The BR-01 desk run uses the existing conduit if the cable slides.
- **Sprinklers and detectors** still clear. Only the building's fire contractor moves a head (§8).
- **Slab scan before any ceiling anchor;** nothing goes through the slab (§12).

### A24.5 Before move-in (about April 2027)

- **Two-week airing** after Homz installs, before anyone sleeps there and before the Saroop enters (A27.5).
- **Geysers fitted** by the brand installer on existing points, with the safety valve (§11).
- **Glass partitions:** a Homz BOQ line, or an outside fabricator only if its quote beats Homz (§11).
- **Contents cover raised before the first owner-bought delivery** (hob, chimney, RO). Homz signs a receipt for each item and holds it at Homz's risk, or deliver in the last fit-out week (§15, A15.3).
- **Building cover raised to include the joinery** at Homz handover: ask the insurer to raise the declared rate per m² (§15, A15.3).
- **Collect every key at Homz handover,** check the log, then fit the smart lock or rekey again (§8).
- **Valuables and personal liability** added at move-in. Jewellery goes to a bank locker or the bolted safe, because the policy pays for theft only within 7 days of an insured event. Burglary cover: an add-on if any of the 3 insurers sells one, otherwise a separate policy (§15, A15.3).
- **Video inventory** and the asset sheet (§15, A15.4).
- **First-aid kit** stocked; numbers on the §19 card (Rainbow 1800 2122 first). Dengue card on the medicine door: no ibuprofen or aspirin for a June–November fever (§17, A17.3).
- **Fire plan card, grab-bag and escape hoods** (§19).
- **Spouse admin rights** on the lock, Alexa, Aqara and Tapo (§16).
- **Password manager** set up with emergency access (§16).
- **Packers booked,** with transit insurance and a lift slot (§18).
- **Purifiers bought** now, not during carpentry (§5).

### A24.6 After move-in

- **Register every warranty** in the delivery week (§16).
- **Homz's first warranty visit** at 3 months: hinges and runners (A22).
- **Nominations and wills** done (§16).
- **Shared maintenance calendar** live (A22).
- **Pigeon watch** for 4 weeks; net only if they nest and the rules allow it (§21).
- **First AGM:** group net metering, RWH certificate, tank-cleaning record (A21.5).

All-in Must + Should ~₹50–59L. Lock the figure once Homz prices the §12 sheet |

Small additions are fine: false-ceiling and profile lights, and a 16 A point where an appliance needs one, run through the false ceiling or inside the joinery ([A7](#electrical-touches-the-flat-still-needs)) | Homz's electrician |

| **Plumbing and waterproofing** | Done | Hookups only, on existing points: sink, RO, washer, dryer, geysers. The hob connects through the PNG utility only | [A7 plumbing and gas touches](#plumbing-and-gas-touches) |
| **Painting** | Done | No repaint. The finish on the new false ceiling and touch-ups at every cove line, point and fixing are in Homz's scope, matched to the builder's brand and shade | Homz ([A7 scope matrix](#scope-matrix-what-to-confirm-in-the-18l-quote)) |
| **Flooring** | Vitrified in living areas (1000×1000 mm) and bedrooms (800×800) except the Master (laminate). Anti-skid ceramic in the toilets | Rugs to zone rooms. No floor laying | — |
| **Kitchen** | A platform, U-shaped on the brochure (confirm on site that it is built). Ceramic dado to 2 ft above it. Chimney and RO points. PNG gas with a prepaid meter | If it is built and sound, keep it and build carcasses under it. Chimney, RO and hob units. The hob must be PNG, not LPG | Homz (§9), you (§4) |
| **Water** | Separate municipal and bore lines | Test both before choosing RO or a softener | §5 |
| **Doors** | Teak main door. uPVC sliders to the balconies, with a mosquito-mesh track provision | Measure the teak leaf before ordering a lock. Count the sill rails before buying mesh | §8, §11 |
| **Windows** | uPVC, with mild-steel safety grills | Check the grill gaps (≤75 mm) and any rust before deciding to keep them | §8 |
| **Power backup** | 100% DG backup, dual-source prepaid meter | Ask what the DG powers, and the per-flat cap | §20 |

### Now out of scope

These earlier recommendations needed work the flat doesn't need. They're dropped or demoted.

|---|---|---|

## A26. Research log and provenance

How the picks were built and checked, so the confidence level is legible. This is the trust
ledger: one line per pass, plus the negative findings a future pass must not silently undo. The
main body carries only the live recommendation and its caveat.

- **2026-09-09 — initial pass.** First buy-list across all systems from the brief + [[my-home]] specs.
- **2026-09-10 — VFM pass + 7-agent audit.** Added a value alternative to every pick, then cross-checked model codes and India availability against manufacturer pages.
- **2026-09-13 — 5-stream pass.** Rebuilt the hob as gas+induction (no single-unit hybrid exists in India); three-way picks for fridge and RO; replaced the interiors execution route with a hands-off model.
- **2026-09-14 — verify-and-expand pass.** Verified a second-opinion document section by section, folded in what held, removed its scaffolding. Added §9 (worktop/sinks/taps), §10 (lamps), §11 (bathrooms and the rest).
- **2026-09-18 — deep + wide enrichment (6 agents, 2 waves).** Added §12–§22 and §8.1. New HIGH gaps neither content stream caught: the sanctioned-electrical-load calc and termite pre-treatment of new joinery.
- **2026-09-19 — targeted refinement (3 streams).** Added prices for termite, false ceiling, grille and load enhancement. **Single-child correction:** one 6-year-old boy, so all nursery/crib/infant-monitor scope was removed. Added §7.2 (smart blinds per room).
- **2026-09-19 — room programme + interior design (6 agents + 3-agent review).** Owner set the final room programme; added §7.3 (detailed designs A–F) and the modern-Indian-minimalist design language. The Gurudwara section follows the SGPC Sikh Rehat Maryada. Review wave caught a closet that could not physically fit (48" → single-sided) and the Master being an unprotected child sleep-room.
- **2026-09-19 — loose-furniture pass (2 agents).** Verified **Alankaram** (names genuine solid teak/ash/oak *and* has a Hyderabad showroom — the guide's hardest gap) and **AKFD/Anantaya**.
- **2026-09-20 — orientation correction (owner's floor plan).** **An earlier pass affirmed a west exposure in error.** The plan shows the west edge is the lift-lobby and corridor core, so there is **no west glazing**; the exterior glazing opens east and south, with BR-02 (N) and BR-03 (NE) on north light. The solar drivers are east morning sun (low-angle, head-on, hard to shade) and high-angle south sun. Recommendations survived a relabel, not a rewrite. **This is the largest error the note has made — do not let "west" return.** Room-level compass is read from the plan, not measured.
- **2026-09-20 — deep audit + timeline + execution (4 analysis + 2 adversarial review).** Rebuilt the spine into six phases with a *Timeline:* cue per section; added the scored 6-route execution matrix (routes 2 and 3 are the only two that clear the bar).
- **2026-09-22 — signal-to-noise + correctness (2 analysis + 2 critic agents).** Removed pass-history narration and diff-narration from the body. Harmonised water pressure to ~1 bar (booster pump, **no PRV at this floor**). Added the Sikh house-blessing Path as first occupation, nomination-vs-will, and PTIN/utility account transfers. **Protected list for future tidiers: no pick, price, model code, VFM alt, hard requirement, or UNVERIFIED / not-in-India / confirm-on-site caveat may be cut.**
- **2026-09-25 — deep research pass (5 analysis + 3 critic agents, with a rebuttal round).** Method: analysis agents proposed, critic agents attacked, analysis agents conceded or defended against the file, then findings were verified against primary sources before landing. **Corrections that changed the plan:** RERA **§19(10)** caps possession at **two months from the OC date**, so §18's leverage is the written snag list under §14(3), not withholding possession (verified verbatim from the Gazette text); the code **exempts balconied apartment towers** from the generic refuge rule, putting the nearest refuge *below* floor 42, and a newer edition, **SP 7 : 2026**, exists on BIS's site — **but this "NBC-2016 is superseded by SP 7 : 2026, Part F" claim is retracted as written**: its parts structure and clause numbering were never retrieved, the agent that reported reading it in full on archive.org had not, and §19 correctly says do not cite it until you hold it; "through-slab" anchoring was replaced with a structural-drawing-and-scan requirement (post-tensioned slab risk); peripheral false ceiling leaves **no plenum** for in-ceiling speakers, ceiling APs or centred pendants; the **LG 614L fridge (914 mm) does not pass an 800 mm lift door** and no jumbo stone slab enters a residential lift; Bharat Griha Raksha defaults Contents to **20% / ₹10L max** and **does not cover burglary of an empty flat**; four geysers plus the MiniBot is **11 kW** of water heating, so "staggered" needed a mechanism; the flat has a **prepaid electricity** meter, so a zero balance is an outage the DG does not bridge; October is a **monsoon month** in Hyderabad, so the post-monsoon caulk moves to Dec–Jan and the pest round to May; the air figure was re-derived from ~26 to **~40 µg/m³** (IQAir's own annual series), lifting filters to ₹35–50k/yr; **Alexa+ costs ₹2,000/month** in India unless the household is on full Prime; the windows **already carry MS safety grills**, so ~₹12–18k of §8's grille estimate may be buying them twice. **Fabrications and dead sources caught:** "IHS Lab Hyderabad" does not exist (that domain is a US cannabis processor — use an NABL lab such as Vimta); **Astberg**'s domain is parked for sale; there is **no 65" TCL C8K** (the 65" set is the C8L); **TL-SG2008P is End of Sale**; **Samsung HW-Q800F reads "Not for sale"** in India; the **Yale Luna Elite+** publishes no door thickness and no mortise designation, so Kyra Pro is the only verified thick-leaf lock. **Corrections to earlier passes:** the 2026-09-18 line claiming "Coway does have an India channel" is **retracted** — `coway.co.in` still redirects to an unrelated domain, so §5's disqualification stands. **Do not print the ~54 m aerial-ladder figure** — it could not be confirmed and every Hyderabad Fire Services domain is dead. Two negative findings moved from this log into §7.2, where they are used: **Vista and MAC are unverifiable as smart-blind brands**, and **Hindware and Somany make no blinds** (both appear here as *tile* brands, which makes the confusion likelier). **Caught by the critic wave *within* this pass, after the findings had already landed — recorded because each is the kind of error that re-enters silently.** A research agent reported reading *SP 7 : 2026* in full on archive.org; **no such item exists there**, and the refuge quote it attached actually comes from **NBC 2016 Part 4, Annex E cl. E-4** — the citation was restored and SP 7:2026 is now flagged as existing but unread. A "you descend, you never climb" instruction was deleted: it chained an unmeasured 126 m onto an assumption that Tower B actually built refuges at 60/90/120 m, when the exemption is permissive at plan approval and the tower may have none. **A direction of travel in a fire SOP must come from the drawing.** A live air reading ("AQI 122 / PM2.5 44") was **not reproducible** and was replaced with IQAir's 2022–23 annual figures plus its monthly series, which also showed the annual mean is the wrong number to size off — the within-year swing is 3.2×, so sizing moved to a **winter design load of ~55–60 µg/m³**. "IHS Lab Hyderabad" was briefly written off as fabricated because one agent checked the look-alike `ihslab.com`; **IHS is real** (the Institute of Health Systems, `ihs.org.in`, NABL T-4179) and the original citation was restored. The fluoride figure was corrected twice: an unsourced 3.28 mg/L, then a verified 3.12–3.15 for *central* Hyderabad, and finally **5.09 mg/L from CGWB's Rangareddy district report** — the flat's own district. That last source also surfaced **nitrate above the permissible limit in 80% of samples, to 515 mg/L**, which nothing in nine prior passes had noticed. The monsoon *schedule* survived but its justification did not: October does **not** equal June (IMD gives ~97 vs ~106 mm, with August the wettest), and Telangana is not a northeast-monsoon subdivision. **Two lessons worth keeping.** Verifying a number is not the same as verifying it is the *relevant* number. And **a domain mismatch refutes a URL, never an organisation** — the IHS error came from treating "`ihslab.com` is an Ohio company" as proof that no Hyderabad lab of that name exists. Query the name against a register, not a guessed domain.

- **2026-09-27 — owner feedback + deep research pass (5 analysis agents with ~40 delegated researchers, 3 rounds of self-correction; every load-bearing claim re-verified by the author against the primary source before landing).** Owner supplied three site facts: **sockets carry neutral + earth**, **every room has a cable pulled to it that looks coaxial**, and **a Sonos Era 300 is already owned**. Added §3.1 (per-room in-ceiling speaker plan, zone map, amp and cable spec), §11 item 3a (the utility enclosure), and a §2 block on identifying the in-room cable. **Corrections that changed a decision:** a **Sonos Beam Ultra does exist** (2 Sep 2026, not in India yet), so the flat denial was wrong; **India is in neither of Sonos's published Alexa region lists**, so Echo-to-Sonos voice may not work here at all; **the KEF Ci160QR cut-out is Ø196 mm ROUND, not square** — an irreversible ceiling cut; **the WiiM Amp Pro does not receive AirPlay while the cheaper base Amp does**, inverting the step-up; **Bosch's Home Connect dishwasher costs ₹3,010 more, not ₹17,790**, which flips that recommendation, and Bosch's real selling price makes IFB ₹6,030 *cheaper* rather than a same-price peer; **Sonos Ray is ₹22,999, not ₹39,999**; **Aqara leak sensors are ₹3,080 each**, lifting §8 row 4 to the top of its band; **GST 2.0 (22 Sep 2025) put appliances at 18%** and leaves two live traps — finished stone slabs stayed 18% while only blocks fell to 5%, and residual job work rose 12%→18%; **the load budget was missing ~5–6.5 kW** of dishwasher, washer and dryer heaters. **Fabrications and dead ends caught:** the claim that Airveda's "Pulse Touch" and "Aurora" do not exist was **flatly wrong** — both are live and priced, and the recommended Pro Touch is a quote-only commercial unit, so the pick moved to the **Pulse at ₹23,999**; **Kaff BLHF 302X does not exist**, collapsing the VFM hybrid-hob path; **Polk RC65i is an in-wall speaker**, so "all Polk RCi are round in-ceiling" is false; **X-Sense has no India channel**, leaving the interlinked-alarm recommendation on a grey import; **IKEA BEKANT is discontinued in India**; **Atomberg publishes no "Renesa+"**; **IHS's phone number was wrong** (+91 40 23210136/23210139) and its ₹2,400 panel is "Groundwater Potability Tests", with a *different* ₹2,400 package also on the list — order by code. **Vendor audit:** **Homz Interio scores 2.45/10 and is rejected** on its own published terms (delay compensation void above a ₹30L budget, all payments non-refundable, it owns your drawings, Bangalore-only jurisdiction); **HomeLane and DesignCafe publish the same legal contact**, so they are not two quotes; **Livspace's own warranty page says one year, not ten**, and publishes a right to "modify or alter any Product design, specifications or materials"; and **none of the four studios previously shortlisted as single-entity design-build actually passes that test** — Urban Tusker was dropped outright (stock-photo portfolio, 10-month-old domain). **The biggest single finding is a "don't":** Telangana's **G.O.Ms.No.168 Appendix-VII undertaking prohibits enclosing balconies and binds successors**, there is **no compounding route for a high-rise**, and a sealed ~9 m³ utility **physically cannot absorb one load of laundry** (~13× more water than its air can hold) — so the utility gets partial glazing, not an enclosure. **Second-wave findings, after the last three agents reported.** The builder's own **Olympus specification sheet was recovered** and says only "Provision for Internet / Telephone / Intercom" — no cable type — though **Sumadhura's sibling projects say "DTH" and Olympus is the sheet that drops it.** **Indian TV faceplates are IEC Ø9.52 mm rated 0–860 MHz, not F-type** — which corrects the identification test *and* blocks MoCA at the faceplate, while signalling the coax was intended for cable/MATV rather than a DTH dish. **IS 732 puts TV and data both in Category 2**, so Cat6 may legally share the coax conduit and never a power conduit — the governing on-site rule. **A common dish reinstates DTH without any façade mount**, reversing the note's dismissal. **EAP653 is not an India product** (EAP650 is, at ₹14,999–16,999, 30–48% above the old estimate, moving Build A to ~₹56–63k), **TL-SG2210P is ₹7,980–8,499** where the note said price-unverified, and **every UniFi AP on the India store is sold out** with reseller warranty counted from the distributor's delivery. **ACT now sells 1 Gbps at ₹2,949–2,999**, but **its "public IPv4 by default" edge does not survive arithmetic** — 280,576 addresses against 1.5M+ subscribers, 39.4% IPv6-capable; its real edge is a bare Ethernet handoff that suits the owned Flint 2. **6 GHz delicensing is confirmed to G.S.R. 47(E) of 20 Jan 2026** with a purchase constraint nobody had noticed: rule 8(1) requires an integrated antenna. **CPWD cl. 3.5(i)/(vi) positively require a neutral at the first switch board**, so the odds are better than folklore — but the real question is **whether the socket shares the light's MCB**, because a borrowed neutral defeats safe isolation. And the **35 mm back-box premise was wrong** (Indian GI boxes are ~50 mm, and a relay is 16.8 mm, so wire volume is the constraint). On vendors: **the note's own 5–10% retention clause voids the Livspace warranty** (clause 6.2, "for any reason whatsoever"), a **Hyderabad consumer commission has held that calling 3D renders "only conceptual" is unfair trade practice** (C.C. 106/2025, 3 Sep 2026, ₹4.25L), **Arrivae's site has been parked since Feb 2026**, **Interior Company is Square Yards not Asian Paints**, and **Livspace cut ~1,000 staff and lost its India CEO in Feb 2026.**

**Third-wave mesh findings.** **The EU scheme that classifies insect screens (Reg. 2019/1188 Table 2) tops out at class 3 = ≥110 N/m²** against ~1,740 Pa of design pressure here — so no class marking is useful evidence at 126 m, and a quoted "class 4–6" means the vendor is classifying under Table 1 as a lateral-guide-rail blind, not as an insect screen. Verified at source. **Plisse is now ruled out on materials, not just wind: pleated fabric exists in only two product groups, so it cannot be had in stainless or in the heavy child-rated polyester** — and Isotra's own manual states the failure chain (dust → rail wear → **cord rupture** → scrap). **Grade correction: SS304 for the grille but SS316 for stainless mesh**, because §8.1's own "sheltered, non-rain-washed, deposit-trapping" carve-out describes a 0.2 mm wire in a recessed track exactly, and Neher puts SS304 in resistance class II ("lightly polluted areas only"). **Price correction: a mesh sash in an existing track is ₹170–350/sqft — the old ₹15–40k per slider was 2–4× high**, which makes counting the sill rails the highest-return five minutes in §11. **Brand reality: only four names actually sell insect screening into India** (Mr. Mosquito, Fenesta, Aparna Venster, Encraft); Netlon India is defunct, Phantom has no India presence, and **NCL Alltek was a paints business whose VEKA joint venture is why VEKA extrudes in Nanakramguda.** Cleaning method pinned from three manufacturers — and **Aparna Venster's own blog advice (ammonia, "rub vigorously") contradicts all three; don't follow it.**

**Final wave — two standards gaps closed and one correction reversed.** **EN 12210's wind ladder ends at class 5 = P1 2,000 Pa, and above it the designation is "Exxxx" carrying the actual tested pressure** (verified verbatim from a UKAS test report) — so the ~2,180–2,490 Pa demand at floor 42 **exceeds the top class**, and the BOQ line became **"class CE2200 minimum"** with the "span/175" wording dropped as belonging to no EN class. **The question that follows is the one to ask Aparna first: is their published 3,000 Pa the P1 or the P3?** If P3, the implied P1 is 2,000 Pa — exactly at demand, no margin. **EN 12608 Class A is ≥2.8/≥2.5 mm, and the trap is that IS 17953 allows −10% on visible wall thickness, so a nominal 2.8 mm profile may legally measure 2.52 mm = Class B** — hence the "as-measured, nominal not accepted" clause. **Correction reversed: Phifer *does* have a wholly-owned Indian subsidiary** (Chennai, since 2008), so the mesh spec is officially buyable — though its India site never names BetterVue/UltraVue/TuffScreen, and "Phifer" reseller listings span 17× in price. **And a pricing basis error worth remembering: Indian marketplace window rates are supply-only — installation adds ₹150–300/sqft**, which moved the utility line from ₹20–28k to ₹22–34k all-in.

**Door, window and entry close-out — and two corrections to items landed earlier in this same pass.** **Barrier height: floor 42 needs 1,200 mm, not the 1,000 mm snag threshold written earlier.** NBC Part 3 cl 12.11 gives a 1.0–1.2 m range, but Part 6 Sec 8 Annex H Table 35 bands it by floor and reads **1,200 mm above the 21st floor** for external balconies and window barriers alike (agent-read from SP 7:2016; not independently confirmed here). **And the 100 mm-sphere rule turns out to be in Indian code** — same annex, with the important addition that the check is made "making due allowance for deflection under load", i.e. with the infill pushed — so §8's ≤75 mm spec is deliberately more conservative than code and now has a domestic citation plus a design load (0.74 kN/m at 1.1 m). **Two reversals of my own additions:** the **night chain is retracted** (NBC Part 4 cl 6.1.1.1(a) prohibits chain-type bolts, and a fixed chain stops the leaf reaching the strike, defeating the motorised lock), and **the finger-guard push-back was overstated** — an Indian 241-injury series puts 74% of door-crush victims under five, so §8's "outgrown at six" was substantially right; what survives is hinge-side guards on two doors plus magnetic stoppers. **New items:** NBC Part 4 cl 6.1.1.1(b) requires **bathroom locks openable from outside without a special key** — the cheapest safety item in the fit-out; **concealed door closers need a ≥40 mm leaf against India's typical 32–35 mm**, making it a joinery-stage decision rather than a retrofit; **acoustic seal sets test at ~31 dB Rw, not the 40–50 dB vendors quote**, and genuine mechanical drop seals have no Indian retail channel; **no code requires a balcony tap or floor drain**, so run a surface CPVC line (~₹2–6k) and never retrofit a floor drain; and the **VRF bedroom spec became a configuration** — small ducted unit with its fan box outside the bedroom envelope, no cassette over the bed, because published low-fan figures are measured 1.5 m directly below the unit, where a sleeper's head is. **On the curtain track, the substance held but the attribution did not:** no systems house publishes a "never drill uPVC" prohibition, so it is trade rationale — while the fixing side got quantified (through-bolt to RCC; an AAC plug carries 30–92 kg and a nylon hammer-fix 13–16 kg against a 30–65 kg track).

**Method note for this pass, and it is the durable lesson.** Across six research threads the agents caught **eight** load-bearing errors in their own and each other's work — a wind class borrowed from the wrong table, an acoustic figure that was a requirement rather than a result, a bite-through aperture cited as a screen aperture, a closer rating off by 25 kg, a paper's temperature figures misread, a retailer listing missed because the SKU carried a space, a G.hn throughput taken from vendor marketing, and a Phifer conclusion drawn from the wrong page. **Every one was plausible, specific and wrong, and every one was caught by opening the cited document rather than reasoning about it.** I caught three more the same way: the EU insect-screen class is open-ended at the top rather than a hard ceiling, the Telangana GO's enforcement clause is narrower than the paraphrase handed to me while its balcony prohibition is broader, and the 100 mm-sphere rule turned out to *be* in Indian code after it had been written off as borrowed. **A subagent's correction is therefore worth as much as its finding — and the vault's "verify before it lands" rule earned its keep about a dozen times in one session.** Where a claim could not be reached at source it is marked as such in place, including the NBC barrier heights.

**Method note:** archive.org (`advancedsearch.php` plus an item's `_djvu.txt`) is the reliable route to Indian Standards, and the **Wayback CDX API** retrieved statutes that `indiacode.nic.in` blocks outright. Plain curl with a browser user-agent reached `bis.gov.in`, `uidai.gov.in`, `pngrb.gov.in`, `sgpc.net`, `bglgas.com`, `aiims.edu`, `mausam.imd.gov.in` and all four hospital sites — so **a subagent's "blocked, unverified" is itself a claim worth re-testing**, and most of what one pass wrote off as dead was live. Genuinely unreachable: `indiacode.nic.in`, `cgwb.gov.in`, `hyderabadwater.gov.in`, `rera.telangana.gov.in`, `imdpune.gov.in`.

**Method + limits:** every model code, India-availability and spec claim is checked against manufacturer/spec pages where reachable. **Amazon.in / Croma / Reliance / Flipkart bot-block automated fetches**, so every INR figure is indicative — re-price locally before buying, and never paste these figures to a vendor. Subagent output is treated as a claim, not a fact: anything not confirmed against a primary source is marked unverified in place.

- **2026-10-01 — owner feedback rewrite.** The owner gave four inputs. (1) Execution is decided: Homz Interio at ₹18 lakh including tax, and the flat needs no plumbing, electrical, painting or waterproofing work; bathrooms came finished except geysers and glass partitions, and the VRF is installed. (2) The note was too detailed: keep the main body simple, push analysis to appendices, keep summary tables with scores, prices and buy links. (3) Is a 25 L geyser really needed, given heat-up time? (4) Add linked images to every interiors recommendation, apt pictures for each room, and room-by-room visualisations. **Method:** 12 domain teams (Homz contract, joinery, bathrooms, kitchen appliances, smart home and network, audio, air and water, safety, furnishing, public rooms, private rooms, admin), each an analyst paired with a critic, for up to 2 critique rounds. An editor then merged the briefs into one main body, resolved cross-team conflicts and checked every anchor. **What changed:** the old Route 2/3, III Studio and VAL Atelier analysis became a 2-line history note; the Homz risks became a 12-ask rider, a materials annexure ([A12.0](#a120-spec-sheet-to-hand-homz)) and inspection-gated payments; new circuits, re-plumbing, re-tiling and repainting were dropped; the 25 L question got a worked answer (master only; A11.1); room plans (SVG) and linked thumbnails were added. **Conflicts the editor resolved:** Drawing chairs (EKENÄSET over the 81 cm Biscoff, which pinched the shoe-cabinet walk to 618 mm); Echo Dots cut from four to two, since no microphone goes where Kabir sleeps or plays (A7.4 rule over the §1 draft); geyser setpoint 55 °C with 50 °C in the master for Kabir (A11.2), not 50 °C everywhere; art spots 4, per the room plans, not ~5; Kabir's chair is a fixed four-leg chair until 7, not ÖRFJÄLL with a BOLMEN step; curtains ~₹1.3–2L made-to-measure, not ₹1–2.5L; shutters 18–19 mm in both §12 and the A7 annexure. **Still unverified:** each team's list sits in its appendix; the largest money items are the Homz BOQ scope, curtain cost, grille area, ceiling height and lift size.

- **2026-10-02 — owner feedback: budget 2×, smart switches back, alternatives, no 25 L, no dishwasher (8 research streams; load-bearing prices re-checked by the author on the seller's page or catalogue).** The owner gave seven inputs: double the budget and prefer quality on ties, use Hyderabad makers rather than defaulting to IKEA; reinstate smart switches; give alternatives for the Drawing room, Kabir's room and BR-01; no 25 L geyser and no dishwasher; an adjustable desk and a new palette for Kabir; exhaustive sofa-cum-bed research with a scored top 5; and a render of the Drawing room from the actual picks. **Corrections that changed the plan:** (1) **Magari's Shopify feed prices are ex-GST**; its pages show ×1.18, found independently by two agents and confirmed on the saved Denver page (₹1,18,000 feed, ₹1,39,240 page). Every earlier Magari figure was ~15% low. (2) **Alankaram's ash Fusi lists "Veneer"** in its spec line; the teak version says solid teak, so the Dining moved to teak. (3) **The 64 cm Drawing chair limit was self-imposed**: the gap between the chairs had no target. With ≥300 mm between them and both walks at 900 mm, chairs up to 75 cm fit. (4) **No electric kids' desk in India goes below ~59 cm**, so "adjustable" means a crank desk at 6. (5) **No Wi-Fi geyser comes in 3 kW**, and every manual read forbids external switching, so geysers stay off the smart-switch layer. (6) IKEA still wins the sofa-beds after ~30 brands: no Hyderabad maker publishes a mattress spec or warrants over a year. **Method:** the render is a composite of the actual product photos placed in a perspective drawn from the room dimensions (ImageMagick), not a generated image. **Not verified this pass:** Jaipur Rugs and Häfele India (blocked), Innovation Living India prices, Homz prices for the new joinery, Sonoff India warranty term.

- **2026-10-02 — critical review and rule corrections.** A review found the research ran with no search engine, and that several agent-made rules had been applied as owner rules. Fixed: the electrical rule (additions allowed, no rewiring), smart bulbs on a dimmer (Dining pendant), the microphone rule (an agent default, now labelled), Kabir's bed (a just-in-case bed, per the owner), the orientation (north, east and south glass; the 2026-09-20 "no west glazing" line is softened to "no large west glazing"), and the dryer (restored to Should, per the owner's 9 Sep ask). Detail and the re-test list: [[u-setup-instruction]].

## A27. Assumptions and derivations

Where the main body's numbers come from. If you disagree with a recommendation, the disagreement is usually with one of these.

### A27.1 The interiors budget — ₹18 lakh with Homz, plus what sits outside it

**The old argument is retired.** It said a ₹25–40L band was "standard-to-mid" and forced a choice between more money and less scope. The flat needs no paint, MEP or waterproofing work, so that band was too wide. The owner has a Homz quote of ₹18L including GST.

**The Homz figure.** ₹18L incl. GST = ₹15.25L ex-GST. That's **₹880/sqft of carpet incl. GST** (₹746 ex-GST). A bottom-up estimate at laminate-tier rates gives ~₹14.0–24.3L incl. GST for the expected list, including the ceiling finish ([A7](#is-18-lakh-sane-for-this-scope)). So ₹18L is credible for modular joinery plus a finished false ceiling. It's thin if it also covers beds, curtains and wallpaper.

### A27.3 Air-quality sizing — why the winter load, not the annual mean

IQAir's published annual series for Hyderabad runs **39–51 µg/m³ (2017–19)**. Its *World Air Quality Report 2023* gives **39.9 for 2023 and 42.4 for 2022**. Five sourced years span 39–51, with a mean of about 43. So "~40" is the honest annual figure.

**The annual mean is the wrong number to size off, because the swing within a year is 3.2×.** The same report's monthly series:

| Low months | Peak months |
|---|---|
| Jun 18.2 · Aug 19.0 | **Nov 58.9 · Jan 56.8 · Feb 54.1 · Oct 51.5** |

**So size purifiers and the air-change target off a winter design load of 55–60 µg/m³.** Quote ~40 only as the annual figure.

**Updated for the Homz schedule.** Move-in is now about April 2027, not Diwali 2026. The Nov–Feb peak falls on the gypsum and installation work instead. Don't run purifiers on site then: gypsum sanding dust clogs a HEPA set within days (₹5–10k per set), and an open site is a theft risk. Dust containment and a post-works clean are Homz's job. Install the purifiers at the cure phase. Change the Master and BR-02 filters every six months. Put an Airveda monitor in early and build your own series, since the published figures are a city average. *(Figures carried from the earlier verification; not re-fetched this pass.)*

### A27.4 The sealed utility — the 13× water deficit

Take the room at about 4'11" × 6'6" × 10', or **9 m³**. A 7 kg load leaving a 1,400 rpm spin carries about **3.5 L** of water. Saturated air at 30 °C holds 30.3 g/m³. So the room's whole air volume holds only **0.27 L at 100% RH**.

That's a **~13× deficit**, and it survives a 2× error in the load figure. **The excess water condenses onto the walls, ceiling, joinery and any network rack.**

**Ventilation can't rescue it in the monsoon.** Assume the room is held at 90% RH (27.3 g/m³). Removing 3.5 L over 8 hours then needs about **41 m³/h** of outdoor air at 30 °C and 55% RH (16.7 g/m³). At 30 °C and 80% RH (24.2 g/m³) it needs about **144 m³/h**. Ventilation only moves water down a humidity gradient, and there isn't one in August. **So a ceiling drying rack and an enclosed utility don't work together.** You need a dryer that condenses to a drain, or a dehumidifier. Separately, a notarised undertaking prohibits enclosing the utility.

### A27.5 Occupancy timing — when the Prakash and move-in can honestly happen

**Target mid-April 2027. Keep your current housing flexible to end-August 2027,** the end of the slip row below. A slip is Homz's base rate in its reviews, so plan housing for it.

**Three constraints set the date:**

1. **Homz's build time.** The Hyderabad FAQ says "within 45–90 working days after design approval". A 3.5 BHK with custom closets sits at the 90 end.
2. **About 2 weeks of ventilated cure (4 if the flat still smells, which pushes the target to late April)** after installation, before anyone sleeps in the flat and before the Saroop enters. It covers formaldehyde from new board, adhesive and laminate (§12).
3. **BR-03 must be complete and Prakash-ready.** The Saroop's lead time is unknown, because SGPC publishes no procedure. A Prakash also needs the whole flat calm: no labour, no dust, no shoes in the room.

**Festivals inside the window.** Diwali and Chhath (6–16 Nov) straddle the 15 Nov sign-off, so book the freeze meeting for early November or expect a week's slip. Christmas to New Year and **Sankranti (about 14–16 Jan 2027, exact dates unverified)** fall inside installation. Sankranti is Telangana's main harvest festival; site labour can leave for up to a week.

**Three scenarios**, all assuming design sign-off on 15 Nov 2026:

| Scenario | Works complete | Cure ends | Earliest Prakash and move-in | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Best case | Late Jan 2027 (45–60 working days, plus a Sankranti week) | Mid-Feb 2027 | **Mid-Feb 2027** | Homz meets the low end of its own claim |
| **Target** | **Mid-Mar 2027** (90 working days at 6 a week ≈ 15 weeks, plus about 2 festival weeks) | **End-Mar 2027** | **Mid-April 2027** (around Vaisakhi; exact date **unverified**) | Homz meets its 90-working-day ceiling |
| **Slip (plan housing for this)** | May–Jul 2027 | Jun–Aug 2027 | Jun–Aug 2027 | Trustpilot shows 45-day promises turning into 4–8 months. A Gachibowli job ran over 8 months |

**Correction to the old note.** It said "the earliest realistic Prakash is around March 2027". That assumed on-site carpentry finishing in February. With a factory build signed off in mid-November, mid-April 2027 is the honest target. June to August is the realistic fallback. Every 2026 festival window (8 Nov, 24 Nov, 27 Nov–11 Dec, 29 Dec–12 Jan) is out of reach. The 15 Jan 2027 Parkash Purab falls before even the best-case cure ends.

**The season rule, re-derived.** Wood settles to the air around it. At ~27 °C its moisture content is **9.1% at 50% RH, 12.9% at 70% and 15.7% at 80%** (FPL Wood Handbook, Table 4-2). IMD's Hyderabad normals put morning RH at **79–82% through Jul–Sep** and **50–55% in Mar–May**. The old rule banned carpentry in June–September, because joinery fitted wet dries out under the VRF and its joints open. Factory panels change that:

- Panels are pressed, laminated on both faces and edge-banded in the factory. Only cut edges, drilled holes and site-applied finishes take up moisture quickly.
- The Nov 2026–Mar 2027 install window is Hyderabad's dry season anyway.
- **So the rule becomes conditions, not a calendar:** keep panels wrapped until fitted, and do any site-applied veneer, polish or PU only between November and May. The VRF stays off with grilles sealed during dusty work. Dry mode is fine once the dust work ends and the filters are cleaned.

The rule bites again only in the slip scenario. A summer 2027 overrun pushes installation into the monsoon. That's another reason rider ask 5 (the delay penalty) matters.

**Action.** Get a written handover date from Homz in the rider. Then ask the Granthi for dates after that date plus three weeks. Book an Akhand Path first if you want one: it needs 48 continuous hours and a relay of Pathis. Or choose a Sahaj Path.

### A27.6 Floor-42 wind — where ~54 m/s comes from

IS 875-3:2015 gives Hyderabad a basic wind speed of **44 m/s** (3-second gust). At about 126 m the height and terrain factor k₂ is **~1.22** for a built-up district (Table 2, Terrain Category 3). 1.28 is the open-terrain Category-1 figure at that height. Don't use it here.

Design gust ≈ 44 × 1.22 ≈ **54 m/s (~193 km/h)**. Design pressure pz = 0.6 Vz² ≈ **1,729 Pa** before the pressure coefficients. A structural engineer sets the local coefficient. Don't read it off this page.

Two consequences for the body: **nothing loose on the balcony, and a hidden tether on anything over 3 kg.** And the window wind-load demand is high enough that any new glazing should ask for a tested pressure, not a class name.

### A27.7 Areas and quoting bases

**2,880 sqft SBUA against 2,045 sqft carpet** (~71% efficiency). The two shift a per-sqft figure by 41%. The full basis table is in [A0](#a0-standing-caveats-gst-and-quoting-bases--detail).

| Basis | Used for |
|---|---|
| Carpet, 2,045 sqft | The Homz ₹880/sqft check, false ceiling and its finish, Wi-Fi AP count, robot-vac sizing, the insurance rebuild rate |
| SBUA, 2,880 sqft | Maintenance charges and the snag-walk fee only. It includes common areas |
| Shutter face or running foot | All joinery |
| Opening area | Grilles, mesh, glass partitions |

Wall-area paint quotes are no longer needed. The ceiling finish is priced inside the Homz quote.

### A27.8 What is assumed rather than verified

- **Window compass faces come from the floor plan, not a site check.** Balcony faces are owner-confirmed (main balcony and utility east, Master balcony south, 2026-09-20).
- **The exterior opening count is contradictory on paper.** [[my-home]] lists three openings. The drawings variously imply four. The grille and mesh counts are provisional until you count on site.
- **The bedroom count** (3 BHK, 3.5 BHK or four as drawn) is unresolved. The joinery quantities assume four rooms: wardrobes in the Master (two), BR-01 and BR-02, and the rumala almirah in BR-03.
- **The VRF indoor-unit type** (ducted, cassette or hi-wall) isn't recorded. It sets the false-ceiling area (assumed 800–1,100 sqft) and the hatch count.
- **Ceiling height** is assumed at ~2.9–3.0 m, not measured.
- **Homz facts checked only through Homz's own site or a single third party:** factory location, GSTIN status, Google rating, the Warranty Letter's contents. All four are **unverified**.
- **The bottom-up rates in A7 and A27.1 are derived estimates**, not quotes. Ask Homz for its line-by-line split.
- **Legal readings in A7** (Consumer Protection Act §34 and §69, Contract Act §28, the labour-code thresholds) come from the statutes as recalled and from PRS summaries. The official texts weren't reachable from this session.
- **Every INR figure is indicative.** Major Indian retailers block automated fetches.

| **K1. Reading and storage bench** (adopted) | A Homz bench on the south wall replaces TROFAST: drawers at his height, a washable cushion, front-facing book ledges above, fixed to masonry | Homz bench 180×45×42 cm, BWP ply in Harbour laminate (~₹25–40k, unverified). Boingg TidyTales front-facing shelf, 107×91×43 cm, ₹12,255, if Homz won't build ledges | ~₹25–52k | In the main body | ~3× TROFAST. Fixed joinery. **Never under the window**: on floor 42 a bench there is a step to the glass |
| K2. Mid-sleeper with a den under it | IKEA KURA reversible bed (209×99×116 cm, 83 cm free underneath) in PS 2026's footprint, floor cushions and a reading light in the den. Flips to a low bed | KURA ₹22,990 + a 90×200 mattress (~₹10–15k, unverified). Premium: Boingg Little Library Loft, HDF, ₹67,627.50; Fun House Loft with a pull-out desk, ₹78,007.50 | ~₹35k–80k | Park until ~age 8, when he actually moves in | IKEA says lofts are "not suitable for children under 6". It removes the sofa and the adult spare bed. The VRF supply may blow at sleeping height. It is a climbing frame |
| K3. Explorer theme | The theme lives only in removable things on one display wall: a world-map decal, a globe, framed star charts, navy constellation bedding. Joinery stays plain | Décor ~₹8–20k (unverified) | ~₹8–20k | A good add-on over Harbour, not a layout | A full theme dates by 9–10. Built-in theme pieces (Boingg's rocket shelf, ₹14,007.50) are the hardest to outgrow |

**Rejected:** a Montessori floor bed and low shelving (built for toddlers; PS 2026 is already 41 cm high), a climbing wall (the walls back onto toilets, the wall type is unknown, and it invites climbing toward the window), Moll Champion desks (not shipped to India), the Stokke Tripp Trapp as a desk chair (built for 72–76 cm tables, too high at his desk height).

**Desk research, in brief.** Electric kids' desks sold in India start at 59–70 cm (ErgoYou E15 frame 59 cm, JIN Titan 62 cm, V ONE and Lukzer 70 cm); Moll's e-Champion starts at 63 cm and is sized for children 127 cm and taller. Flexispot India didn't respond. BERGLÄRKA (52–75 cm crank, tilting top, EN 17191) is the only verified low-start adjustable desk with a published child-safety test. StarAndDaisy's tilt set (₹10,999) starts at 61 cm; IKEA RELATERA (₹11,990) is 65–108 cm, a teen desk.

Images: [book by a window](https://images.unsplash.com/photo-1720706405494-e552f264dd8d?w=600) (K1) · [ladder and bookshelves](https://images.unsplash.com/photo-1662379940109-1026a0dcfe95?w=600) (K2) · [desk globe](https://images.unsplash.com/photo-1521295121783-8a321d551ad2?w=600) (K3).

## Open inputs

Facts still to collect on site. Each one changes a line in the main body.

| Input | Changes | How to get it |
|---|---|---|
| Homz's itemised BOQ and payment stages | Every "confirm" line; the outside-Homz budget (beds, curtains, glass, cleaning); whether the gates need renegotiating | Ask Homz in writing, then map its stages onto the 5 gates |
| Builder platform build, stone thickness, PNG point position | Built-in vs tabletop hob; sink-side drawer bay; corner pull-outs | Tape and photos under the stone; stone vendor; the PNG operator |
| Existing 16 A points and gas outlet | Where each appliance goes, and which points Homz must add | Photograph each point and trace it to the DB labels |
| VRF unit type per room, bulkheads, drain pumps | False-ceiling area (~800–1,100 sqft assumed), hatches, AP and spot positions | Site walk and the VRF installer's drawings |
| Ceiling height per room (~2.7–3.0 m assumed) | Loft height, curtain drop and cost, pendant choice | Laser-measure each room |
| Service lift car and door (~1,100 × 1,300 × 2,300 mm assumed) | Fridge crate, stone length, 206 cm IKEA boxes, the Palki | Measure, or ask the facility manager |
| Shower-zone depth and geyser point position per bath | Panel vs door; vertical vs horizontal geyser and an access panel | Tape measure; builder's electrical drawing |
| Water hardness, TDS, fluoride per line | Softener tier; RO need; which line feeds the kitchen | IHS GPT + BPT tests |
| Sprinkler heads and refuge floors | Ceiling layout and drop-extension cost; the fire card | Count on site; facility manager's fire file |
| Window faces and widths; is there a small window in the Master's west wall? | Curtain spec and cost; art and sun placement; the bed wall | Compass and tape on site |
| Supply phase, sanctioned kW, DG cap, PNG meter type | Geyser schedule, SPD pole count, top-up method | Facility manager; count the incoming MCB poles |
| ISPs with fibre in Tower B; intercom video | ISP pick; network shelf location; whether the doorbell is needed | Facility manager |

## Notes

- **Naming.** B4206 = Tower B, floor 42, flat series 6. Brochure sheets titled "FLAT No. 6" apply; Tower A Flat 6 is the mirror unit, so its photos show inverted light.
- **Two areas, not interchangeable.** 2,880 sqft SBUA vs 2,045 sqft carpet. Homz's ₹880/sqft is on carpet. Joinery is quoted on shutter area, grilles and mesh per sqft of opening. Put the basis on every quote ([A27.7](#a277-areas-and-quoting-bases)).
- **Orientation.** The west edge is the entry and lift lobby, with no large glazing; the plan may show one small window in the Master's west wall. Read with north at the top, the Drawing and BR-02 face north, the Dining, sit-out, utility and BR-03 east, the Master, BR-01 and kitchen south. Confirm each face on site.
- **What the builder delivered.** VRF, wiring, plumbing, paint and finished bathrooms (no geysers or glass). Rewiring, new pipes, re-tiling and repainting are out of scope; a point added through the false ceiling or joinery is not ([A25](#a25-decisions-locked-constraints-and-what-the-builder-fitted)).
- **Prices rot.** INR figures are October 2026 street prices; Amazon.in moves weekly. Re-check on order day, and never paste these numbers to a vendor.
- **Commit the room plans.** `assets/personal/olympus/` holds the 12 SVGs this note embeds. Commit it with the note, or every plan breaks.
- **Money and documents stay out of the vault.** Quotes, payment schedules and the Homz contract live in `~/private/`, referenced from here.
